# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.0.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

## [Unreleased]

### Fixed
- **mux: `new-workspace` auto-disambiguates its first session's name; `--session-name` decouples it from the workspace name** (card 01a18e57, filed by par-term; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/{command,dispatch/sessions.rs,tree}`, `docs/MUX.md`): the workspace's first session was hard-coupled to the workspace's name, so on any daemon holding a session `main` a bare `new-workspace` could never succeed, and `-n X` failed while any session `X` existed. The workspace still keeps the requested name, but the first session's default name now auto-disambiguates against the global session roster tmux-style (`main`, `main-2`, `main-3`, …, resolved before the workspace row exists — a refused create still leaves no half-created workspace); the new `new-workspace -n X --session-name <name>` names the first session explicitly and keeps the `duplicate session` refusal on a clash. Pinned by `new_workspace_auto_disambiguates_its_first_session_name`, `new_workspace_session_name_decouples_and_explicit_clash_refuses`, and the updated `unknown_targets_error_exactly_and_change_nothing` pin.

## [0.73.0] - 2026-10-30

### Added
- **mux: a daemon restart resumes agents with a continuation prompt** (`f3ea6a7f`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/{config,agent_resume}.rs`, `persist/{restore,format}.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): a resumed `claude --resume <id>` restores context but not execution — the model idles at its prompt until a human types. The new `[daemon] agent-resume-prompt` (default ON, assess-first wording) is passed as the trailing positional on the resume argv, so the restored session starts working immediately and the roster follows. A persisted park message (`report-state -m`) composes in front (`You were parked as: <message>. <prompt>`); the injection is allowlisted to agents whose resume CLI verifiably takes a trailing positional (claude today — the resume table's no-guesses rule), empty disables, and the start-command restore path is untouched. Pinned by `a_restart_passes_the_continuation_prompt_to_a_resumed_claude`, `an_empty_resume_prompt_config_injects_nothing`, `a_parked_message_composes_into_the_continuation_prompt`, and `agents_without_a_verified_resume_prompt_shape_are_not_injected`.
- **streaming: the roster frame carries the v0.72 activity/presence stats** (`efd30c29`; `proto/terminal.proto`, `src/streaming/{protocol,roster,roster_watcher}.rs`, `docs/STREAMING.md`, filed by pardeck): `AgentEntry` gains additive fields — `last_activity_at`/`last_report_at`/`last_input_at`/`last_viewed_at` (unix ms), `viewer_count`, `cpu_ms`, and `stalled` — parsed from the `list-agents` row's same-named tokens, so a streamer client sees everything the mux CLI roster shows. State-change deltas enrich from the watcher's cache, now including the stats: a push carries none of these tokens, so the fetched values hold until the next full snapshot refetches them, matching the pane_label/project contract. proto3 omits zero defaults, so pre-0.72 wire bytes are unchanged; the Python dict shape gains the seven keys additively (`roster_dicts_match_pinned_shape` re-pinned). Pinned by `presence_tokens_fill_the_activity_and_presence_fields`, the stats-carrying warm-cache asserts in `state_deltas_enrich_from_the_cache_and_first_seen_panes_send_bare`, and a populated golden fixture entry carrying every stat.

### Fixed
- **app: the Par-mux launcher's microphone status probe works under hardened runtime** (`0c051f4e`; `Makefile`, `crates/par-mux/app/Par-mux.entitlements`, `docs/MUX.md`): tccd refuses kTCCServiceMicrophone outright for a hardened-runtime binary without `com.apple.security.device.audio-input`, so the launcher's status-only mic probe could never leave `notDetermined`. The entitlements plist now ships in the repo and rides both Developer-ID codesign calls; documented alongside the stale-grant re-grant path.

## [0.72.0] - 2026-10-29

### Added
- **mux: per-pane human-presence and process stats in `pane-info` and the roster** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/{pane.rs,tree/layout_ops.rs,hooks/seen.rs,process_cpu.rs,dispatch/{panes,client}.rs}`, `docs/MUX.md`, PRD D35/D44): `pane-info` gains additive whitespace-free `key=value` tail tokens — `viewer_count=<n>` (the attached clients whose focused window shows the pane zoomed-or-active, the seen rule's slot), `last_viewed_at=<epoch-ms>` (read as now while a viewer holds the pane; frozen at the moment the last viewer left otherwise), `last_input_at=<epoch-ms>` (the last CLIENT-sent keystroke/paste through `send-keys`/`paste-buffer` — attach input chunks and the streamer bridge spell the same verb — stamped separately from the agent-hook `last_report_at`, so a supervisor can tell a human is driving), and `cpu_ms=<n>` (cumulative CPU-milliseconds of the pane's child plus its live descendants, walked by OS parent links; absent for a dead pane) — and `list-agents` rows gain `last_input_at=`. Pinned by `send_keys_stamps_last_input_and_pane_info_and_roster_report_it`, `pane_info_reports_viewer_count_and_last_viewed_at`, and the process-CPU tests `own_process_reports_cumulative_cpu`, `a_burning_child_accumulates_cpu`, and `an_absent_pid_reports_none`.
- **mux: a supervised pane keeps its start command across a restart** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/{config,pane,command,dispatch,tree,persist/}`, `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_restart.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): `new-session --restore start-command` (the flag refuses any other value, and refuses to fire without a start command) records the pane's restore choice, so restore re-runs the ORIGINAL start command — every flag — instead of an agent resume invocation, handing the hook-reported session id to that command as `PAR_MUX_RESUME_SESSION_ID` so a wrapper passes `--resume` itself; the hook-reported identity still persists with the pane for the roster. `set-workspace-restore-policy -t +N [start-command|agent-resume|none]` (bare form = read-only query, replies the effective value) does the same at workspace scale, and `none` disables restore of the workspace's panes entirely — its sessions never rebuild and the workspace comes back empty — which is also the no-restore workspace opt-out; `none` outranks the per-pane flag. Both choices ride the v3 state file as omitted-when-default fields, so existing files load unchanged and older daemons ignore the fields. Pinned by `a_flagged_pane_restores_via_its_start_command_with_the_resume_session_id`, `the_workspace_start_command_policy_restores_agent_panes_via_their_start_commands`, `the_workspace_none_policy_drops_its_panes_and_keeps_the_workspace`, `restore_policy_and_the_pane_flag_round_trip_through_the_state_file`, `parses_set_workspace_restore_policy_values_and_query`, and the live-daemon `a_restart_reruns_a_flagged_panes_start_command_with_the_resume_session_id` and `a_workspace_none_policy_drops_its_panes_at_the_next_restore`.
- **mux: `report-state`, a control verb for an agent's own parked-work state** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/{command,dispatch,hooks,scrape}`, `docs/MUX.md`, `crates/par-mux/skills/par-mux/SKILL.md`): `par-mux --cmd 'report-state -t %N [-a agent] [-s state] [-m message]'` writes the same claim a hook `pane.report_agent` writes — same state set minus `unknown`, same whitespace-collapsed message, same per-source staleness and activity stamp, same `%agent-state-changed` broadcast — with provenance `self` in the roster's source column, so an agent that ends its turn while harness-tracked background work still runs parks the pane (`-s working -m 'awaiting 2 background agents'`) instead of reading idle, and clears with the bare form (`-s` omitted reports `idle`, derived to `done` for an unseen pane like a hook's). The scrape tier now skips a `self`-authoritative state exactly as it skips a hook's, and a later hook report re-takes the state column. Pinned by the `report_state_*` dispatch and parse tests and the live-daemon `report_state_rides_the_hook_pipeline_over_a_live_daemon`.
- **mux: per-agent activity signal and a replayable pane-event ring for supervisors** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/{events.rs,dispatch/client.rs,hooks,pane.rs}`, `crates/par-term-emu-core/src/pty_session/`, filed by par-director): the roster's `list-agents` rows gain additive `key=value` tokens — `last_activity_at=<epoch-ms>` (the later of the pane's last processed PTY output read, stamped by the reader thread next to the generation bump, and its last accepted hook report), `last_report_at=<epoch-ms>` (hook reports only; scrape classification never stamps it), and `stalled=1` derived at query time when a `working` agent shows no activity for five minutes — so a hung tool call is distinguishable from active work within the card's five-minute bound. Every lifecycle notification the daemon broadcasts (except `%layout-change` churn) now also lands in a bounded in-memory ring (1024 events, monotonically increasing `seq`, survives client disconnects, never persisted) replayed through the new `events [--since <seq>]` control command — first body line `head <seq>`, then `<seq> <epoch-ms> <wire-line>` per retained event — so a reconnecting consumer reads the pane-exited events its disconnected interval dropped instead of polling blind. Pinned by `the_roster_carries_activity_timestamps_and_derives_stalled`, `the_event_ring_replays_lifecycle_lines_and_since_resumes`, `events_since_past_the_head_is_an_empty_ok`, `parses_events_with_and_without_since`, the event-ring unit tests, and the live-daemon `pane_exits_replay_through_the_event_ring_after_a_reconnect`.
- **Python: `TerminalClipboard`, a feature-scoped facade over a `Terminal`'s clipboard history** (`src/python_bindings/terminal/clipboard_api.rs`, ARC-002): `TerminalClipboard(term)` exposes `add`/`history`/`latest`/`clear`/`set`/`get`/`search` and delegates to the wrapped terminal, so both spellings act on the same state. It is a separate class, so the frozen flat `Terminal` surface does not grow; the flat `*_clipboard_history` methods stay for compatibility. Pinned by `tests/test_terminal_clipboard_facade.py`.

### Changed
- **BREAKING (par-term-streamer): legacy htpasswd hashes are refused at startup by default** (SEC-003; `src/bin/streaming_server/{cli.rs,bootstrap.rs}`, `docs/SECURITY.md`, `docs/STREAMING.md`): an HTTP Basic Auth credential that is a `{SHA}` (unsalted SHA-1), `$1$`, or `$apr1$` (MD5-crypt) hash now makes the streamer exit with an error naming the user and the format (never the hash) — the default flip promised when `--require-bcrypt` shipped. Regenerate with `htpasswd -nB <user>`, or pass the new `--allow-legacy-hash` (`PAR_TERM_ALLOW_LEGACY_HASH`) to keep accepting it with the release-cut warning. Cleartext passwords and bcrypt hashes are unaffected; `--require-bcrypt` still rejects cleartext. The library's `verify_htpasswd_hash` is unchanged. Pinned by `legacy_hashes_are_refused_by_default`, `allow_legacy_hash_keeps_legacy_hashes_accepted`, and `allow_legacy_hash_requires_http_user_and_excludes_require_bcrypt`.
- **PtySession full-buffer exports no longer walk the live grid under the terminal lock** (`crates/par-term-emu-core/src/terminal/export_snapshot.rs`, `pty_session/query.rs`, ARC-004): `PtySession::{export_text, export_styled, scrollback}` and the Python `PtyTerminal` methods of the same names hold the read lock only to clone a new `TextExportSnapshot` (primary grid, plus the alternate grid when active), then build the text after the lock drops, so a large export no longer stalls the PTY reader's write lock for its full duration. Output is byte-identical: `Terminal`'s exports run the same grid functions. Pinned by `snapshot_export_matches_live_export_on_the_primary_screen`, `snapshot_export_matches_live_export_on_the_alt_screen` and `session_exports_match_terminal_exports`.
- **streaming: the agent-roster Python dicts are derived instead of hand-written** (`derive/src/lib.rs`, `src/streaming/{protocol,py_convert}.rs`, ARC-008): `PyDictConvert` gains struct-typed `#[pydict(nested)]` fields, `#[pydict(nested(keep_empty))]` (an empty `Vec` stays `[]`), and a struct-field `#[pydict(skip)]`, which retire the hand-written `AgentRoster`/`AgentStateChanged` dict converters. The dict shape is unchanged (`AgentEntry` still exposes `pane_id`/`agent`/`state`/`source`/`reason` only), pinned by `roster_dicts_match_pinned_shape`, which passes against both the old and new code. `par-term-emu-derive` is bumped to 0.49.0 for these attributes; at the release cut, publish derive before the members and root.

### Fixed
- **mux attach: a re-land (tab/workspace/roster-click/landing switch) now deletes the previous view's kitty graphics from the host** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/{navigate.rs,kitty.rs}`): `reseed_window_inner` reset the cursor and chrome baselines on a view change but never the kitty state, so the previous view's placements stayed on the host — visibly, whenever the new window's `%layout-change` lags the reseed (the old layout still stands and its placements diff as current, so the placement diff emits nothing), and on principle against the documented seed/`resize_to` contract that a full view change deletes everything the client put on the host before the next frame redraws. The re-land now drops every mirror's graphics (`PaneRenderer::clear_all_graphics`, the per-pane drop `feed_replay` serves) and invalidates `HostGraphics` through the frame's raw outbox; the next frame re-uploads and re-places only what the new view wants. Pinned by `reseed_window_deletes_the_previous_views_kitty_graphics` and, red against the old seam, `reseed_window_deletes_the_kitty_graphics_even_when_the_layout_lags`.
- **mux: a nested `claude -p` no longer hijacks — and then releases — the parent pane's agent claim** (`crates/par-mux/integrations/hooks/par-mux-claude-session-hook.{sh,ps1}`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/hooks/{report,release}.rs`, observed live on macdaddy, hook v3): a claude spawned by the pane's claude (a sub-agent's Bash tool) inherits the pane env, so its SessionStart re-keyed the pane's claim to the child's session and its SessionEnd released the claim while the parent still ran — a daemon restart would resume the child in the parent's pane, and after the child exited the parent stayed off the roster with no resume identity. The hook (v4, `PAR_MUX_INTEGRATION_VERSION`) walks its process ancestry and stays silent when the claude it serves is nested (two or more claude processes above it); every report now carries the session id, so the daemon drops a fresh-start (`startup`/`new`) session report that would replace a live same-agent identity, refuses a release whose `agent_session_id` proof mismatches the claim, and re-establishes identity from the parent's next state report without a SessionStart (restore rebuilds `claude --resume <id>` via the per-agent table). Pinned by `a_fresh_session_report_that_would_replace_a_live_identity_is_dropped`, `a_resume_session_report_may_replace_a_live_identity`, `a_release_with_a_mismatched_session_proof_leaves_the_claim`, `a_state_report_with_identity_re_establishes_a_released_claim`, and the hook-runtime tests in the claude integration module.
- **mux attach: a roster agent-row click on a pane in a multi-pane tab lands focused on the clicked pane even when the tab's `%layout-change` lags the landing** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/{mod.rs,navigate.rs}`): the v0.71 landing (`5c4bde0e`) carries the clicked pane only through the reseed's synchronous drain — when the switched-to window's layout event has not arrived by drain time, the landing completes on the old layout, the re-focus is ignored, and the event's later install through the pump's `PendingWork` resets the focus to the window's FIRST pane (the reported multi-pane-tab wrong-pane). The landing now parks the clicked pane as `PendingWork::layout_focus`; the async install re-focuses it after applying the layout, a reseed's `clear_view_work` drops a stale park, and a fresh click supersedes one. Pinned by `focus_pane_landing_focus_survives_a_lagging_layout_event`, red-proven against the v0.71 landing. The split chord parks the fresh pane the same way when its layout event lags (`split_focus_survives_a_lagging_layout_event`).

## [0.71.1] - 2026-10-28

### Fixed
- **streaming: a client text frame no longer kills the mirror streamer session** (`3c3d5a13`; `src/streaming/client.rs`): `Client::recv()` — the tungstenite transport serving `par-term-streamer --mux-socket` — returned `StreamingError::InvalidMessage` on any TEXT frame, and the ws_session loop treats a recv error as fatal, so one control text frame from a probing client (pardeck sends one on every connect) tore down an otherwise healthy mirror session. The path now logs and skips text frames exactly like the axum `AxumTransport` already did, making text handling consistent across both server paths (kanban 01a16f84d1ac777c833425ba6dd522a9, filed from pardeck against 0.66.0, still present at 0.71.0). Pinned by `recv_skips_text_frames_instead_of_closing_session` (in-process duplex WebSocketStream), red-proven against the old recv.

## [0.71.0] - 2026-10-28

### Changed
- **parking_lot 0.12.5 → 0.13** (lock_api 0.4 → 0.5; root `Cargo.toml`, `crates/par-mux/Cargo.toml`, `crates/par-term-emu-core/Cargo.toml`): unblocks par-term crossing to parking_lot 0.13 — its workspace bump hit E0308 against core's exported `Arc<parking_lot::RwLock<Terminal>>` (kanban 01a184c8, filed from par-term's update-packages sweep). No source changes; tokio's own internal parking_lot 0.12 dependency stays in-tree independently and crosses no API boundary. The release carrying it rides the next release cut.

### Added
- **streaming: env-gated OpenTelemetry OTLP metrics for the streamer** (`d5bb185`; `src/streaming/metrics.rs` + `metrics/{otel,stub}.rs`, `Cargo.toml`, `src/bin/streaming_server/{main.rs,cli.rs}`, `benches/streaming_otel.rs`, `Makefile`, `docs/{STREAMING,CONFIG_REFERENCE}.md`): the `par-term-streamer` health series par-obs wants as a Grafana panel row — connected clients, active sessions, event-send and protobuf-encode latency histograms, connections/reconnects counters (a reconnect = connect to a session whose previous client left within 5 minutes), and mux control-socket errors — recorded through a no-op stub unless the new opt-in `otel` cargo feature is compiled, and silent (zero OTLP calls) unless `--otel-enabled`/`PAR_TERM_STREAMER_OTEL_ENABLED` is set. Default builds carry zero OpenTelemetry code (`make otel-check`, new in `checkall`, pins both directions), the opentelemetry/sdk/otlp trio is pinned at 0.33 lockstep (`cargo tree -d` clean), and the series are pinned by an in-memory-exporter unit test. See [STREAMING.md → OpenTelemetry Metrics](docs/STREAMING.md#opentelemetry-metrics).

### Fixed
- **mux: rebuilding Par-mux.app no longer revokes macOS TCC grants** (`5ddfac57`; `Makefile`): `make app` hardcoded `codesign -s -`, so every rebuild changed the bundle's ad-hoc signature, and macOS keys TCC grants (screen recording, microphone, local network) to that signature — each redeploy silently revoked every grant and re-prompted. The target now signs the launcher and the bundle with the first Developer ID Application identity in the keychain (hardened runtime + timestamp), falling back to ad-hoc with a loud warning only when no identity exists (CI runners); the stable identity makes grants survive rebuilds.
- **streaming: the streamer's OTLP exporter no longer fails silently** (`860fa01`; `src/streaming/metrics/otel.rs`, `src/bin/streaming_server/main.rs`): the 0.33 SDK compiles its error reporting out by default (`internal-logs` off), so a misconfigured endpoint or a dead collector vanished — a smoke run once claimed "exports shipped" while its series never landed — and a short-lived process's final flush could lose the race with runtime teardown. A delegating `ReportingExporter` now prints the first consecutive export failure and every 30th after (success resets), and `main` shuts the provider down explicitly via `OtelGuard::shutdown` while the async runtime is alive. Pinned by the reporting-exporter unit tests.
- **par-mux attach: a side-panel agent-row click on a pane in another window lands focused on the clicked pane** (`5c4bde0`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/navigate.rs`): the click's `select-pane` moved the daemon pointer and `switch-client` landed on the pane's window, but the re-seed seeded and focused the window's first pane — the renderer's focus still named the old window's pane, so `focused_pane_or_first` and the layout install both fell back to pane 0; a second click on the same row was the only way to focus the agent. The landing now carries the clicked pane through `land_window_focused`/`reseed_window_focused`: it seeds the size report and is re-applied after the layout install. Pinned by `focus_pane_on_another_window_lands_focused_on_the_clicked_pane`, red-proven against the old landing.
- **par-mux attach: reconnecting after a daemon restart no longer draws inside the dead session's scroll region** (`bfbe7e9`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/{mod.rs,pump.rs,render/sink.rs}`): a pane app's DECSTBM reaches the host terminal through passthrough bytes and outlives the daemon that pane ran under — the attach client cleared the screen (`ESC[2J ESC[H`) but reset the scroll region, origin mode, SGR, cursor visibility, and autowrap only on pump exit, so a reconnect (restart or not) drew the seed replay, the status row, and every later scroll inside a region sized for the pre-restart screen; render mode never reset it at all. One shared reset (`ESC[r ESC[?6l ESC[0m ESC[?25h ESC[?7h`) now precedes the clear at the guard's enter/drop, the resync replay, and render's full repaint, leaving the host shell a default terminal on detach as well. Pinned by `attach_entry_resets_stale_host_terminal_state` (both modes), red-proven against the old entry path.
- **streaming: a `workspace-active` dial's Connected handshake reports the resolved pane id** (`e0a3f80`; `src/streaming/{mux_factory.rs,session.rs}`, `docs/STREAMING.md`): the handshake echoed the requested session id verbatim, so a client dialing `?session=workspace-active` received `sessionID "workspace-active"` and could not label the landed pane without a second control round-trip. The mirror factory now shares the mirror's live pane handle with follows-active sessions at setup, and `build_connect_message` reports `pane-N` of the pane being mirrored right now (re-resolved after every workspace follow) in place of the sentinel; every other id — `pane-N` dials and the first-pane fallback — still echoes verbatim. Pinned by `a_workspace_active_handshake_names_the_resolved_pane`.

## [0.70.0] - 2026-10-28

### Added
- **mux: named layouts — save, load, list, and delete a pane tree by name** (`72357fa2`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/layouts.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/layouts.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/persist.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/modal_layouts.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): four control verbs — `save-layout <name>`, `load-layout [-f] <name>`, `list-layouts`, `kill-layout <name>` — capture the live tree into a named JSON layout in the daemon's layouts store, list the store, replace the live tree with a stored layout, or delete one. `load-layout` swaps the tree in place and refuses without `-f` when live panes the layout does not cover would be killed; the attach TUI menu gains a layouts dialog (save with overwrite confirm, load with pick and confirm) driving the same verbs. Pinned by the `mux_daemon` layout suites and the `mux_attach` dialog tests.
- **mux: a `--state-dir` daemon keeps named layouts beside its state file** (`19ace30b`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/layouts.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/persist.rs`): the layout verbs resolved the store through the platform state dir only, so a `--state-dir` daemon shared layouts with the default instance, and Windows tests — where the `dirs` crate ignores HOME/XDG_* — could not isolate the store by env. A persisting server now hands its layouts dir (the state file's directory + `layouts`) through dispatch; embedders that dispatch without a persisting server keep the platform default, and the daemon layout tests un-gate from `cfg(unix)`. Pinned by `layout_verbs_serve_the_dispatch_layouts_dir` and `save_layout_lands_beside_the_state_file`.

### Fixed
- **par-mux attach: a text drag ends at its release even while a full-pointer modal is up** (`5f67482a`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/input_route.rs`): the text-drag continuation sat below the menu/settings/layouts modal arms in the mouse router, so a drag armed before a modal opened never saw its release — the sweep stayed armed until the next release after the modal closed. The continuation now runs above the modal arms (a release always ends the drag; motion stays modal-gated by design, with the content coordinates computed locally via `host_to_content_clamped`). Pinned by `text_selection_release_under_a_full_pointer_modal_ends_the_selection`, red-proven against the old ordering.
- **par-mux attach: the save-name and rename prompts accept shifted CSI-u characters** (`a5633033`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/text_input.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/{modal_layouts.rs,modal_prompt.rs}`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/input.rs`): the layouts save prompt matched `(TermKey::Char, 0)` — modifiers exactly zero — so a shifted report like `ESC[64;2u` for `@` carried the shift bit, missed the arm, and fell into consume-nothing; the character never reached the buffer (the rename prompt accepted shift but had no ctrl/alt gate). One shared `prompt_text_char` predicate now serves both prompts: shift passes, and ctrl/alt chords no longer type characters into either buffer. Pinned by `csi_u_shifted_text_codepoint_decodes_as_char_with_shift`, `csi_u_shifted_chars_land_in_the_rename_prompt_buffer`, and `layouts_save_prompt_accepts_shifted_csi_u_chars`, red-proven against the old arm.
- **mux: load-layout names a corrupt layout file instead of surfacing serde's encoding text** (`c084eece`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/layouts.rs`): a corrupt `<name>.json` kept its raw serde Display (`state encoding failed: ...`), reading as an encoding problem rather than a broken file; the verb's error remap now says `corrupt layout: <name>` (NotFound still says `no such layout: <name>`). Pinned by `load_layout_bad_file_errors_without_swap`, red-proven against the old mapping.
- **mux: the Windows cross-target gate compiles again** (`3b151615`; `crates/par-mux/src/bin/par_mux/main.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/persist.rs`): the bin's unit tests (9c33e68f) drag the auto-attach block into the Windows cross-target check, where its unix-only `isatty`/`exec` lines are undefined and the detach-fork helper dead; the state-lock helpers (93f7dada) likewise leave unix-only locals dead on Windows. All are cfg-gated now, and the non-unix auto-attach fallback message names the platform truth. Host behavior unchanged.
- **par-mux attach: a drag released over the side panel no longer strands the pointer** (`ab40934a`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/input_route.rs`): in the mouse router the panel's strip branch ran before the in-flight drag continuation checks, and `sidebar_hit` consumes every motion and release — a divider drag or a text-selection sweep whose release landed over the visible panel never ended, leaving `drag`/`text_drag` armed: every later wheel was eaten by the pane-area guard (mouse "stops working" until reconnect), every hover kept re-driving `resize-pane` or extending the selection. Both continuation checks now run before the panel branch, and a release always ends a text drag (motion stays modal-gated). Pinned by `divider_drag_release_over_the_sidebar_ends_the_drag` and `text_selection_release_over_the_sidebar_ends_the_selection` (red-proven against the old ordering).
- **mux: a bare start hands off to the app wrapper only when no daemon serves** (`f19012b4`; `crates/par-mux/src/bin/par_mux/main.rs`): a bare `par-mux` against an already-serving daemon exec'd into the Par-mux.app launcher anyway, whose daemon.log redirect swallowed the follow-up attach — the TUI and the multiple-sessions refusal landed in the log and the invoking terminal saw a silent no-op. The hand-off now probes the socket with `connect_local_stream` first and runs only for a real start, restoring the direct attach path.
- **mux: the app-bundle launcher no longer requests microphone access** (`ece0787f`; `crates/par-mux/app/{launcher.c,permissions.m}`): the mic REQUEST aborts the launcher when the responsible bundle lacks `NSMicrophoneUsageDescription` (TCC `TCC_CRASHING_DUE_TO_PRIVACY_VIOLATION`), and under pane/GUI attribution the responsible bundle is par-term.app — so every pane-launched `par-mux` start died "Abort trap: 6" before the daemon could exec, and the client had already exec'd into the launcher so the bare-fork fallback never ran. The launcher now reads `AVAuthorizationStatus` only (no prompt, no crash; `notDetermined` is left alone — the pane's own app is still prompted for mic when it asks). Fix is live only after redeploying `Par-mux.app` (`make app`, then rm-before-cp into `/Applications` — the installed 2026-10-20 launcher still crashes).
- **mux: `--stop` inside a pane refuses until `--yes`/`--force`** (`9c33e68f`; `crates/par-mux/src/bin/par_mux/main.rs`, `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_nested.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): a `--stop` typed inside a par-mux pane kills the daemon hosting the caller's own shell — a session died exactly this way during the crash-loop restarts. When `PAR_MUX_ENV` is set and the target socket matches one of the pane's exports (`PAR_MUX_SOCKET` / `PAR_MUX_CONTROL_SOCKET`), the stop refuses with the explanation unless `--yes` or `--force` confirms it; a stop from outside, an explicit `--socket` naming a different daemon, and `--restart` (the deliberate kill-server exemption) never refuse. `--force` sheds its clap-level `requires = gen-config` for a dispatch-time check, so it now serves the stop confirmation too. The wrapper hand-off also moved after the nested-daemon refusal, so an inside-pane start keeps its deliberate serve-refusal and bare-restart semantics byte for byte instead of being silently rerouted through the app. Pinned by `stop_inside_pane_guard_branches` and `stop_inside_a_pane_refuses_until_confirmed`.
- **mux: panes keep a spawn-cwd floor, the exit clock tolerates stalls, and bare starts hand off to the app wrapper** (`13d7440c`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/{persist.rs,pane.rs}`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/server/{mod.rs,tests.rs}`, `crates/par-mux/src/bin/par_mux/main.rs`, `crates/par-mux/tests/{mux_daemon.rs,mux_hooks.rs}`, `docs/MUX.md`, `docs/CONFIG_REFERENCE.md`, `Makefile`): the cwd a save persists falls back to the directory a pane was spawned in when neither the shell's OSC 7 report nor the child's live cwd answers — agent TUIs never emit OSC 7 and churn-killed children have no readable cwd, which is why the crash-loop restores relaunched every agent in `$HOME` (the save/restore pipeline itself is proven correct end to end by `restore_spawns_each_pane_in_its_persisted_cwd` and `a_dead_panes_cwd_is_seeded_for_its_resume`). The exit-when-empty clock provably cannot run while its read is stalled — a held tree mutex past the whole grace produces no `empty_since` and no verdict, and genuine continued emptiness still fires after it (`exit_when_empty_clock_does_not_run_under_a_stalled_read`), the crash-loop card's load-tolerance criterion. And on macOS a detached start hands off to the installed `/Applications/Par-mux.app` launcher (original argv forwarded, `PAR_MUX_BIN` honored), so the daemon serves under the app bundle's TCC local-network grant instead of being blocked for phone/LAN clients; the hand-off prepends a hidden `--par-mux-in-app-wrapper` marker so it can never loop, the bundle's own binary and `--foreground` never hand off, `PAR_MUX_NO_APP_WRAPPER=1` opts out (documented in CONFIG_REFERENCE; the binary-spawning test suites opt out wholesale via the Makefile), and an exec failure degrades to the bare fork (`app_handoff_decision`).
- **mux: the daemon claims its state file, and every exit logs its reason** (`93f7dada`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/persist.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/server/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/bin/par_mux/main.rs`, `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_daemon.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): two live daemons on one state file race `write_state`'s fixed `<state>.tmp` — the 2026-10-27 crash loop: a socket-stealing restart orphaned the previous daemon (alive, still saving), the saves interleaved, the 161 MB state tore at byte ~107 MB (`trailing characters`), and the quarantine/restart churn dropped every attached client (15 daemon boots in one day). A daemon now holds an advisory `flock` on `<state>.lock` for its whole run — through the `--restart` fork, the serving child inherits the open file description — and a second start on the same state refuses after a 5 s handoff window, naming the holder's pid; Windows serves in-process and takes no lock yet. Every exit also logs its reason (`daemon exiting (stop request|exit-when-empty|listener fault)`), the forensics line the crash loop never had. Pinned by `state_lock_refuses_a_second_holder_until_released`, `leftover_tmp_never_defeats_the_state`, `persist_worker_survives_a_failed_write_and_completes_the_next`, `second_daemon_refuses_an_occupied_state_file`, and `daemon_exits_log_their_reason`.
- **mux: `resize-pane` moves the pressed divider, not the nearest ancestor's** (`2042ba9e`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/layout.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/tree/layout_ops.rs`): resizing a pane whose boundary sat deeper than its nearest same-axis split moved a DIFFERENT divider — with a right-leaning three-pane row, dragging the rightmost divider moved the left one, and the keyboard path proportionally rescaled both left panes (`set_leaf_extent` adjusted the pane's nearest same-axis ancestor, which for right-leaning trees is the wrong split). The relative resize now walks to the deepest split whose boundary the pane touches on the pressed side, funnels the delta to the pane with every off-path extent pinned at its pre-move value, and cascades the absorption down the boundary-adjacent chain, so only the panes touching the moved divider change; a pane spanning the pressed side falls back to its opposite divider (the T4.C edge rule), and no divider at all stays an error. Pinned by the `toward_*` unit tests (`toward_grows_the_target_and_absorbs_at_the_boundary_neighbor`, `toward_keeps_far_panes_at_a_deeper_boundary`, `toward_through_a_cross_axis_split_moves_the_shared_boundary`) and the `mux_attach` resize suites.
- **mux: a `--restart` relaunch writes the shared `daemon.log` and keeps hook debug lines** (`44177748`; `crates/par-mux/src/bin/par_mux/main.rs`, `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_hooks.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the self-relaunch pointed the detached daemon's stderr at `<state>.json.log` — a sibling nobody tails — while `daemon.log` was only ever written by launchd and the Par-mux.app launcher, so a restarted daemon's forensics trail vanished (observed 2026-10-26/27: the live daemon wrote neither). The default instance now continues `~/Library/Logs/par-mux/daemon.log` (macOS; `daemon.log` beside the state file elsewhere); other instances and explicit state dirs keep `<state>.log`, so test daemons never touch the default log. The stderr logger also admits Debug records from `par_mux::mux::hooks` only, putting the per-hook-report forensics line (256f6ab) on disk. Pinned by `restart_relaunch_writes_the_default_daemon_log_with_hook_debug_lines` (a real `--restart` plus a hook report through the socket, red-proven against the old `main.rs`); deploying the fix to the live daemon still needs `make par-mux-install` + `make app-restart`.
- **mux: send-keys `-l` byte-exactness pinned** (`917436fa`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/tests.rs`, `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_send_keys.rs`): a roster report of caret-notation mangling did not reproduce — control bytes reach the pty byte-exact (`1b5d3107` hex-dumped off a raw-mode pane) and single-quoted backslashes survive the argument split; the sighting traced to the pane tty's cooked-mode echo. Five pinning tests keep a future parser change from regressing either.
- **par-mux attach: floored tab cells keep readable text when the strip overflows, and a tab-width change repaints at once** (`b1052f54`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/tabs.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/session.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/tests/chords.rs`): with `tab-width-min` set, a strip too narrow for every tab at the floor degraded to stage 3's budget-1 text — wide blocks showing a single character plus an ellipsis; the visible run now budgets its text to fill the floored cell (`min − 2·pad − 1` name columns), so a 20-column tab shows up to 16 name columns, and the unbounded default layout keeps budget 1. A `tab-width-min`/`tab-width-max` change through the settings dialog (or the reload chord) repainted only at the next periodic status refresh (~5 s); `apply_chords` now repaints the strip the moment the bounds change. Pinned by `stage3_with_a_floor_fills_the_cell_with_text` and `tab_width_change_repaints_the_strip_immediately`.

## [0.69.0] - 2026-10-27

### Changed
- **Gate: load-sensitive tests are labeled and leave the default gate** (`04f3471`; Makefile`, `crates/par-mux/tests/{mux_daemon,mux_cli,mux_attach,mux_reattach,mux_end_to_end,mux_restart}.rs`, render tick tests, `CLAUDE.md`): the PTY/attach integration suites and timing-bound render tests carry `#[ignore = "load-sensitive ..."]`, so default `cargo`/`make checkall` runs skip them with a loud deferred count instead of flaking on a loaded host; the new `make test-rust-sensitive` runs exactly the labeled set, and `make checkall-ci` keeps full coverage via nextest `include-ignored` — it remains the done gate for changes touching labeled tests (bare `checkall` no longer covers them).
- **Dependencies: sweep to latest stable across Rust, Python, the web frontend, and CI** (`b16168b6`): `cargo update` bumps openssl-probe 0.2.2 and serial2 0.2.39 (14 crates correctly held at semver majors — pyo3 0.30, syn 3, toml 1.1, zbus 6, criterion 0.8, parking_lot 0.13, …; the fuzz lockfile re-locked locally), `uv lock --upgrade` bumps pytest-asyncio 1.4.1 with its `pyproject.toml` floor synced to `>=1.4.1`, and ncu + `bun install` bumps autoprefixer 10.6.2, postcss 8.5.32, pako 3.0.3 with the static export rebuilt. Action pins: `taiki-e/install-action` v2.87.32 and `claude-code-action` v1.0.263; every other pinned action was already current.

### Added
- **par-mux attach: the pointer wheel scrolls menus, dialogs, and pickers** (`684e9ba`)
- **streaming: mux graphics frames and `graphics_added` events carry their pane** (`13ca1c3`; proto/terminal.proto`, `src/streaming/{protocol.rs,terminal.pb.rs,proto_golden_tests.rs,inline_image.rs,mux_factory.rs,session.rs}`, `web-terminal-frontend/lib/proto/`, `tests/test_streaming_dict_api.py`, `docs/{MUX.md,STREAMING.md}`): every `InlineImage` frame and `GraphicsAdded` announce gains an optional `pane` field holding the serving pane's session-style id (`pane-12`; absent on non-mux traffic and on old servers — both directions compatible, the `%gfx` seed manifest stays 6-field). The mirror factory keeps a graphics-id → pane side map: seed frames tag the seed request's pane, live frames the pane served at placement, a `workspace-active` re-seed replaces the entries of the frames it replaces, and `Connected.graphics` stamps each replayed frame from the same map, so `workspace-active` mirrors and future multi-pane clients can attribute a frame to its pane. Pinned by `a_graphic_placed_before_attach_replays_in_connected_graphics`, `a_workspace_reseed_tags_frames_with_the_new_pane`, the extended `a_pane_graphic_streams_as_an_inline_image_frame`, and the proto golden suite.
- **mux: workspace exit actions — remove, hold, or drop to a shell — reachable from the attach TUI** (`684e9ba`; crates/par-mux/src/mux/{config.rs,command/{mod.rs,sessions.rs},dispatch/{mod.rs,sessions.rs},tree/{mod.rs,lifecycle.rs},persist.rs,server/reap.rs}`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/{mod.rs,modal_menu.rs}`, `docs/MUX.md`): a new `[daemon] exit-action` key (default `remove`) sets the daemon-wide default for what happens when a pane's child exits, folding the legacy `remain-on-exit = true` to `hold` when the key is absent; `set-exit-action -t <workspace> [remove|hold|shell]` (bare form = query, replies the effective value) overrides it per workspace and persists in the state save, so restart-resumed (`[daemon.agent-append]`) panes inherit their workspace's choice. `hold` keeps dead panes for `respawn-pane`; `shell` supersedes keep-one-tab by respawning every dead pane into the user's default shell past the same 1 s crash-loop guard the keep-one-tab respawn uses. The workspace context menu carries three `exit:` rows (after `respawn on exit`) that send the verb and flash the resulting value. Pinned by `exit_action_resolves_from_key_remain_on_exit_and_defaults`, `reload_applies_exit_action_live`, `parses_set_exit_action_values_and_query`, `set_exit_action_queries_replies_and_persists`, `exit_action_shell_respawns_the_dead_pane_into_a_shell`, and `workspace_menu_exit_rows_send_set_exit_action_and_flash`.
- **par-mux attach: the tab strip's per-tab width is configurable with a floor and a cap** (`65b7a42`; crates/par-mux/src/mux/config.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/tabs.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/{session.rs,navigate.rs,modal_settings.rs}`, `docs/MUX.md`): two new `[client]` keys — `tab-width-min` (default 0 = no floor) pads a short tab's block up to the floor and `tab-width-max` (default 0 = no cap) truncates a longer name with an ellipsis at the cap, max winning when min exceeds it; both live on reload and ship as `general`-tab settings rows (the standing TUI-settings rule), written through the same generic key path. gen-config emits both defaults. Pinned by `min_width_pads_short_tabs_up_to_the_floor`, `max_width_truncates_long_names_to_the_cap`, `a_min_above_the_cap_clamps_to_the_cap`, and the extended `herdr_parity_display_options_resolve_and_gen_config_emits`.
- **par-mux: named layouts save and restore the whole tree** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/persist.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/layouts.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/`, `docs/MUX.md`, `crates/par-mux/schema/par-mux-commands.json`): four control verbs — `save-layout <name>`, `load-layout [-f] <name>`, `list-layouts`, and `kill-layout <name>` — store the live tree under `<platform state dir>/par-mux/layouts/<name>.json` in the persistence format. `load-layout` replaces the live tree and respawns the stored panes, refusing without `-f` while any live pane exists; names are `[A-Za-z0-9._-]+`. The attach TUI's command menu gains a `Layouts…` row opening a dialog with `Save…` (name prompt, overwrite confirmation) and `Load…` (pick list, "Replace current panes?" confirmation, sends `-f` after it). On Windows the layouts directory is `%APPDATA%\par-mux\layouts`. See [Named Layouts](docs/MUX.md#named-layouts).
- **par-mux: a `--state-dir` daemon serves named layouts beside its state file** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/persist.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/{mod.rs,layouts.rs}`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/server/{mod.rs,client.rs,reap.rs}`, `crates/par-mux/tests/{common/mod.rs,mux_daemon.rs}`, `docs/MUX.md`): the layout verbs resolved the store through `persist::platform_state_dir()` only, so a `--state-dir` daemon kept layouts in the shared platform dir and Windows tests — where the `dirs` crate ignores `HOME`/`XDG_*` — could not isolate the store by env. A persisting server now hands its layouts dir (the state file's directory + `layouts`) through dispatch; embedders and tests that dispatch without a persisting server keep the platform default. The daemon layout tests un-gate from `#[cfg(unix)]` — the store isolates by the flag on every platform; `save_load_layout_round_trip` stays Unix-gated on the pane-info process snapshot alone. Pinned by `layout_verbs_serve_the_dispatch_layouts_dir` and `save_layout_lands_beside_the_state_file`.

### Fixed
- **mux: reload-config and `set-option remain-on-exit` re-fold the derived exit action, so a flipped config stops holding panes** (`731ae58`)
- **gate: the isolated re-exec helper runs labeled tests via include-ignored, and the mux-ci nextest legs take run-ignored all** (`d2e3505`, `27dbe58`)
- **web: pane upload UI driving the chunked upload protocol** (`02425d7`)
- **streaming: a mux pane's graphics placed before the mirror attached now replay to late joiners** (`e48db81`; src/streaming/mux_factory.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/client.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/{mod.rs,panes.rs}`, `docs/MUX.md`): the mirror's seed (`refresh-client -t %N`) reproduced the visible grid but not the pane's graphics placements, so `Connected.graphics` was empty for anything placed before attach — a phone reconnect or web reload showed the pane without its images until a new one was placed. The seed now carries them: a `-G` flag appends a `%graphics-begin`/`%graphics-end` manifest (placement id, cell position, size, base64 RGBA per graphic, newest first, bounded like the connect replay) to the seed reply, the factory ingests it into the mirror store through the same `apply_inline_image` path live frames use (ids offset out of the mirror counter's range), and the workspace re-seed path gets the same treatment; an older daemon's rejection of the unknown flag falls back to the plain screen-only seed. Live frames are unchanged. Pinned by `a_graphic_placed_before_attach_replays_in_connected_graphics` (attach after placement replays; a placement after attach still streams live) and `refresh_client_seed_with_graphics_carries_a_placement_manifest`.
- **mux: two suite tests hardened against host-shaped flakes** (`crates/par-mux/tests/mux_cli.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/agents_tests.rs`): the discovery test asserts cold instead of polling for the registry write that trails the listener bind (two failures at host load ~700 with the identical tree), and the agent-append resume test string-compared tempfile's unresolved temp path against the kernel-cwd fallback's resolved form — it only ever passed where host shells emit OSC 7 (deterministic fail on MiniMac, pass on MacDaddy). Both compare/poll with the same 10 s bounds the suite uses elsewhere; pinned by `agent_start_appends_the_configured_resume_words` and `list_servers_prunes_only_dead_entries_and_reregistration_is_a_noop`.
- **mux: a resumed agent's SessionStart identity report now classifies the pane immediately** (`6c3d864`; crates/par-mux/src/mux/scrape.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/hooks/report.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the report carries no state and the roster lists only panes WITH state, so a resumed session stayed invisible until its first state event — hours for a quiet session. The session-report path now runs the scrape tick's evaluate arm once before replying (scrape provenance; a hook-stated claim is never re-classified), making the pane roster-visible on its resume hook alone. Pinned by `a_session_report_classifies_a_stateless_claim_immediately` and `a_session_report_never_reclassifies_a_hook_stated_claim`.
- **par-mux attach: an agent-row click lights its row on the click's own frame** (`5b6a1cd`; crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/navigate.rs`, `session.rs`, `render/tests/session.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): clicking an agent in the side panel switched to that agent but the row's highlight waited for the throttled status refresh — a refresh a quiet pane may not tick for until the next input event, which is exactly what a second click was. The click's own path now moves the client-local focus (`renderer.focused()`, what the panel's rows activate on) and rebuilds the panel from the cache; a cross-window click's landing reseed repaints the panel again with the landed focus. Pinned by `sidebar_agent_row_click_highlights_the_row_immediately`.
- **mux: daemon startup sweeps upload spool files orphaned by dead daemons** (`d30ecdf`; crates/par-mux/src/mux/uploads.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/bin/par_mux/main.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): committed staging files persist for pane apps by design and OS temp cleanup was the only backstop, so files a dead daemon left behind lingered indefinitely. A daemon start now deletes `upload-*` staging files older than 24 h — in-flight uploads die with their daemon, so no concurrently running daemon's upload can be that old and the age guard is the concurrency guard; live uploads are never touched. The sweep fails open (an error logs and daemon start continues) and logs what it removed. Pinned by `startup_sweep_deletes_stale_upload_files_and_keeps_fresh_ones`.
- **mux: the daemon's background cadence no longer shares the accept loop's failure domain** (`256f6ab`; crates/par-mux/src/mux/server/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/hooks/mod.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the scrape tick, the dead-pane reap, the plugin hook reaps, and the exit-when-empty check all ran inline in the accept loop's idle arm — one wedged call between accepts froze every duty at once (a labeled pane went unscraped for ~50 minutes on 2026-10-26 while control commands stayed instant). They now run on a dedicated cadence worker thread (10 ms tick, joined bounded at shutdown like the probe and badge workers; the worker's exit-when-empty verdict rides a shared `exit_request` back to the loop), and hook reports log a debug line — pane, method, accepted/rejected — the restart-roster forensics trail that investigation lacked. Pinned by `cadence_advances_while_a_slow_client_holds_its_connection` (a registered client that stops draining its socket while a pane floods `%output` cannot stall the cadence: control round trips stay instant and the emptied tree still exits).

## [0.68.0] - 2026-10-26

### Added
- **streaming: the connect handshake's graphics replay is bounded and seeds newest first** (`bd2a26c1`; src/streaming/session.rs`, `tests/test_streaming.rs`, `docs/STREAMING.md`, `docs/SECURITY.md`): `build_connect_message` walked the WHOLE graphics store into `Connected.graphics` — a long-lived pane's store replayed every image ever placed to each new client. The replay now seeds NEWEST graphics first (the ones likeliest still on screen) and stops at the first frame that would bust either bound — `MAX_CONNECT_REPLAY_FRAMES` (24) or `MAX_CONNECT_REPLAY_BYTES` (8 MiB aggregate frame payload, never more than the single-frame wire cap); the skipped tail is logged. Constants live beside the session's other caps (`/// cap:` annotations, SECURITY.md's caps table regenerated); no new config surface. Pinned by `connect_replay_is_bounded_and_seeds_newest_graphics_first` (a 30-image store replays 24, ids newest-first) and `connect_replay_byte_budget_stops_mid_store` (twelve 1 MiB store graphics replay eight and stop — real placements cannot build that store on a 24-row screen, so the bound is tested against the store the replay reads, through the public mutable store).
- **streaming: client uploads reach the daemon through the hub mux-control channel** (`bd2a26c1`; proto/terminal.proto`, `src/streaming/protocol.rs`, `proto.rs`, `server/mod.rs`, `server/messages.rs`, `terminal.pb.rs`, `proto_golden_tests.rs`, `src/streaming/mux_factory.rs`, `docs/STREAMING.md`, `docs/SECURITY.md`): the phone drives the daemon's new upload verbs over the WebSocket: `upload_begin`/`upload_chunk`/`upload_commit`/`upload_abort` client messages and `upload_started`/`upload_finished` server messages (chunk payload capped at the new `MAX_UPLOAD_CHUNK_BYTES`, 64 KiB, enforced by the `upload_chunk_data` wire rule at both boundaries; upload replies are always-deliver direct replies, unfiltered by event subscriptions). Each mux-backed session gets an `UploadForwarder` (`MuxUploadPipe`): its OWN daemon control connection (the mirror link's is read solely by its drain thread), one bounded op queue drained by one worker thread — the serialization that keeps a back-to-back `upload_chunk`+`upload_commit` in flight order, a race the per-verb-handler spawns of a simpler shape lose — with every verb's reply block read and the pane resolved at commit time from the live link. Handlers reply synchronously (bounded wait on the one-shot reply channel; a wedged daemon stalls only the uploading connection). Non-mux sessions refuse every verb with an `upload_finished` error. Pinned by `a_client_upload_reaches_the_pane_as_a_bracket_pasted_path` (in-process daemon + streamer + a real WebSocket client: begin id, chunk, commit path lands bracket-pasted in the pane's capture, byte-exact file, re-begin after settle, abort settles with the aborted reason), `upload_messages_without_a_mux_pipe_are_refused_inline`, and the golden fixtures (45 server / 16 client variants).
- **par-mux: chunked client-upload control verbs stage files for a pane** (`bd2a26c1`; crates/par-mux/src/mux/uploads.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/uploads.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/uploads.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/server/reap.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`, `docs/SECURITY.md`, `crates/par-mux/schema/par-mux-commands.json`): a client (the phone, over the mux-control channel) sends a file into a pane with four control verbs — `upload-begin <filename> <total-bytes>` (replies the upload id), `upload-chunk <id> <base64>`, `upload-commit -t <pane> <id>` (replies the materialized absolute path and bracket-pastes THAT PATH into the pane, framed by the pane's own bracketed-paste markers when DECSET 2004 is on), and `upload-abort <id>`. This is the control-verb half of the documented input semantic: the daemon never injects an escape sequence carrying the bytes into pane input — the pane app reads the file it is pointed at. Staging (`UploadRegistry`, a daemon-wide global like the git badge cache) spools each upload to `$TMPDIR/par-mux-uploads/` (dir 0700, files 0600) and is bounded four ways — 64 KiB per chunk, 100 MiB per upload, 4,096 chunks, 8 concurrent uploads — plus idle (30 s) and total (10 min) timeouts whose sweep runs inside every verb and on the reap tick; any rejected chunk aborts the upload and invalidates the id. Filenames are sanitized to bare names (path prefixes stripped, control characters and `<>:"|?*\` refused), a restart-id collision suffixes `-N` instead of failing, committed files persist for the pane app (never daemon-deleted), and in-flight staging is never persisted. Pinned by `the_upload_staging_lifecycle_and_bounds_hold` (round trip, every cap, abort cleanup, sweep, sanitization), the `upload_*` parser tests, and `paste_body_frames_only_when_the_pane_has_bracketed_paste_on`.
- **streaming: inline-image frames carry their cell position** (`06c1e2e3`; proto/terminal.proto`, `src/streaming/protocol.rs`, `src/streaming/inline_image.rs`, `src/streaming/terminal.pb.rs`, `proto_golden_tests.rs`, `tests/test_streaming.rs`, `tests/test_streaming_dict_api.py`, `src/streaming/mux_factory.rs`, `web-terminal-frontend/`): a frame placed a mirrored image at (0, 0) because it carried no placement. `ServerMessage::InlineImage` gains `col`/`row` (wire fields 7/8, plain uint32) — `inline_image_frame` reads the stored graphic's `position`, and `apply_inline_image` places the ingested graphic there instead of the origin (a re-sent frame re-places as well as replaces). The web renderer saves the cursor, moves it to the frame's cell (VT 1-indexed), draws the iTerm2 sequence, and restores, so replayed frames land where the store says instead of wherever the cursor happens to sit. Pinned by the golden fixture (now carrying 12/34), `inline_image_round_trip_and_payload_cap` (col/row survive JSON + protobuf), `frame_fetch_resolves_the_event_id_to_stored_pixels` / `fabricated_frame_ingests_into_the_pane_emulator` (placement round-trips through the store both directions), the mirror-drain assertion tying the frame's cell to the mirror store, and the frontend placement test.
- **streaming: a late joiner's Connected replays the frames already on screen** (`06c1e2e3`; proto/terminal.proto`, `src/streaming/protocol.rs`, `src/streaming/session.rs`, `src/streaming/inline_image.rs`, `src/streaming/terminal.pb.rs`, `src/streaming/proto_golden_tests.rs`, `tests/test_streaming.rs`, `docs/STREAMING.md`, `docs/MUX.md`, `web-terminal-frontend/`): `Connected` gains `graphics` (repeated `InlineImage`, wire field 13) — `build_connect_message` seeds every store graphic as a cap-checked `InlineImageFrame` carrying its cell position (the same wire message the `inline_image` variant maps, behind a re-used app struct rather than nested server messages), so a client connecting after images were placed renders them instead of a text-only history. Replayed frames tag no pane: they belong to the session's own terminal. The web client stashes the seed at connect and renders it once the post-connect screen restore lands (cleared after one render, so resize-triggered refreshes never re-draw). Pinned by the golden `Connected` fixture (byte-exact with one frame), the nested over-cap `Err(InvalidMessage)` lossy case, and `test_connect_message_replays_existing_graphics_with_positions`.
- **par-mux attach: the pointer takes the pointing shape over clickable tabs** (`fd914ba7`; crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/input_route.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/pointer.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/tabs.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/tests/session.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): with `change-cursor-shape` on (the new default) a no-button hover over a tab block in the tab strip writes OSC 22 `pointer` plus its X11 `hand2` synonym — the I-beam is the text-area default and wrong for a clickable tab; leaving the tab, hovering the bar between tabs, a modal up, or the option off all restore the terminal's own pointer. Pinned by `tab_hover_shapes_the_pointer`.

### Changed
- **par-mux: `[client]` `drag-cursor-shape` is now `change-cursor-shape` and defaults ON** (`fd914ba7`; crates/par-mux/src/mux/config.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/{modal_settings.rs,input_route.rs,mod.rs,pointer.rs,session.rs,cursor.rs,navigate.rs}`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/tests/{session.rs,mod.rs}`, `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_attach.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the setting's semantics outgrew the drag-only name and the off default read as "the resize cue is a bug" — the shaping is now the built-in behavior, with `change-cursor-shape = false` as the classic-look opt-out. The `Option<bool>` merge is unchanged: an explicit `false` in an existing file keeps beating the default; the `[client]` section tolerates unknown keys, so a pre-rename file loads with the old key ignored and the new default applying. gen-config emits `change-cursor-shape = true`, and the settings dialog's toggle (now labeled `change cursor shape`) reads and writes the new key. Pinned by `change_cursor_shape_renamed_key_and_merge` and the updated `herdr_parity_display_options_resolve_and_gen_config_emits`, `change_cursor_shape_shapes_the_cursor_during_a_drag`/`_shapes_the_pointer` and `render_mode_change_cursor_shape_writes_osc22_to_the_host`.
### Fixed
- **par-mux: the attach seed's status snapshot rides the pump's `send_then` continuation** (`4b4febfe`; crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/session.rs`, `attach/render/mod.rs`, `render/tests/session.rs`): on a `client-snapshot` daemon the seed no longer runs the status refresh synchronously — the last snapshot call site that parked the client before its first frame. The snapshot is written through the ENH-049 machinery the pump uses (one in flight; a dirty mark landing mid-flight stays set for a later re-issue) and the reply applies as a pump-tick continuation (`apply_seed_snapshot`) that runs the seed's post-refresh steps — the transition baseline adoption with the pre-refresh pushes left out of it, the side panel's install from the committed roster, the query-free pane titles — around the same facts commit and landing contract the pump's continuation runs (the shared `land_gone_view`). The seed's observable outcome is unchanged; the pinned `the_seed_installs_the_agents_section_after_the_roster_lands` now drives the reply through the settle helper and pins the before/after (facts uncommitted at seed return, agents section installed once it lands). The legacy per-fact seed path stays synchronous.
- **par-mux: the SessionGone landing resolves from the cached roster first** (`4b4febfe`; crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/status.rs`, `attach/render/mod.rs`, `attach/render/navigate.rs`, `render/tests/session.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the snapshot continuation's cascade landing issued interactive (1 s-bounded) queries even when the last snapshot had already listed a survivor of the gone window. The landing now resolves from the cached window list first — the cached active window when it survives the gone one, else the first listed sibling (`StatusState::cached_cascade_landing`) — and reseeds onto it with no query, so a pathological daemon parks nothing extra on the landing path; a cache with no survivor, or a cached landing that died before it could be shown (`reseed_window` now reports whether the view moved), walks the interactive ladder as before. The legacy path's ladder is unchanged. Pinned by `cached_cascade_landing_picks_a_survivor_from_the_cache`, `a_gone_window_lands_on_the_cached_sibling_without_queries`, and `a_dead_cached_landing_falls_back_to_the_interactive_ladder`.
- **par-mux: the re-seed's status snapshot rides the pump's `send_then` continuation too** (`faf8d0e0`; crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/navigate.rs`, `attach/render/session.rs`, `render/tests/session.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): `reseed_window` still called `StatusState::refresh` inline — the last synchronous snapshot call site on `client-snapshot` daemons, parking the view mid-switch for the round trip the seed and pump already write without waiting. The re-seed now arms the same one-in-flight machinery (a snapshot already owed leaves the bar to it and re-marks the state dirty, so the owed reply's application re-issues for the new window) and its reply applies as a pump-tick continuation (`apply_reseed_snapshot`) running the re-seed's own post-refresh step — the transition baseline seed and the bar/strip repaints with the strip's scroll reset — while a session gone mid-switch lands through the shared gone-view contract instead of the sync path's `return false`. The legacy per-fact refresh keeps the synchronous round (its `SessionGone` still returns `false` to the landing caller's ladder); no inline snapshot round trip remains on snapshot daemons outside explicit continuations. Pinned by `a_reseed_issues_its_snapshot_without_blocking_and_applies_on_dispatch`.
- **par-mux: a push handled while the seed's snapshot is in flight stays out of the transition baseline** (`faf8d0e0`; crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/status_patch.rs`, `attach/render/mod.rs`, `attach/render/session.rs`, `render/tests/session.rs`): the async seed opened a race its sync path closed. A push handled between the snapshot write and the continuation's baseline adoption toasts (nothing is committed to judge it against), but `record_pushed_state` seeds no baseline from the uncommitted view — so when the adoption then took the snapshot's roster, a pane the snapshot already showed in the pushed state entered the baseline on a record the push stream never made, and an identical rebroadcast (`pane.report_agent_session` re-announcing the held state) suppressed instead of toasting. Pushes handled while the seed's snapshot is in flight now join a raced set at the push handler (the sync path's pre-refresh pushes, which the adoption excludes); the raced panes stay out of the baseline, so the rebroadcast toasts — and once recorded, identical repeats stay silent again. Pinned by `a_push_handled_mid_seed_stays_out_of_the_baseline_and_its_rebroadcast_toasts`.
- **par-mux attach: the tab context menu opens under the clicked tab** (`fd914ba7`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/{renderer.rs,mod.rs,input_route.rs}`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/tests/session.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): a right press on a tab anchored its menu dead-center in the terminal — far from the pointer and wrong for a row-level target. The menu now pins its left ring to the tab's left edge and its top ring to the first row below the strip, pulling back left for far-right tabs and up when the rows below cannot hold it. The overlay anchor grew a direction: `Above` keeps the command menu's chip-hugging opening, `Below` is the tab menu's. Pinned by `tab_menu_opens_under_the_clicked_tab` and the `Below` cases in `anchored_overlay_stays_inside_the_frame`.

## [0.67.0] - 2026-10-25

### Added
- **streaming: inline-image frames render and stream end-to-end** (`ecee59b6`; `src/streaming/inline_image.rs`, `src/streaming/mod.rs`, `src/bin/streaming_server/bootstrap.rs`, `src/streaming/mux_factory.rs`, `crates/par-term-emu-core/src/terminal/event.rs`, `event_fields.rs`, `src/streaming/protocol.rs`, `docs/STREAMING.md`, `docs/MUX.md`, `web-terminal-frontend/`): the render half lands on the wire half's `ServerMessage::InlineImage`. `TerminalEvent::GraphicsAdded` now carries the placement's store id (`image_id`) beside the row, raised by all three producers (kitty, Sixel, iTerm2), and the new `streaming::inline_image` module turns it into traffic: `inline_image_frame` fetches a stored graphic by id and builds the frame (format tag `rgba`, payload checked against `MAX_INLINE_IMAGE_BYTES` — oversize is logged and skipped, never truncated or panicked), `event_messages` is the event-pump seam that emits the cheap `graphics_added` announce plus the frame, and `apply_inline_image` ingests a received frame into a mirror terminal's graphics machinery (replace-on-same-id, `rgba`-only). The standalone streamer's event forwarder converts every event through the seam (pane id `None`); mux-backed mirrors drain their terminal's events in the `%output` arm and stream the announce plus a `pane_id`-tagged frame, guarded by a per-mirror sent-set so a graphic streams once, not on every status poll. The web frontend dispatches `inlineImage` and re-materializes an `rgba` frame as the iTerm2 sequence the xterm ImageAddon already decodes (`lib/inline-image.ts`). STREAMING.md documents the frame, the emission, the client render contract, and the output-only input semantic: the daemon never injects escape sequences into pane input — a wanted input path is a control verb (e.g. `par-mux paste-image`) that materializes to a temp file and bracket-pastes the path. Pinned by `frame_fetch_resolves_the_event_id_to_stored_pixels`, `event_pump_emits_announce_plus_frame`, `fabricated_frame_ingests_into_the_pane_emulator`, `a_pane_graphic_streams_as_an_inline_image_frame` (in-process daemon, red-path diagnostics included), and `inline_image_round_trip_and_payload_cap`. Completes the streaming half of card 01a1713f888371bd84b2ec978f065f30 (wire half: ea6baf04).
- **streaming: inline-image frames on the wire** (`ea6baf04`; `proto/terminal.proto`, `src/streaming/protocol.rs`, `proto.rs`, `server/mod.rs`, `terminal.pb.rs`, `proto_golden_tests.rs`, `tests/test_streaming.rs`, `docs/SECURITY.md`, `web-terminal-frontend/lib/proto/terminal_pb.ts`, `web_term/`): the wire gains a frame-typed `ServerMessage::InlineImage` (terminal graphic id, optional par-mux pane id, format tag, dimensions, bytes) so mux mirrors can receive inline images — today OSC 1337's `InlineImageDropped` is diagnostic-only and nothing image-bearing crossed the stream. Payloads cap at 8 MiB (`MAX_INLINE_IMAGE_BYTES`), enforced server-side at encode and client-side by the `inline_image_data` wire rule; the zlib decompression ceiling rises 1 MiB → 9 MiB so the largest legitimate frame can decode — the bound stays hard and the WS inbound frame cap still dominates (see docs/SECURITY.md's caps table). Emission is the render half's work: no core event carries image data yet, so nothing streams until that lands. Pinned by the golden fixtures (42 → 43: a byte-exact full-frame round trip, the empty frame, and the over-cap `Err(InvalidMessage)`), and `inline_image_round_trip_and_payload_cap`. Card 01a1713f888371bd84b2ec978f065f30 (wire half; render half: `ecee59b6` above).
- **par-mux: the move-tab actions surface in the TUI — tab-menu and palette rows for left/right/start/end** (`0da5d551`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/modal_menu.rs`, `render/mod.rs`, `modal_picker.rs`, `attach/panels.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the move-tab chords (`prefix <` / `prefix >`) had no visible surface, and no move-to-start/end existed anywhere. The tab context menu (a right press on a tab) gains `move left` / `move right` / `move to start` / `move to end` — `move-window -s {id} -t {slot}` for the RIGHT-CLICKED tab from the client's cached window order (the daemon clamps out-of-range slots to the ends), a no-op move sending nothing, an edge left/right flashing `already the first/last tab` exactly as the chords do. The command palette gains `move tab left` / `move tab right` (each spelling its effective chord) and `move tab to start` / `move tab to end` (chordless), all running for the shown window. Pinned by `tab_menu_move_rows_reorder_the_targeted_tab`, `palette_move_tab_rows_send_the_move_window_command`, the re-pointed `compose_menu_panel_maps_rows_to_actions` / `menu_arrows_move_the_selection_across_header_and_footer`, and the move-row asserts in `palette_type_to_filter_and_keybind_rows`.
- **par-mux: the command palette shows each action's live keybind and filters from the first keystroke** (`59e5df87`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/panels.rs`, `attach/render/modal_picker.rs`, `attach/tests.rs`, `render/tests/chords.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): palette rows carry their action's EFFECTIVE chord in the keybinds panel's spelling (`C-b x`) — from the live config, so a remap shows the remapped key, and the chordless settings row shows none — and the palette opens with its filter live: typing filters with no `/` first, a chord fragment matches its action too, arrows move the selection without leaving the filter, and Enter runs the selected action straight from the filter. The session/workspace pickers keep their `/` machinery unchanged (their compose now takes the footer line, the palette spelling its own). Pinned by `palette_type_to_filter_and_keybind_rows`, `palette_arrows_move_while_filtering`, `palette_action_chord_spells_the_live_binding`, and the footer-parameterized `compose_picker_panel_filters_marks_and_maps_clicks`.

### Fixed
- **par-mux: the attach status refresh stops blocking the pump** (`3145cdfa`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/mod.rs`, `attach/status.rs`, `render/tests/session.rs`): on a `client-snapshot` daemon the render pump's status step now writes the snapshot through the ENH-049 `send_then` machinery and keeps reading stdin while the reply is owed — a pathological daemon stall no longer parks input for one `STATUS_TIMEOUT` (500 ms) bound per pass. The reply applies as a pump-tick continuation (`apply_status_snapshot`): facts commit through the shared `StatusState::apply_snapshot_reply` (one classification site with the legacy sync path), the bar and strip repaint, and the nothing-left-to-show `End` reaches the pump through a `status_end` flag the continuation raises. One snapshot in flight at a time; a reply owed past the timeout is abandoned lazily at the next dirty attempt with no immediate re-issue (the storm rule stands), and a reply for a window the view already left is dropped. The legacy per-fact path and the seed stay synchronous for pre-snapshot daemons. Pinned by `status_snapshot_issues_without_blocking_and_applies_on_dispatch`, `status_snapshot_end_lands_through_the_continuation`, `status_snapshot_timeout_abandons_without_re_issuing`, and the re-pointed `a_refresh_that_already_saw_the_new_state_still_lets_its_push_toast` (its settle now runs the pump's continuation); `status_refresh_times_out_and_keeps_replies_aligned` passes unchanged. Card 01a1714357b274e59142245eb5949a09.
- **par-mux: the daemon never runs `git status` in a reply path; the attach client stops the timeout re-query storm** (`1b634dbb`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/git_status.rs`, `dispatch/client.rs`, `server/mod.rs`, `attach/render/mod.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the sidebar git badges resolved inside every `client-snapshot` reply — serial, 2 s-bounded `git status` runs per workspace on the requesting connection's thread — while the attach pump's status refresh blocks on that reply and, on timeout, re-dirtied the status for an immediate re-issue that queued behind the same slow reply: every poll cycle could freeze the TUI's input and painting in 500 ms stretches with no visible trigger, worst under host load. The badges now resolve on a daemon-wide refresher thread (the host-probe worker's lifecycle: 2 s wakes, exits within one wake of shutdown, `[client] git-status`/`git-poll-secs` read fresh each wake) that refreshes cache entries older than the poll-minus-one-second TTL and prunes what the workspaces leave behind; `client-snapshot` serves the cache alone, so its git rows cost map reads. The client's timed-out re-query waits for the next poll tick or event instead of re-issuing immediately. A workspace's badge appears within one refresher tick; until then the sidebar shows none. Pinned by `client_snapshot_serves_git_from_the_cache_alone` (cold cache: no row; one refresher tick: the row serves), the re-driven `client_snapshot_git_badge_follows_the_active_pane` / `client_snapshot_git_badge_falls_back_and_respects_the_key` (the pins now tick the refresher inline), `cache_serves_stale_entries_and_prunes_expired_ones`, and the re-pointed `status_refresh_times_out_and_keeps_replies_aligned` (a timeout no longer re-dirties).

## [0.66.1] - 2026-10-24

### Changed
- **Dependencies: sweep to latest stable across Rust, Python, the web frontend, and CI** (`6c502933`; `Cargo.lock`, `uv.lock`, `pyproject.toml`, `web-terminal-frontend/`, `.github/workflows/`): `cargo update` across the workspace and the detached fuzz lockfile (tokio 1.54.1, aws-lc-sys 0.46, icu 2.4, syn 3.0.9, …; no direct-dependency requirement changes), `uv lock --upgrade` plus floor sync in `pyproject.toml` (pillow floor `>=12.3.0` → `>=13.0.0`, pre-commit 4.7.0, ruff 0.17.2, websockets 17.3), and ncu + `bun install` in the web frontend (next 16.4.3, react 19.3.1, vitest 5.0.5, buf 1.75; frontend tests 81/81 and the static build pass). GitHub Actions pins refreshed to current releases (setup-python v7.1.0, gh-action-pypi-publish v1.14.3, claude-code-action v1.0.261, install-action v2.87.31, setup-uv v10.3.2, dtolnay/rust-toolchain stable+nightly branch heads), and `.github/dependabot.yml` now covers all four ecosystems (cargo at the root and `/fuzz`, pip, bun frontend) alongside github-actions.

### Fixed
- **par-mux: a dropped `MuxClient` closes its daemon connection** (`f04f2336`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/client.rs`, `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_end_to_end.rs`): the client's reader thread held the socket's original fd, so dropping the handle closed only the writer clone — the daemon saw EOF, and withdrew the dead client's viewer claim and size-constraint contribution, only when some later push failed to forward to the dropped channels, which in a quiet window is never (the streaming `mux_factory` suite's viewer-membership waits raced exactly that push: one 90 s expiry here, a different wait expiring there, green only when the pane's shell output happened to flow). The roster watcher's redial cycles left daemon connections lingering the same way. Drop now sets a `closed` flag the reader checks on every poll wake — the client stream gains a 200 ms recv timeout, best-effort, since named pipes (Windows) reject I/O timeouts and keep the old push-triggered close there — so a dropped client's claim is withdrawn in well under a second and the `mux_factory` suite drops from ~90 s to 15 s. Pinned by `a_dropped_mux_client_withdraws_its_viewer_claim` (red on a quiet-pane fixture pre-fix: the claim lingered the full budget) and `read_daemon_line_aborts_when_the_handle_is_dropped`.
- **par-mux: the detached-daemon tests run from inside a par-mux pane** (`4274a4e7`; `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_cli.rs`, `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_daemon.rs`): three daemon-spawn sites across the two suites did not clear the pane env contract, so a gate run from inside a par-mux pane (`PAR_MUX_ENV=1`) had every spawned daemon refused by the nested-daemon guard — in mux_cli the socket never appeared and each test burned its 90 s wait, and `the_auto_spawned_daemon_runs_in_its_own_session` hit the library's up-front refusal in `connect_or_spawn_at` (its comment describes exactly this case). The suites' other spawn sites already removed `PAR_MUX_ENV`; the three outliers now do too (mux_cli's two via `env_remove`, mux_daemon's via a process-level removal ahead of the auto-spawn).
- **par-mux: the host-probe OSC 7 test no longer races the developer's shell** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/pane.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/host_probe.rs`): `sweep_probes_child_cwd_not_osc7` spawned the user's shell as the pane child and then wrote a synthetic OSC 7 report — on a machine whose shell integration emits its own OSC 7 at startup, that report landed after the test's write and overwrote it, failing the persistence-cwd assertion locally while CI's bare shells stayed green. `ShellPaneFactory` gains `default_command` (the pane command when the client gave none; unset spawns the user's shell exactly as before) and the test pins a quiet `sleep 120` child — the probe still reads its kernel cwd, and no output ever races the write.
- **par-mux: the attach notification tests no longer depend on the terminal that launched them** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/notify.rs`, `render/tests/session.rs`, `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_attach.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the `terminal` delivery resolved the kitty host detection from the live environment per delivery, so under a kitty-launched `cargo test` the four OSC 9-shape tests observed chunked OSC 99 and failed (main was red on a kitty host, green on CI runners — the suite had never run under its OSC 99 arm), and the same env inheritance made the two `mux_attach` render-mode notification e2es fail the same way. The detection now resolves once at attach construction into `NotifyState::host_is_kitty`, the unit tests pin it, and the two e2e spawns pin `TERM_PROGRAM=iTerm.app` through the harness's `spawn_attach_with_env` — every wire shape is asserted regardless of host; a new pin covers the OSC 99 chunked form itself. `docs/MUX.md` also drops a citation of `render_mode_terminal_delivery_after_reload_is_osc9_under_a_leaked_kitty_env`, a pin test that does not exist. Pinned by `terminal_delivery_shapes_are_pinned_per_host_not_per_environment`.
