- **par-mux: adopted agent panes survive a daemon restart on the roster** (`4274a4e7`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/persist.rs`, `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_agents.rs`, `crates/par-mux/tests/assets/par-mux-scrape-agent.sh`, `crates/par-mux/tests/common/mod.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): a scrape-tier claim (adopt-pane, or an agent-start whose agent never reported identity) saves no `agent_session` — the save format copies session identity only — so `spawn_restored_pane` re-attached claims solely for identity-carrying panes and an adopted pane came back from a restart with no `agent` metadata: absent from `list-agents` and the side panel forever (until its agent's next hook report, which adopted panes never send). Restore now re-derives the label from the respawn command's first token (basename, case- and `.exe`-tolerant) under the same gate `adopt-pane` applies — the bundled scrape patterns must cover the kind — and the 1s tick rewrites the scraped state and broadcasts. Dead panes are never re-labeled. Pinned by `a_restart_relabels_an_adopted_pane_from_its_invocation` (integration, red before the fix), `an_adopted_pane_relabels_from_its_spawn_command_at_restore`, `a_shell_invocation_stays_unlabeled_at_restore`, and `agent_of_invocation_names_the_bundled_agents_by_first_token`. Card 01a16e614dbe73bda083c2dade8e8c0a.

## [0.66.0] - 2026-10-23

### Added
- **par-mux: keyboard chords scroll the tab strip without switching** (`ffae7bb`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/config.rs`, `attach/render/navigate.rs`, `attach/tabs.rs`): new management chords `prefix H` / `prefix L` shift the overflowing strip's visible run toward the hidden tabs — the tab-strip wheel's gesture on keys, 3 tabs per press toward the earlier/later windows, clamped at both edges, a strip that fits ignoring them, and nothing sent to the daemon — while the shown window stays shown. Rebindable as `[client] tab-strip-left` / `tab-strip-right` (defaults `H`/`L`, chosen over the brief's `[`/`]` because a management chord takes precedence over the fixed prefix table and `[` would have shadowed the scroll-mode chord), live on reload, listed in the settings keys tab and the keybinds help; passthrough flashes render-mode-only. Pinned by `tab_strip_chords_scroll_an_overflowing_strip` and `tab_strip_chords_parse_and_rebind`. Card 01a16c1f5fb5762aa81a6d9d9c32b954.
- **par-mux: the attach context menus take keyboard navigation** (`3ffe21f`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/modal_menu.rs`, `render/mod.rs`, `input_route.rs`): the tab/workspace/command menus were mouse-only — `MenuState` carried a hover with no selection, every key except Escape was consumed, and the footer said `action click · close esc/q`. The menus now run the picker's cursor: the selection opens on the first action row (past a name header), Up/Down — and `j`/`k` as bytes — move it wrapping past the header and footer rows, Enter (the plain CR byte, or CSI-u under kitty keyboard protocol) dispatches the selected action through the exact `menu_click` path so a rename prompt replaces the menu, and the pointer's hover writes the same index — one reversed-label highlight serves both. `q`/Escape still close, and the footer names the controls (`navigate arrows/j/k · action enter/click · close esc/q`). Pinned by `menu_arrows_move_the_selection_across_header_and_footer`, `menu_enter_dispatches_the_selected_action`, `menu_q_and_escape_still_close`, `menu_hover_and_keys_share_one_selection`, `menu_keyboard_selection_paints_the_clickable_row`, `menu_footer_names_the_keyboard_controls`, and the re-pointed `menu_hover_highlights_the_row_under_the_pointer`. Card 01a169c0f70e73fabca13ca33ce20e6a.
- **Streamer: `workspace-active` mirror mode follows the daemon's active workspace** (`e7ced20`; `src/streaming/mux_factory.rs`, `docs/STREAMING.md`): a viewer pinned to a pane never followed a desktop `switch-workspace` — the streaming mux contract had exactly two selectors (`pane-N`, or the first-pane fallback) and both pin a pane id. The new `workspace-active` session id resolves the active workspace's active pane at mirror creation (`switch-client` bare → the active session, `list-panes -t $N` → its `*`-marked pane), and re-resolves on every `%workspaces-changed` the daemon broadcasts: the mirror retargets in place — terminal reset + fresh `refresh-client` replay of the new pane, viewer-status refresh, and a screen-restore frame to the attached viewers on the SAME streaming session, no reconnect — and the input (`send-keys -H`) and size-contribution paths read the current pane per call, so they follow. `pane-N` and fixed-pane mirrors never follow. Exposed as `par_term_emu_core_rust::streaming::ACTIVE_WORKSPACE_SESSION_ID`; a phone opts in with `?session=workspace-active`. Pinned by `a_workspace_active_mirror_follows_a_desktop_workspace_switch` (two workspaces, mid-mirror switch, same-session assertion via `Arc::ptr_eq`, input-follow assertion) and `active_workspace_resolution_parses_the_daemon_replies`. Card 01a16ab7370d71ba8441b534430f2cf3.
- **par-mux: `adopt-pane` labels a hand-launched agent pane; claude hooks re-register mid-flight** (`829213a`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/agents.rs`, `command/`, `scrape.rs`, `integrations/claude.rs`, `integrations/hooks/par-mux-claude-session-hook.{sh,ps1}`): the visibility contract had a hole — a pane reaches the roster only through the TUI's agent-start or a session hook report, so a claude started by hand in a plain shell pane (or before the hooks were installed) stayed invisible, actively working or not. `adopt-pane -t %N <kind>` writes the `agent` tag `agent-start` writes, minus the launch: the scrape tier gains the pane, an immediate classification answers `%N <kind> <state> scrape` (`unknown none` until a rule matches — never a guessed idle), a kind without scrape patterns is refused, and the `%agent-state-changed` broadcast rides the reply. And the claude hook script gained the mid-flight events — UserPromptSubmit → `working`, Notification → `question` on a permission prompt else `idle`, Stop → `idle` (installer registers `prompt`/`notify`/`stop` actions under the three events, asset v2 → v3 so status surfaces flag stale installs) — so a live session re-registers between its start and end even when SessionStart was missed. Pinned by `adopt_pane_labels_the_pane_and_classifies_at_once`, `adopt_pane_refuses_a_pane_that_already_hosts_an_agent`, `adopt_pane_refuses_kinds_without_scrape_patterns`, `adopt_pane_without_a_matching_rule_reports_unknown_and_no_roster_row`, `install_registers_the_mid_flight_state_entries`, and `the_hook_assets_handle_the_mid_flight_state_actions`. Card 01a16a75cba7705a8d81aef0110dc9d7.
- **par-mux: hotkeys move the active tab left/right in the tab order** (`bc4865e`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/config.rs`, `attach/render/navigate.rs`): new management chords `prefix <` / `prefix >` (tmux's move-window spelling) swap the shown window with its neighbor via `swap-window` — the `%sessions-changed` broadcast repaints the strip, the shown window stays shown by identity, and an edge press flashes instead of wrapping. Rebindable as `[client] move-window-left` / `move-window-right`, live on reload, listed in the settings keys tab and the keybinds help, emitted by `--gen-config`; passthrough flashes render-mode-only. Pinned by `move_window_chords_swap_the_shown_window_with_its_neighbor`. Card 01a16a53774b75808b26cce08a7eb68b.
- **par-mux: the agents panel's roster ordering is a header toggle** (`7dbc37a`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/panels.rs`, `render/sidebar.rs`, `config.rs`): the agents area's header carries a clickable right-aligned `grouped`/`priority` label (herdr's `agent_sort_toggle`) — `grouped` keeps herdr's workspace groups with the agents beneath by status priority (question > blocked > done > working > idle), `priority` is the attention queue (status first, most urgent at the top, workspace beneath); a click flips the ordering in place and persists `[client] agents-sort` (default `grouped`) through the settings write path, and the settings dialog's general tab gains the matching `agents sort` choice row. Pinned by `agents_header_sort_toggle_is_clickable_and_names_the_mode`, `grouped_mode_subsorts_agents_by_status_priority`, `priority_mode_sorts_by_status_then_workspace`, and `agents_sort_round_trips_through_the_file_and_chords`. Card 01a169c5057b704b9e120a1b4083ffa7.

### Changed
- **par-mux: the tab strip's ` + ` follows the last tab, and the strip scrolls both ways** (`70ed04e`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/tabs.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the button no longer owns the strip's right edge — it reserves three columns at the row's tail and starts on the blank column right after the last visible tab (or right `…` marker), so it never runs past the line at any width (including with the side panel's lead segment). The wheel offset's `0` no longer means both "auto" and "leftmost": the offset is an `Option` (`None` = the active-centered auto run, `Some(i)` = an explicit run seed), so wheeling from the auto run moves the visible run toward earlier tabs as well as later ones, clamped to both list edges. Keyboard reachability stays on the existing prefix `n`/`p` (next/previous window) — each landed tab re-centers the run. Pinned by `wheel_scrolls_both_directions_from_the_auto_run`, `plus_clamps_to_the_line_when_tabs_fill_it`, `lead_segment_offsets_the_tabs_and_the_plus_follows`, `plus_button_lays_out_paints_and_hits`, and the re-pointed `render_mode_plus_click_*` e2es (which now discover the button's painted column from the reconstructed screen).
- **par-mux: the new-window chord opens the new-tab prompt** (`2098814`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/navigate.rs`): `prefix c` created an unnamed window outright while the tab strip's ` + ` and the tab menu's add-tab action both open the seeded new-tab name prompt — the chord now opens that same prompt (default name one past the highest window ordinal), so every new-tab surface names the window before it is created; the commit path (`new-window -t <session> -n <name>`, select + reseed) is unchanged, and with no known session the prompt still opens but its commit cancels silently. Pinned by `new_window_chord_opens_the_seeded_new_tab_prompt`.

### Fixed
- **par-mux: the move-tab settings rows carried the swap-pane label** (`685fa33`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/modal_settings.rs`): the settings keys tab showed "swap prev pane" for both `move-window-left` and `move-window-right` — a copy-paste from the swap-prev row; they now read `move tab left` / `move tab right`.
- **par-mux: a degenerate-width strip no longer paints the right overflow marker under the ` + ` button** (`01525f6`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/tabs.rs`): at widths where even a budget-1 tab block barely fits (cols 4–12 swept), the stage-3 right `…` landed inside the button's three-column tail and the paint erased it — such a marker is now suppressed (the button owns the tail; the left marker still signals hidden tabs). Pinned by `right_marker_never_lands_in_the_plus_span`.
- **kitty level 1: functional keys keep the spec's table forms — forward delete works in claude panes again** (`ed5c9e9`; `crates/par-term-emu-core/src/keyboard.rs`, `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_attach.rs`, `tests/test_keyboard_encoding.py`, `docs/API_REFERENCE.md`, `docs/FFI_GUIDE.md`): under the kitty keyboard protocol (disambiguate flag, which claude pushes via `CSI > 1 u`) the shared encoder emitted every functional key as a PUA `CSI u` code — Delete as `ESC [ 5 7 4 2 7 u` — and a real Claude Code pane ignores that spelling entirely, so through par-mux attach only Backspace edited: type `abc`, cursor left, forward Delete did nothing (reproduced live on claude 2.1.310). The spec's functional-key table (kitty's keyboard-protocol.rst, auto-generated) has no PUA codes for Insert/Delete/PageUp/PageDown/Home/End/arrows — 57426..57435 are KP_DELETE/KP_BEGIN and the media keys, and 57376..57387 are F13..F24, not F1..F12 — and disambiguate mode retains the legacy forms: `CSI 3 ~` (modified `CSI 3 ; 5 ~`), `CSI 1 ; {mods} <letter>` for the cursor group and F1/F2/F4, tilde forms for F3 (the spec dropped `CSI R` — it collides with the Cursor Position Report) and F5..F12. Only Escape/Enter/Tab/Backspace keep `CSI number u` (27/13/9/127). `encode_kitty` now emits exactly those table forms, `TermKey`'s discriminants are documented as internal/FFI ids (never wire codes), and the attach render-mode e2e pins the pane-is-authority contract both ways: under pushed kitty flags the pane receives `ESC [ 3 ~` and never `57427` (screen cleared between stages so the earlier stage's output cannot satisfy the capture). Pinned by `kitty_functional_keys_keep_table_forms`, `kitty_disambiguate_mode`, `conformance_kitty_unchanged`, the Python `test_kitty_disambiguate_mode`, and `render_mode_forward_delete_reaches_the_pane`.
- **par-mux: control-command quoting is normalized across verbs** (`325f6e9`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/{mod,panes,agents,buffers}.rs`): a quoted pane target (`send-keys -t '%0'`) parsed to a bogus name and was refused while every other `-t` verb accepted it, and a fully quoted start tail (`split-window -t %0 'sleep 60'`) rode its quotes into `sh -c` and fused into one unrunnable word — the pane died instantly and under auto-remove the reaper cascade could empty the tree. `send-keys` now cuts its `-t` with the shared shell-word grammar (quoted id or spaced name ride whole; the payload stays the raw remainder after the target word), and every raw-tail surface (`split-window`/`new-session`/`new-window` start commands, `respawn-pane`'s command — joining `agent-prompt`'s text and `show-notification`'s title, which already did this) strips one level of quotes from a tail that is exactly one quoted word, tmux's argv grouping; any other tail keeps its raw bytes. Pinned by `send_keys_parses_a_quoted_target_like_pane_info` and `quoted_start_tail_groups_like_tmux`. Card 01a1699e4dcd73fabc47fe41ac838605.
- **par-mux: the attach client bridges pane OSC 52 clipboard writes to the host** (`eee3cc9`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/mod.rs`): a pane program's copy — local or over SSH — updates the daemon pane emulator's clipboard state and stopped there, so under the attach TUI a remote claude's selection copy flashed success but a paste still held the old clipboard (par-term's native mux windows had the bridge; the attach CLI client did not). The focused pane's clipboard state now re-emits as the same OSC 52 wire form the scroll-mode yank writes whenever it changes — deduped per pane, a pane switch forwards the pane it lands on, and the host terminal's clipboard policy applies. Pinned by `pane_osc52_clipboard_bridges_to_the_host_outbox`. Card 01a16a17713a72efa962dde198156bf6.
- **par-mux: a re-attach with quiet agents no longer shows an empty agents section** (`c859437`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/session.rs`): the seed installed the side panel before the status refresh — against an empty roster — and never re-installed it after the roster committed, so the panel stayed agents-empty until an unrelated event marked the status stale (the first attach only looked fine because agent-state pushes patched it). The seed now re-installs the panel from the committed cache. Pinned by `the_seed_installs_the_agents_section_after_the_roster_lands`. Card 01a16a1b1ff274f4853b3143f23047d8.
- **par-mux: the attach input parser decodes kitty CSI-u key sequences** (`bee4244`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/input.rs`): `csi_key` handled arrows, the `~` family, and SS3 forms only, so a host's CSI-u Shift+Enter (`ESC [ 13 ; 2 u` — par-term sends it whenever the hosted TUI owns the alternate screen) hit the unknown-final arm and was consumed whole: claude panes never saw Shift+Enter, and every CSI-u-only chord died the same way. CSI-u now decodes (kitty functional codes map onto `TermKey`'s identical discriminants, scalar codepoints decode as Char keys, modifier param in the standard xterm order, absent = none) and re-encodes against the pane's tracked input state, so a legacy pane gets LF where a kitty-negotiated pane gets the CSI-u form back. Pinned by `csi_u_shift_enter_decodes_to_enter_with_shift`, `csi_u_without_modifier_param_is_the_plain_key`, `csi_u_functional_codes_decode`, `csi_u_text_codepoints_decode_as_char_keys`, and `csi_u_shift_enter_reencodes_to_lf_for_a_legacy_pane`. Card 01a169736b617377932539aec33f24f6.

## [0.65.0] - 2026-10-23

### Added
- **Mux-enabled streamer release track** (`7fc0f191`; `.github/workflows/deployment.yml`, `crates/par-term-emu-core/src/self_update.rs`, `src/bin/streaming_server/main.rs`): the release matrix builds a second `par-term-streamer-mux-<os>-<arch>` artifact per platform (streaming-bin,mux) beside the plain streamer, each with its own `.sha256` sidecar, and a `Binary::StreamerMux` engine variant selected at compile time via `cfg!(feature = "mux")` so each build self-updates from - and can never cross-install - the other artifact. macOS release binaries are now Developer ID signed and notarized by `scripts/ci-macos-sign.sh` (ad-hoc until the APPLE_* secrets exist), so the application firewall keeps its per-binary allow rule across self-update swaps.
- **Streaming pane size constraints and viewer presence** (`b0c4c9a`; cards 01a166b974, 01a166b977): mux-backed viewers receive `pane_size_status` with the effective grid and separate column/row constraint owners, plus full `viewer_presence` sets combining daemon attach clients and this session's web/mobile connections. Joins, leaves, identity changes, and unchanged-grid owner changes push without reconnecting. The daemon's backward-compatible `pane-info` tail tokens expose the source status, with `-M` excluding streaming mirror connections from attach counts. JSON, protobuf, generated TypeScript, and protocol goldens are synchronized. See `docs/STREAMING.md` and `docs/MUX.md`.
- **curl-style installers** (`48b483ac`, `caa8580`; `install.sh`, `install.ps1`, README Quick Install): checksum-verified one-liner installs of the release binaries — both `par-term-streamer` and `par-mux` by default, with streamer/mux selectors (see `install.sh --help`; PowerShell switches likewise), a version pin, and a destination override; installs into `~/.cargo/bin`, verifies the par-mux `SHA256SUMS.txt` line or the streamer `<asset>.sha256` sidecar fail-closed, and swaps atomically keeping a `.previous` backup. Streamer sidecars exist only in releases cut after the self-update feature, so older releases refuse with a pointed message. Card 01a167096e8c7375a6c3da1eba1bbbcd.
- **Standalone binary self-updates** (`eb1b0f7`): `par-term-streamer` and `par-mux` gain `--check-update` and `--self-update`, plus opt-in automatic installation at startup and hourly/daily/weekly intervals (daily by default). One core `self-update` engine selects exact GitHub release assets, requires SHA-256 sidecars, stages beside the executable, and preserves a backup before replacement. Streamer releases now include binary checksum sidecars. Streamer restarts through its configurable launchd label or re-executes, while mux uses its same-socket stop/save/restore restart flow. App-bundled mux binaries refuse replacement unless explicitly forced. The existing mux `update` command also uses the verified shared engine. Card 01a166d8fc6070189ffa83b541c4713a.

### Fixed
- **A phone viewer's Fit resize no longer sticks after it disconnects** (`fe01165`): mux mirror sessions now close when their last websocket viewer leaves, withdrawing the streamer's size contribution immediately instead of idling until reaping, so the remaining viewers' minimum re-fits the pane (desktop attach clients were stuck at phone geometry until a streamer restart). Admission is serialized against last-viewer teardown so a reconnect cannot acquire a dying mirror; ordinary PTY sessions keep their idle behavior.
- **Streaming-only clippy gates no longer trip over mux-gated helpers** (`90c72f8`, `8e7c7b3`, `216e066`): `parse_titles_output`, `handle_test_ws`, and `update_mux_status` carry feature-conditional dead-code allows, keeping `streaming`-without-`mux` builds at `-D warnings`.

## [0.64.0] - 2026-10-22

### Added
- **par-mux: git badge under the sidebar's displayed workspace row** (`6098a247`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/git_status.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the client-snapshot gains an appended `git +W <branch> <ahead> <behind>` row kind (snapshot stays v1, unknown-row-tolerant both directions), and the sidebar stamps a herdr-style ` main ↑2` badge on the displayed workspace row, resolved through the active pane's live cwd (OSC 7 first, then the kernel cwd) or the workspace's launch folder. The daemon runs the contract's exact 2s-bounded `git status -sb` with a 3s TTL cache outside the tree lock, computed on client-snapshot requests only and gated by [client] git-status (default on, settings-dialog toggle, live on reload); the client re-queries on a 10s poll while the panel is up. Pinned by the git-status header parse, chain selection, TTL cache, row round-trip, and badge-application tests.
- **par-mux: per-workspace sidebar git badges, brighter badge color, configurable poll** (`8d0f2897`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/sidebar.rs`): every workspace row matching a git row gets its own badge instead of only the displayed one; the badge paints ANSI bright-blue (Indexed 12) instead of dark blue for legibility; and [client] git-poll-secs (default 10, clamped 1-600, live on reload, sidebar-tab settings row, emitted by --gen-config) sets the cadence. Card 01a16490060f.
- **par-mux: git badge cache tracks the poll interval; 0 disables polling** (`ff81cfbf`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/git_status.rs`): the badge cache TTL becomes the configured poll minus one second, so every scheduled poll lands after expiry and runs fresh — only the double-reads of one cycle are served from the cache. git-poll-secs now accepts 0 to disable polling entirely (the client skips the due check; resolve and reload keep 0), with the floor moving to 3 seconds — never below the 2s-bounded git run — and the settings row accepting 0-600. Card 01a164bd8bbc.
- **par-mux: question agent state, status-glyph workspace markers** (`c11017f2`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/scrape.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/agents.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): question is a first-class agent state end to end — the hook validator accepts it, the scrape tier's dialog rules (29 across 13 agents: permission prompts, approval panels, trust and pick dialogs) report it, and it outranks blocked in the workspace aggregation (question > blocked > done > working > idle > none). It is blocked-class for the prompt verbs (agent-prompt refuses and ends waits on it), the sounds, the toast lifetime, and notifications, so existing notify-states configs keep working; the done flip counts it as active work. The side panel's workspace rows carry their agents' most urgent status glyph in the marker column, and the working glyph is the half-circle in the rail, markers, and state icons. MUX.md and SKILL.md state vocabulary updated. Card 01a16406340877.
- **par-mux: [daemon] split-start-dir picks the folder a split-window pane starts in** (`26826d70`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/config.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/panes.rs`): home-dir | previous-pane-dir | workspace-dir (default home-dir) chooses the start directory when no -c is given — the target pane's OSC 7-tracked live cwd or the workspace's launch folder. An explicit -c always wins; an unknown spelling, a stale pane cwd, or a workspace without a launch folder keeps the factory default. The third live daemon setting: reload-config applies a change at once and reports applied: daemon.split-start-dir. Pinned by the dispatch-to-factory handoff tests through `Harness::with_factory`.
- **Streaming: `AgentEntry` project and pane-label population lands** (`1ae46ad9`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/host_probe.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/panes.rs`, `src/streaming/roster.rs`, `src/streaming/roster_watcher.rs`, `docs/STREAMING.md`): the two empty fields are now filled. The daemon's `list-panes -F` gains `#{pane_title}` (effective title: user title when set, else the pane terminal's OSC 0/2 title; CR/LF flattened to spaces for the line-framed reply), and the roster watcher fills `pane_label` from one bounded `list-panes -F '#{pane_id} #{pane_title}'` query per fetch. The watcher parses the `host_telemetry=` token's probe-attributed project into `AgentEntry.project`, keeps both fields across state-change deltas (the pushes carry neither), answers `%agent-telemetry-changed` with a full refetch, and patches labels directly from `%pane-title-changed` pushes (a cleared user title falls back to the OSC title via a bounded per-pane query). The daemon's host-probe sweep teaches clients when a pane's project changes: it broadcasts one identity-only `%agent-telemetry-changed` push after the tree lock drops, only when the served project actually changed. Pinned by dispatch, roster, watcher, and sweep tests on both sides of the socket.
- **Streaming: `AgentEntry` carries workspace, project, and pane-label fields** (`0c40753f`; `proto/terminal.proto`, `src/streaming/roster.rs`, `docs/STREAMING.md`): the agent roster entries pushed to web clients gain optional `workspace`, `project`, and `pane_label` strings (empty = unknown, never guessed), so ParDeck's phone roster can render them. `workspace` was populated end to end in that commit: the roster parser consumes the `ws=` token from both `list-agents` rows and `%agent-state-changed` pushes, pinned by the parser test and the watcher's snapshot test asserting the field rides to the wire. `project` and `pane_label` stayed empty there — this release's population entry above fills them (the probe-attributed project and the daemon's global pane-title query).
- **par-mux: per-agent resume words land in `[daemon.agent-append]`** (`27d3acb1`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/config.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/agents.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): `agent-start` appends the configured words for its kind to the typed command line — the resume flags a shell alias would carry (`--continue` and friends), one key per supported agent (the fourteen kinds the bundled scrape patterns and the hook arms know), all defaulting to blank. `{name}` expands daemon-side to the pane cwd's basename (what an alias's `$(basename "$PWD")` sees); every word — explicit and configured — is shell-quoted on typing, so a configured value can never close the quoting and execute shell syntax. The keys are the fourth live daemon setting: `reload-config` reports one line per kind (`applied:`/`unchanged: daemon.agent-append.<kind>`), `--gen-config` emits the whole table, and a sentinel round-trip test pins every supported kind to a key. Pinned by the config tests and the live `agent_start_appends_the_configured_resume_words` / `agent_start_shell_quotes_the_configured_words` dispatch arcs. A daemon restart's resume invocation carries the same words (`07259527`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/persist.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/agent_resume.rs`): the restored agent pane rebuilds through the resume chain (hook-reported invocation, else the per-agent table) and the configured words join that argv too, deduped against the tokens the resume shape itself carries, with `{name}` expanded from the persisted cwd's basename.

### Fixed
- **par-mux: the pane-stream seed's race check now covers the export window** (`ab7b9a0`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/server/client.rs`): `observe-pane`/`control-pane` export the seed off the clients lock and re-check the daemon's output epoch under it, but the epoch baseline was sampled AFTER the export — the check only ever covered the lock-acquisition gap, so output that raced a slow export shipped a stale seed (its `%output` queued ahead of it while the seed predated it; measured on the macos runner, `seed_export_racing_output_stays_ordered`, run 39705399886). The baseline now precedes the export, so the re-export fires for anything that raced it. Pinned by `seed_export_racing_output_stays_ordered`.
- **Core: full-screen erase clears soft-wrap metadata** (`crates/par-term-emu-core/src/grid/erase.rs`): ED2/ED3 no longer leave erased rows joined in `content()` and par-mux `capture-pane`. A wrapped shell command before an image script could make the repo's own kitty graphics positioning tests report the marker one or more logical rows above its physical position. Pinned by `erase_display_clears_wrapped_line_metadata` and the kitty attach test with a command path long enough to wrap. (`fd52ed49`)
- **Core: PointerShapeChanged observers were silently dropped from kind-filtered subscriptions** (`4abf8fcc`; `crates/par-term-emu-core/src/terminal/event.rs`): `TerminalEventKind::ALL` had 26 entries for 27 variants, so `parse_event_kind`-filtered subscriptions to pointer_shape_changed never matched, and the parity table shared the omission and self-passed. The missing kind is added in declaration order, pinned in the parity table and the set_event_subscription docstring list, and guarded by a compile-exhaustive ALL length test plus a Python regression test for a kinds-filtered observer. The D9 citations now point at `docs/MUX_DECISIONS.md`, where D7+ are recorded. Cards 01a1626d9fce, 01a1626cec81.
- **par-mux: CI mux job timing flakes** (`7cecd5be`; `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_hooks.rs`): the pane-endpoint search took only the first `PS=` occurrence per line, so a styled grid row sharing one protocol line masked the real `PS=<endpoint>` later in it; the search now walks every occurrence. Separately, the ci profile's test-threads=8 oversubscribed macos-latest's 3 vCPUs and timing-flaked heavy PTY tests, so the CI mux job pins --test-threads 4 (the ubuntu/windows vCPU count); the dev machine's profile default is unchanged.
- **par-mux: bulk `--install-agent-hooks` / `--uninstall-agent-hooks` covers every library hook arm** (`2bc84c5d`; `crates/par-mux/src/bin/par_mux/main.rs`): the bulk flag ran exactly seven arms — claude, codex, grok, muse, droid, copilot, cursor — while the library's `CONFIG_HOOK_ARMS` also defines qwen, qodercli, devin, kimi, and mastracode, so clients could not install those five and docs promising the full set (MUX.md's command table) lied. The bulk table now lists all twelve, `bulk_arm_covers_config_hook_arms` fails when a library arm ships without its bulk line, and the five arms are exported from `par_mux::mux::integrations` beside the other three.
- **par-mux: the workspace status glyph sits next to the workspace name, not in the marker column** (`f5be9ea4`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/panels.rs`): the sidebar's workspace rows composed their agents' most-urgent status glyph INTO the marker column — displacing the active pointer, and glyph-replacing the second character of the row's continuation lines, so a git badge line rendered `◐ main ↑27` with the indent swapped. The glyph now composes directly after the workspace name as the `state_icon` placeholder (the same mechanism the agent rows use), the renderer paints it wherever it appears, the marker column keeps `▸`/space, and a workspace with no agent state carries no icon at all. Pinned by `compose_sidebar_puts_the_workspace_status_next_to_the_name`.
- **par-mux: the agent roster survives a daemon restart** (`3f6282cf`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/persist.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the last-known agent state was deliberately excluded from persistence ("the post-restart roster is empty by design"), so after a state-restore restart `list-agents` returned nothing until the agent's next hook transition — potentially never, for an agent that only reports on change. `PersistAgentSession` now carries `state`, `state_source`, and the blocked message beside the identity, and restore re-seeds them into the pane: hook authority travels with the state (the scrape tier cannot overwrite it), the liveness sweep still clears a claim whose agent is provably out of the pane's process tree, and `agent_seq`, the miss counters, and `session_start_source` stay process-local. The new fields are `serde(default)`, so older state files load unchanged. Pinned by the updated `agent_session_identity_round_trips` and the live `the_agent_arc_lives_and_dies_with_the_daemon` restart arc, which now asserts the roster row returns with its state.
- **Streaming: a session close survives running off the Tokio runtime** (`c78ccc2f`; `src/streaming/server/sessions.rs`): the mux bridge's drain thread closes a dead upstream's session from a plain std thread, where `close_session`'s bare `tokio::spawn` for the delayed factory teardown panicked ("there is no reactor running") — the production streamer panic at `server.rs:551` in 0.61.x. The close now schedules the 500 ms-delayed teardown through `Handle::try_current` (`spawn_blocking` on-runtime, a detached thread off), keeping the shutdown-before-teardown order in both shapes. Pinned by `dead_upstream_closes_the_mirror_off_the_runtime`, which kills the daemon under a live mirror and fails against the bare spawn (the mirror link stays parked forever).
- **par-mux: `reload-config` re-reads the applied copy's stamped config path** (`80753289`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/config.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/buffers.rs`): the dispatch resolved the config file through the global lookup, so on any machine whose real `config.toml` sets `[daemon]` keys the `reload_config_reports_restart_required_per_changed_setting` test diffed the HOST file against the test's fake applied copy and failed (5 vs 7 unchanged) — CI stayed green only because runners have no config file. `EffectiveConfig` gains `config_path`, stamped by the daemon at startup; the reload re-reads THAT path (an env change mid-run can no longer move the file under a running daemon) and hand-built copies fall back to the canonical lookup. Pinned by the fixed test plus the `reload_config_reads_the_applied_copys_stamped_path` positive control.
- **par-mux: `test_clients` compiled dead on the Windows cross-target** (`b3a9ad61`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/host_probe.rs`): the sweep helper had no cfg gate while its every caller is a `#[cfg(unix)]` PTY-bearing sweep test, so `cross-target-check` (`-D warnings`) failed with `function test_clients is never used` — checkall-ci caught it before any push. The helper is `#[cfg(unix)]` like the tests that call it.

## [0.63.0] - 2026-10-21

### Added
- **par-mux: self-update channels and installer scripts** (`2863aa0`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/update.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/bin/par_mux/update_cli.rs`, `scripts/install-par-mux.sh`, `packaging/homebrew/README.md`, `docs/MUX.md`): `par-mux update [--channel stable|preview] [--check]` resolves the channel against the GitHub releases (stable = latest release; preview = newest prerelease, falling back to stable), downloads the platform archive, verifies the new binary's `--version` before anything is replaced, swaps it in keeping `par-mux.previous` beside it, and restarts the daemon through `--restart` so its sessions ride the stop-and-restore handoff (D3.5/D10). A missing platform archive or a version mismatch refuses without touching the installation; the network rides curl exactly as the plugin installer rides git. Installer paths: the curl|sh script (checksum-verified against the release SHA256SUMS, `~/.local/bin` by default) served from this repository, and the Homebrew tap formula template plus publishing steps in `packaging/homebrew/` — pushing the tap is a release-cut action. Pinned by the update unit suite (channel resolution, platform asset selection, prerelease-aware version ordering, check-touches-nothing, and the full download→extract→verify→replace→restart flow against a real archive through the UpdateIo seam).
- **par-mux: plugin system — manifests, event hooks, trust-gated install** (`d89cbab`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/plugins.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/bin/par_mux/plugin.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): a plugin is a directory with `par-mux-plugin.toml` whose `[[hook]]` entries (event, command, timeout-ms) run shell commands when the daemon broadcasts matching notifications. `par-mux plugin install <dir-or-git-url>` resolves the source and prints the trust preview — source, identity, and every command each event would run — and installs only with `--trust` into `<state-dir>/par-mux/plugins/`, recorded in `installed.json`; `list`/`update`/`remove` complete the lifecycle. Dispatch rides the notification broadcast (a plugin sees the exact wire line, same emitter), with a curated 14-event allowlist; hook processes run detached through `sh -c`/`cmd /d /c` with `PAR_MUX_EVENT`/`PAR_MUX_EVENT_LINE` and `PAR_MUX_SOCKET` deliberately removed, killed at their timeout (default 3 s), capped at 8 concurrent daemon-wide, and dropped — never stalling the daemon. Invalid manifests skip with a warning; `reload-config` re-reads the plugins directory. Pinned by the plugins unit suite (validation, event-name derivation from the emitter, dispatch with env, skip-not-fatal, records) and the mux_cli lifecycle tests (preview/trust/install/list/update/remove, invalid manifest refusals).
- **par-mux: saved machines and the SSH thin client** (`fd198d1`, `bebcc5c`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/machine_config.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/bin/par_mux/machine.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): `[machines.<label>]` profiles in the config (target, optional default session, enabled) managed by `par-mux machine add/list/rename/enable/disable/remove` through comment-preserving surgical edits. The profiles hold no credentials — ssh owns authentication. `par-mux --machine <label> …` relays the invocation as `ssh <target> par-mux <args>` (each argument quoted for the remote login shell, `--machine` stripped, ssh's exit code returned): one-shot verbs (`--cmd`, `status`, `ls`, `observe`, `control`, `--stop`) under `BatchMode=yes` for fail-fast scripting, and `--machine attach` as the thin client — `ssh -tt` plus forced `--mode passthrough` (an explicit `--mode` wins), so remote scripting needs no TUI and remote attach rides the passthrough byte pump. Local-machine verbs (config generation, the integration installers, `--foreground`, `--restart`) refuse the relay with the reason. Completions regenerated; the docs gate's pi-resume `--session` allowlist entry retired (machine add made it a real flag).
- **par-mux: persistence resilience — snapshot history, logind shutdown inhibitor, live-handoff design (D8-D10)** (`0205adc`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/persist.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/shutdown_inhibit.rs`, `crates/par-mux/Cargo.toml`, `docs/MUX.md`, `docs/MUX_DECISIONS.md`): every pane-bearing save at least 15 minutes after the newest kept snapshot copies the state file into `snapshots/` beside it (newest-last, capped at 48 — herdr's `session-snapshots/` numbers), and the copies form persistence's third recovery tier: an unreadable state file restores through `.lastgood`, then the newest snapshot, and a raced empty state (reboot race with `.lastgood` lost) restores through the tier; a DELIBERATE empty save (kill-pane of the last pane, exit-when-empty) clears the history with `.lastgood`, keeping the fresh-start contract. On Linux, a build with the new `shutdown-inhibit` feature holds a logind delay lock and pushes a Shutdown save when login1 announces `PrepareForShutdown` (logind caps the delay, so a stuck save costs the window, never the shutdown; off by default — the zbus dependency tree against the Linux CI budget, D9). Live handoff (replacing the daemon without losing live panes) is recorded as a design note (D10): `--restart` remains the stop-and-restore handoff. Pinned by `snapshot_history_writes_throttles_and_prunes`, `an_empty_state_recovers_through_the_history_tier_only_when_raced`, `a_corrupt_state_recovers_through_lastgood_then_the_history_tier`, and the extended `a_corrupt_lastgood_is_ignored`; the Linux feature compile-checked on the Ubuntu ARM64 VM.
- **par-mux: herdr agent-integration ports — scrape patterns for kimi/qwen/qodercli/devin/gemini/amp and session-hook arms for qwen, qodercli, kimi, devin, and mastracode** (`e7a1123`, `fd7069b`, `c9c2c3d`, `880e8ba`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/patterns/*.toml`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/integrations/config_hooks.rs`, `crates/par-mux/integrations/hooks/`, `docs/MUX.md`): five new agents ride the generic `--install-agent-hooks`/`--uninstall-agent-hooks`/`--agent-hooks-status`/`--integration-status`/`--heal-agent-hooks` flags (no new CLI surface). qwen (`$QWEN_HOME/settings.json`) and qodercli (`$QODERCLI_CONFIG_DIR/settings.json`) use the claude-schema nested group; devin (`config.json` under XDG/`~/.config`, `%APPDATA%` on Windows) registers the `session` action across six lifecycle events; kimi (`$KIMI_CODE_HOME/config.toml`) gets a marked block of twelve `[[hooks]]` TOML tables (herdr's block-marker discipline — rebuilt whole on install, cut on uninstall, `^AskUserQuestion$` tool matchers preserved); mastracode (`~/.mastracode/hooks.json`) gets `{type, command, timeout(ms), description}` entries under event keys at the document root, a new root-level splice discipline alongside the existing `hooks`-object one. kimi and mastracode hooks are state-capable (`pane.report_agent` for `working`/`blocked`/`idle`), making their panes hook-authoritative; devin's reporter resolves its session id from the payload or `devin list --format json` by working directory. All five assets speak the control socket directly (herdr's shelled out to the herdr CLI). Six new scrape patterns (kimi, qwen, qodercli, devin, gemini, amp; gemini/amp pattern-only — herdr ships no installer for them); qwen's `osc_tool_progress_working` rule is dropped pending a `PaneSnapshot` progress field; mastracode has no upstream manifest; opencode remains unported (herdr uses a JS plugin, not the hook transport).
- **par-mux: macOS app-bundle wrapper for TCC permissions + launchd autostart** (`ed65f30`, `ef89d5d`; `crates/par-mux/app/{launcher.c,permissions.m,Info.plist,par-mux-daemon.plist.template,par-mux-logcap.plist.template,logcap.sh}`, `Makefile` `app` target, `docs/MUX.md`): panes under the daemon attribute macOS permissions to the daemon's responsible process, and a bare CLI has no grantable identity — TCC silently blocks screen capture, microphone, and local-network sockets with no prompt. `make app` builds and adhoc-signs `build/Par-mux.app`, an LSUIElement bundle whose C launcher preflight-fires the Screen Recording, Accessibility, Microphone, and Local Network prompts (silent once granted) and then execs `par-mux --foreground` in-process, so every pane child inherits the bundle's grants; arguments are forwarded verbatim, making the binary a faithful client proxy (`Par-mux status` is the prompt-trigger test that never disturbs a running daemon). The target deliberately never installs: grants bind to the bundle's adhoc signature, so a granted `/Applications/Par-mux.app` must never be rebuilt over or re-signed (fresh grant cycle instead). Two LaunchAgent templates wire autostart through the bundle (`KeepAlive` restarts only a crash, and no `ProcessType=Background` — launchd's background class starved the panes until it was dropped) and cap the daemon logs hourly in place at 50MB with a single `.1.gz` (launchd never rotates `StandardErrorPath`; truncate-in-place is safe under `O_APPEND`). Full Disk Access, Developer Tools, and per-target Automation have no prompt API and stay manual, documented in MUX.md.
- **par-mux attach: interactive session picker** (`679e861`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/picker.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/targets.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/{mod.rs,render/mod.rs,render/modal_settings.rs}`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/config.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`, `docs/CONFIG_REFERENCE.md`): new `[client] session-picker` key (default off). With it on, a target-less attach against several sessions shows an inline list before the attach takes the screen (both modes) — title, one row per session with id, name, and window count, the selection `>`-marked and bold, a key footer — where `↑`/`↓`/`j`/`k` move, Enter attaches, and Esc/`q`/Ctrl-C cancel to the unchanged refusal (exit 1). It disables itself unless stdin and stdout are both terminals, so scripts and agent shells keep the refusal, and reads the roster over a short-lived control connection so an open picker never registers as a viewer; with the key off the refusal is byte-identical to before. A toggle in the settings dialog's `general` tab, emitted by gen-config. Pinned by `picker_runs_only_with_the_setting_and_both_stdio_terminals`, `picker_state_moves_wraps_picks_and_aborts`, `frame_lists_aligned_rows_with_the_selection_marked`, `frame_fits_narrow_and_short_hosts_and_strips_controls`, `run_picker_redraws_in_place_and_returns_the_pick`, `host_keys_map_to_picker_keys`, `picked_target_hands_several_sessions_to_the_chooser`, `picked_target_abort_is_the_refusal`, `picked_target_stays_out_of_the_way`, `session_picker_defaults_off_resolves_and_reload_keeps_it`, `settings_row_toggles_the_session_picker` and, against the real binary, `session_picker_off_or_without_a_terminal_keeps_the_refusal_bytes`, `session_picker_needs_both_stdin_and_stdout_on_a_terminal`, `session_picker_pick_attaches_the_chosen_session_render`, `session_picker_pick_attaches_the_chosen_session_passthrough`, `session_picker_abort_prints_the_refusal_and_exits_nonzero`.
- **par-mux attach: kitty graphics rendered in panes** (`bcc0b8f`; herdr `terminal.kitty_graphics` parity; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/kitty.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/conn.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/input.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/{mod.rs,session.rs,renderer.rs,navigate.rs,modal_settings.rs}`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/config.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`, `docs/CONFIG_REFERENCE.md`): the render client draws the kitty graphics protocol images panes emit (direct `t=d` and temp-file `t=t` media) on the host terminal. Each visible placement in a pane's emulator is re-emitted as the client's own upload (`a=t`, RGBA, 4096-byte base64 chunks) and placement (`a=p` with `c=`/`r=`, cropped to the pane's content view, `C=1` so the host cursor never moves, `q=2` so the host never answers), diffed per frame so a quiet frame writes nothing; placements hide under modal overlays and popups and yield to the toast; detach deletes everything. Fallback: the launch probe asks the host with a kitty `a=q` query plus a DA1 sentinel and nothing is drawn unless the host answers `OK` (a late answer is consumed by the stdin parser and enables graphics live, never reaching a pane); a host without the protocol receives only that query. The emulators adopt the reported cell pixel size so image row spans match the daemon's pane, and replays drop the mirror's images instead of leaving them at stale rows. New `[client] kitty-graphics` key (default on, live on reload, a toggle in the settings dialog's `general` tab, emitted by gen-config). Pinned by `placement_maps_through_the_layout_and_sidebar`, `emulators_use_the_reported_cell_size_for_image_spans`, `placement_crops_to_the_pane_content_view`, `host_bytes_upload_once_place_and_diff`, `upload_chunks_carry_keys_only_on_the_first`, `session_gates_on_support_and_config_and_hides_under_modals`, `settings_row_toggles_kitty_graphics_live`, `replay_drops_the_mirrors_graphics`, `toast_overlap_suppresses_the_placement`, `invalidate_deletes_what_the_host_holds_then_redraws`, `kitty_query_reply_and_da1_sentinel_strip_in_order`, `late_kitty_reply_reports_and_never_reaches_the_pane` and, PTY-level against the real binary, `render_mode_kitty_graphics_reach_a_kitty_host_positioned`, `render_mode_kitty_graphics_stay_off_a_silent_host`, `render_mode_kitty_graphics_config_off_sends_nothing`.
- **par-mux attach: popup command windows** (`ae8cfd4`; herdr `type = "popup"` custom commands parity; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/popup.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/popup.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/config.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/modal_settings.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/settings_write.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): `[[client.popup]]` tables (`key`, `command`, optional `width`/`height` as cells or `"N%"` of the pane area, `description`) bind an after-prefix key to a command that runs in a floating, bordered box over the panes without changing the layout. The command runs on the daemon in a scratch window beside the shown one, mirrored into the box over a `control-pane` stream whose size report fits the window to the box's inner grid. While it is up the popup owns all input (keys re-encoded against its own input modes). It closes when its command exits or on prefix + its key again, which kills the scratch window; prefix `d` closes it and detaches. Geometry ports herdr's `popup_size` (outer box, half the area by default, 6x4 floor, centered). The settings dialog's `general` tab lists every popup plus a `+ add popup` row edited as `KEY [WxH] COMMAND`; an empty commit deletes one. Deviations from herdr: the close key is a par-mux addition, the command starts in the daemon's directory, and other clients see the scratch window as a tab while the popup is up. Pinned by `popup_size_parses_cells_and_percentages`, `popup_tables_deserialize_and_round_trip`, `validate_refuses_bad_keys_commands_and_duplicates`, `shadowed_popup_keys_warn`, `geometry_centers_clamps_and_floors`, `settings_spelling_round_trips`, `popup_key_opens_a_scratch_window_and_mirrors_its_stream`, `popup_captures_input_and_its_key_closes_it`, `popup_closes_when_its_command_exits_and_on_detach`, `popup_refusals_flash_and_leave_the_panes_in_charge`, `popup_tables_apply_on_reload`, `settings_popup_rows_add_edit_and_delete`, and the end-to-end `render_mode_popup_runs_a_command_in_a_floating_box_and_closes`.
- **par-mux attach: theme presets with auto light/dark switching** (`3e35b5e`; herdr `[theme]` parity; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/theme.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/config.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/input.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/session.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/modal_settings.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/tabs.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/status.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): `[client] theme` picks one of herdr's 17 built-in palettes (ported with Apache-2.0 credit) for the render chrome — pane rings and dividers, tab strip, status row, side panel band and state glyphs, toasts, overlay rings — or `default` (the unchanged built-in look, and the default). `theme = "auto"` uses `theme-dark` (default `default`) or `theme-light` (default `catppuccin-latte`) by the host's appearance, read from the launch OSC 11 probe, late OSC 11 replies (now consumed by the stdin parser instead of dropped), and mode 2031 `CSI ? 997 ; 1|2 n` reports, which the render client enables and which win over inference; a host that answers neither counts as dark. Explicit `border-active-color` / `border-color` still win. All three keys are live on reload and cycle in the settings dialog's `borders` tab; unknown names warn and use `default`. Pinned by `theme_names_resolve_to_their_presets`, `preset_panels_match_their_appearance`, `auto_picks_from_the_host_appearance`, `theme_warnings_name_unknown_keys`, `theme_presets_and_auto_follow_the_host`, `color_scheme_reports_reach_the_theme_not_the_pane`, `late_osc11_reply_is_dropped_not_forwarded`, `paint_uses_the_theme_palette`, `status_row_uses_the_theme_palette`, `borders_section_lists_the_theme_rows`, and `settings_theme_rows_write_and_apply_live`.
- **par-mux: `agent-start` confirms droid, copilot, and cursor** (`ae81d4a`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/scrape.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/patterns/{droid,copilot,cursor}.toml`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/agents.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/foreground.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): these CLIs draw no idle marker and their session hooks report identity only, so a start for them always timed out. A pattern set may now declare `idle_fallback = true` (set for these three, versions bumped to `-parmux2`), porting herdr's known-agent idle default (`DEFAULT_KNOWN_AGENT_IDLE_FALLBACK`): an unmatched screen reads as `idle` under rule `idle_fallback`, but only while the process table shows the agent (its label or the set's id) in the pane's process tree. `agent-start` accepts a fallback `idle` only 3 s after typing the command (herdr's start settle) and a deadline inside that window reports ` (still settling)`. Behavior change: `list-agents`, `%agent-state-changed`, and the attach roster now show `idle`/`done` for these three agents when nothing matches, and `done` notifications fire for them. There is no debounce yet: a single tick where the working marker is off screen reads as idle (or done), which can end an `agent-prompt -w` wait or fire a done notification early. Windows has no process table, so the fallback never fires there. Also fixes the macOS process-table argv read, which dropped each process's last argument (the script path of a `node …/agent` CLI), weakening the hook-claim liveness sweep's match. Pinned by `idle_fallback_needs_the_agents_process_and_loses_to_a_rule`, `idle_fallback_probes_the_set_id_under_an_alias_label`, `a_set_without_idle_fallback_stays_unknown_on_no_match`, `agent_start_confirms_droid_copilot_and_cursor_by_the_idle_fallback`, `agent_start_idle_fallback_needs_the_agents_process`, `agent_start_timeout_inside_the_settle_names_it`, `the_real_argv_keeps_its_last_argument`, and `agent_start_confirms_droid_by_the_idle_fallback_through_the_cli`.
- **par-mux: droid, copilot, and cursor agent integrations** (`2b54bd5`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/integrations/config_hooks.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/integrations/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/integrations/hooks/par-mux-{droid,copilot,cursor}-session-hook.{sh,ps1}`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/patterns/{droid,copilot,cursor}.toml`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/scrape.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/bin/par_mux/main.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`, `docs/CONFIG_REFERENCE.md`): three more agents join claude/codex/grok/muse, each ported from herdr (Apache-2.0) with both halves — a session hook for identity and a bundled scrape pattern set for state. `--install-agent-hooks` / `--uninstall-agent-hooks` now merge a SessionStart entry into droid's `~/.factory/settings.json` (nested group), copilot's `$COPILOT_HOME/settings.json` (flat `{type, bash|powershell, timeoutSec}`), and cursor's `$CURSOR_CONFIG_DIR/hooks.json` (bare `{command}` under `sessionStart`, adding the required `version` when missing), byte-preserving the user's file through the same splice engine; an absent agent (no config directory) prints `skipped` instead of failing the bulk install, and each reporter sends herdr's resume invocation as `session_resume_argv`; `--agent-hooks-status`, `--integration-status`, `--heal-agent-hooks`, and the settings dialog's integrations rows and update button cover them. - **par-mux: the `done` agent state (finished, unseen)** (`b348ad4`; herdr's `done`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/hooks/seen.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/hooks/report.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/scrape.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/panels.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/sidebar.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/toast.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/notify.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): an `idle` (hook report or scrape match) that follows `working`, `blocked`, or `done` while no render client views the pane is stored as `done`; `list-agents`, `client-snapshot`, and `%agent-state-changed` carry it. "Viewed" is decided daemon-side so every client agrees: a connection's `refresh-client -C` view displays the pane's window (and the window is not zoomed onto another pane). After every control command the daemon clears each viewed pane's `done` to `idle` and broadcasts the flip with the claim's own provenance. Hooks may also report `state: "done"`, and scrape rules may target it. The side panel ranks it blocked > done > working > idle with a cyan `●`, and `notify-states` accepts `done`; the daemon's seen flip (`done` → `idle`) never notifies. Pinned by `done_holds_until_a_client_views_the_pane_then_clears_to_idle`, `an_explicit_done_report_is_accepted_and_seen_when_in_view`, `a_finish_out_of_view_is_done_until_a_client_views_the_pane`, `a_scraped_finish_out_of_view_is_done_and_holds_quietly`, `an_override_rule_can_match_done`, `sidebar_shows_done_agents_and_ranks_them_over_working`, and `done_notifies_and_the_seen_flip_does_not`.
- **par-mux: `wait-output` control command** (`28ded09`; herdr `pane wait-output` parity; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/buffers.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/buffers.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/bin/par_mux/main.rs`, `crates/par-mux/skills/par-mux/SKILL.md`, `docs/MUX.md`): `wait-output -t %N (-m TEXT | -r REGEX) [-T ms] [-S start] [-E end]` blocks until a line of the pane's capture matches, so scripts stop polling `capture-pane`. It matches the same content `capture-pane -S/-E` returns (one shared function, default 100 history lines plus the screen) and replies with the first matching line. That shared plain capture now walks only the history rows its `-S/-E` range can reach instead of the whole scrollback. A timeout, pane exit, pane close, daemon shutdown, or the issuing client hanging up is an `%error` with a stable `wait-output:` prefix. Through `--cmd` a match exits 0 and a failure exits 1, and the CLI's reply deadline stretches to `-T` plus 5 s. The wait parks on the pane's update signal with no lock held, so other clients are served while it waits. Advertised as `wait-output match regex` in `list-commands`; the embedded agent skill moves to integration v3. - **par-mux: git worktree workspaces** (`8d55355`; herdr `worktree create/open/remove` parity; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/worktree.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/worktrees.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/worktrees.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/tree/worktrees.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/persist.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): `worktree-create -c <dir> -b <branch>` (the branch flag matches `git worktree add -b`; `-n` stays `new-workspace`'s name flag, decision D7) runs `git worktree add` (default checkout `<parent>/<repo>-worktrees/<branch-slug>`) and opens a workspace on the checkout grouped under the repo's workspace; `worktree-open` focuses or reopens one; `worktree-remove [-f]` removes the checkout and closes its workspace, refusing a dirty checkout or a pane with an agent claim without `-f`; `list-worktrees` lists the groups. - **par-mux: `agent-start`, `agent-prompt`, and `agent-explain` control commands** (`d37731e`; parity with herdr's `agent start`, waiting `agent prompt`, and `agent explain`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/agents.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/agents.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/scrape.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/pane.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): `agent-start -t %N [-T ms] [-x program] <kind> [args]` types an agent into a pane at its shell prompt, tags the pane's agent label so the scrape tier watches it, and replies `%N <agent> <state> <source>` only once a hook claim or scrape rule reports it ready (`idle` or `done`; a `working` startup keeps waiting, as in herdr). A blocked startup, timeout (default 30 s), pane exit, or a different detected agent is an error, and an undetected start removes its tag. droid, copilot, and cursor are confirmed by the scrape tier's process-gated idle fallback after a 3 s settle (see the idle-fallback entry). `agent-prompt -t %N [-w] [-u states] [-T ms] -- <text>` submits text (as a bracketed paste when the agent enabled it) then Enter 300 ms later. With `-w` it blocks until the agent reaches an `-u` state (default idle, done, blocked) behind an anti-stall gate: unless the agent enters `working` or `blocked` within 5 s of the prompt (a transition counter, so the daemon's `done`-to-`idle` seen flip never counts), the reply is a `stalled` error rather than a false "finished". `agent-explain -t %N` reports the stored state and its source (hook reporter and seq, or scrape rule), the pattern file (`bundled:patterns/<id>.toml` or the override path) and version, and the rule matching the live screen. The daemon's loaded scrape engine is shared so explain reads the rules the tick runs. `--cmd` stretches its reply deadline over both blocking verbs. `agent-start` saves state (the tagged label). - **par-mux attach: jump to the last notification's pane** (`9fc21c6`; herdr `open_notification_target` parity; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/config.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/notify.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/navigate.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/modal_settings.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/panels.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): prefix `j` (`[client] notify-jump`) focuses the pane behind the most recent agent notification the client delivered, across windows and workspaces, and dismisses the toast; nothing delivered flashes `no notification to jump to`. - **par-mux: `show-notification` control command and the `%notification` broadcast** (`9fc21c6`, `d277911`; herdr `notification show` parity; `crates/par-term-emu-core/src/tmux_control/`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/buffers.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/buffers.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/emit.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/notify.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/toast.rs`, `src/python_bindings/types/notification.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`, `docs/API_REFERENCE.md`): `show-notification [-b body] [--] <title>` lets a script or embedder raise a notification; the daemon broadcasts `%notification <b64 title> [body=<b64>]` and every attached render client delivers it through its own `notify-delivery` channel with the script's title (ignoring `notify-states` and the background-only rule; an `inside` toast has no pane, so a click only dismisses it). Each render client rate-limits its ad-hoc deliveries to one per second (herdr's bound) — a second inside the window drops with a status flash, and an inside delivery refuses while its toast is still up — and the notify-jump target becomes a bounded, deduplicated backlog (the last eight distinct panes, a repeat moving to the back) that the jump pops, so repeated jumps walk back through earlier notifications. The grouping rides new `client-snapshot` `worktree` rows, which are exactly `list-worktrees`' rows through one shared `WorktreeRow` grammar (an appended row kind; `snapshot 1` unchanged). - **par-mux: raw pane streams for local clients** (`e534d1c`, `991486f`; herdr `terminal session observe` / `terminal session control` parity; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/server/client.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/pane_stream.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/streams.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/bin/par_mux/main.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): `observe-pane -t %N` turns its connection into a read-only stream of one pane — a `pane %N COLSxROWS` header plus the screen-restore seed, then only that pane's `%output`, `%pane-exited`, `%pane-respawned`, and a closing `%exit` when the pane leaves the tree — and any number of clients can observe the same pane. `control-pane -t %N` is the writable form: the same narrowed output, with `send-keys` input and a connection-scoped `refresh-client -t %N -C WxH` size report on the same socket (withdrawn on disconnect). The seed and the narrowing are queued atomically against output pushes, so a consumer drops every `%output` before the reply and applies everything after. `par-mux observe -t PANE [--no-seed]` and `par-mux control -t PANE [--size WxH]` wrap them for the shell (raw bytes to stdout; `control` forwards stdin until EOF and follows the terminal's size), and `par_mux::mux::pane_stream` is the Rust embedder API. Pinned by `several_observers_each_get_only_their_panes_output`, `an_observer_is_read_only_and_its_stream_ends_with_the_pane`, `an_unknown_pane_is_refused_and_the_connection_stays_a_control_client`, `a_controller_writes_input_and_its_size_report_is_withdrawn_on_disconnect`, `the_observe_and_control_subcommands_stream_raw_bytes`, `output_before_the_seed_reply_is_dropped_and_after_it_is_delivered`, `the_filter_passes_replies_exit_and_only_the_streamed_panes_lines`, and `parses_the_pane_stream_verbs`. The pane-stream seed export holds no clients lock, so a slow export never stalls other clients' commands.
- **par-mux: shell completions** (`89eed5e`, `8f2d19c`; herdr `completion <shell>` parity; `crates/par-mux/src/bin/par_mux/main.rs`, `crates/par-mux/completions/`, `crates/par-mux/Cargo.toml`, `Makefile`, `.gitattributes`, `docs/MUX.md`): `par-mux --print-completions <bash|zsh|fish|powershell|elvish>` prints a completion script generated by `clap_complete` from the binary's own clap definition and exits without contacting a daemon. Generated copies are tracked under `crates/par-mux/completions/` (`par-mux.bash`, `_par-mux`, `par-mux.fish`, `_par-mux.ps1`, `par-mux.elv`); `make completions` regenerates them from the `mux-bin,attach` build, and `make completions-check` (now in `checkall`) fails when they drift from the CLI, so changing a flag, subcommand, or flag doc comment now needs a `make completions` in the same commit. Generation only: par-mux never edits a shell rc file. `clap_complete` is a `mux-bin`-only dependency, so library builds stay clap-free. - **par-mux attach: IME / CJK input handling** (`0c536be`; herdr IME parity, partial; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/text_input.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/input_route.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/session.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/renderer.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/tabs.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/status.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/input.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): a non-ASCII key after the prefix (an IME left in a CJK mode) used to be dropped as an unbound chord while its UTF-8 continuation bytes leaked into the pane; the whole character is now swallowed (render and passthrough, split reads included) with a `chords are ASCII keys` hint, and fullwidth forms U+FF01..U+FF5E fold to their ASCII chord. - **par-mux attach: drag to highlight and copy pane text** (`0a4d466`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/{input_route.rs,mod.rs,renderer.rs,modal.rs,paint.rs}`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/input.rs`): a left-button drag over a pane that is not capturing the mouse selects its text — the press arms a pending selection (a plain click still only focuses), the first motion promotes it to a character sweep painted REVERSED as it extends, and release copies the swept text to the host terminal's clipboard as an OSC 52 write (the same write copy mode's `y` uses) and clears. Pinned by `drag_selects_and_copies_over_a_non_tracking_pane`, `tracking_pane_keeps_reports_and_shift_drag_selects`, `drag_selection_paints_the_swept_cells_reversed`, `char_selection_text_slices_rows_in_reading_order`, and `pane_view_cell_maps_live_rows_under_scroll`. No new `[client]` setting, so the settings dialog is unchanged.

### Fixed
- **Core: the ansi scrollback export joins a soft-wrapped row to the line it continues** (`808443a`; `crates/par-term-emu-core/src/grid/export.rs`): `export_scrollback("ansi")` / `Grid::export_scrollback_styled` walked rows newest-first but joined each wrapped row to the row it reached next, which is the OLDER neighbor. A wrapped line's continuation came out in front of its head and glued onto the previous line. Logical lines are now emitted newest-first with each line's rows in grid order (head first), and every line ends with `\x1b[0m\n`, including one whose head falls outside `max_lines` (only its newest rows are exported) and a newest row whose continuation is still on screen. Output change for ansi consumers, including `capture-pane -e -S/-E` history in par-mux: a wrapped line now reads `head+tail` instead of `tail\nhead` glued to the older line. The plain and html formats never join rows (one line per grid row) and are unchanged. Pinned by the regenerated `test_styled_exports_golden_bytes`, `export_scrollback_joins_a_wrapped_line_with_the_row_it_continues`, and `export_scrollback_ansi_terminates_a_row_whose_continuation_is_on_screen`.
- **par-mux attach: an agent's toast is no longer lost when its state change lands during the seed or behind a status refresh** (`037fd15`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/{mod.rs,notify.rs,status_patch.rs,navigate.rs,session.rs}`): with `notify-delivery = "inside"`, a background agent could show `blocked` in the side panel while its toast and bell never came. Two seams lost the push. (1) The window seed drained the push queue twice (after the registration and after the size report) and kept only `%pane-exited` and the shown window's `%layout-change`, so an `%agent-state-changed` (or `%notification`, `%sessions-changed`, …) that arrived while the client was still seeding was discarded. Those events now wait for the pump's first drain, in order; replay-covered output and the pre-size-report layout are still dropped. (2) A pushed state change was judged against the client's agent roster, which the status refresh also writes. The daemon applies a hook report before it broadcasts it, and the client reads command replies and pushes on separate channels, so a refresh between the two committed the new state while its push was still queued, and the push read as a repeat (`blocked -> blocked`). Transitions are now judged against a per-pane baseline that only pushes move, seeded once from the first committed roster minus any pane whose push is already queued. Pinned by `a_push_drained_by_the_seed_still_toasts`, `a_push_queued_across_the_seed_refresh_still_toasts`, `a_refresh_that_already_saw_the_new_state_still_lets_its_push_toast` and `a_push_before_the_first_roster_does_not_seed_a_partial_baseline`.
- **par-mux attach: the server and session pickers say what the options are** (`a1c17ca`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/discovery.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/bin/par_mux/main.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/targets.rs`): the multi-server refusal listed bare rows (`$10 sessions=1 0.62.0+…`) with no hint of what the names were, where the sockets live, or which server default resolution picks — a leaked test daemon was indistinguishable from a real choice — and its `attach <name|path>` suggestion ignored `-t`, while the multi-session refusal's `-t <session>` suggestion ignored the server dimension, so following one refusal landed on the other. Both refusals (and `--list-servers`) now print one row per server with the label, socket path, the daemon's pid and age from its registry entry, session count, build stamp, a `[default]` marker, and a `(pid-named)` marker on generated `$NN` names; the multi-server hint names the composed `attach <name|path>` + `-t <session>` form and the multi-session hint names the server-qualified spelling. Pinned by `describe_server_names_the_pickers_dimensions`, `up_duration_renders_minutes_hours_and_days`, and the extended `multi_session_error_lists_every_session_and_the_hint`.
- **par-mux: `worktree-remove` no longer deletes a dirty checkout when git's filesystem monitor lags** (`ac671cc`, `2493d3b`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/worktree.rs`): the worktree plumbing ran git with the user's own `core.fsmonitor` setting. A monitor that has not yet received a write's event answers "nothing changed" (macOS FSEvents defers delivery under load), so `git status` reported a modified checkout clean. Both of `worktree-remove`'s guards (the `is_dirty` pre-check and the child status that `git worktree remove` runs without `--force`) then passed, and the checkout's untracked files were deleted. The same lag made `mux::worktree::tests::add_list_dirty_and_remove_round_trip` flake under load. Every worktree git run now passes `-c core.fsmonitor=false`, which reaches the child status too, and the dirty check adds `--no-optional-locks` so it never rewrites the user's index. Pinned by `is_dirty_ignores_a_lagging_fsmonitor` and `remove_refuses_a_dirty_checkout_despite_a_lagging_fsmonitor` (a repo-local fsmonitor hook that always reports no changes, with the untracked cache on; both fail without the fix). Every git-invoking site in par-mux is now audited (a per-site bypass-or-why-safe marker), and `build.rs`'s `git status --porcelain` — the build stamp's `-dirty` decision — passes the same bypass (`-c core.fsmonitor=false --no-optional-locks`), so a lagging monitor can no longer stamp a dirty build clean; the staged-edit-reads-clean anomaly from the load runs was the host probe's TEST fixture (its `git add --all` inheriting the machine's `core.fsmonitor=true`), bypassed now. Pinned by `staged_edit_reads_dirty_despite_a_lagging_fsmonitor`.
- **par-mux: an ended raw pane stream releases its daemon threads on Windows** (`bf6ef3c`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/server/client.rs`): when an `observe-pane`/`control-pane` stream ended with `%exit`, the daemon only raised the connection's teardown flag. Named pipes have no I/O timeout, so on Windows the connection's reader stayed parked in `ReadFile` (and the pipe stayed open) until the client hung up. A client that never closed its end pinned both threads. The `%exit` line is now queued as the connection's final item. Once the writer has written it, the writer calls the registry entry's `ConnectionAbort::cancel_blocked_io` (the eviction path's `CancelIoEx`). The cancel runs after the write so it cannot abort `%exit` itself. If a client is too wedged to drain `%exit`, the pane watcher cancels after a 5 s grace instead. Unix behavior is unchanged (the cancel is a no-op there, and the flag's poll already woke the threads). Pinned by `a_pane_streams_exit_closes_the_connection_while_the_client_stays_silent` (a silent client still reads `%exit` and then EOF, and leaves the registry; built to fail on Windows without the cancel) and `the_final_marker_flags_only_the_last_item_and_leaves_its_bytes_intact`.
- **`export_scrollback(..., max_lines=n)` returns the NEWEST `n` scrollback lines (Python API + Rust)** (`b59b9fb`; `crates/par-term-emu-core/src/terminal/semantic_snapshot.rs`, `crates/par-term-emu-core/src/grid/export.rs`, `src/python_bindings/terminal/scrollback_api.rs`): the limit selected the OLDEST `n` rows while the export walks newest-first, so a tail limit returned the far end of history. All three formats (`plain`, `html`, `ansi`) now keep the newest rows, in the unchanged newest-first order, so `Some(n)` is always a prefix of the unlimited export (its first `n` rows). Output changes for callers who passed `max_lines` and relied on the old oldest-rows selection. par-mux's `capture-pane -S`/`wait-output -S` bounded history walk now uses the fixed API instead of its own index math (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/buffers.rs`); its output is unchanged.
- **Grid snapshot no longer panics after a narrowing resize** (`crates/par-term-emu-core/src/grid/mod.rs`): scrollback rows keep the width they were pushed at, and `capture_snapshot` computed `cols - row.len()` per row, which underflowed once the grid had narrowed. Debug builds panicked on every snapshot of such a pane (par-mux's render-client replay and state save among them). Wider rows now truncate to the fixed snapshot width. Pinned by `snapshot_after_narrowing_truncates_wider_scrollback_rows`.
- **par-mux: `--cmd` client memory stays bounded during a long `wait-output`** (`56a6fb8`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/client.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/bin/par_mux/main.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the `--cmd` client never read pushed notifications, but its reader queued every `%output` line the daemon broadcast into an unbounded channel, so a wait parked on busy panes grew the CLI by wait duration times output rate. New `MuxClient::discard_notifications()` makes the reader parse and drop pushed lines while still reading the socket in full (so the daemon never sees the client back up); `--cmd` turns it on before sending, and replies, including `%error`, are unaffected. Pinned by `discarded_notifications_are_never_queued` and `wait_output_under_continuous_output_exits_zero`.
- **par-mux: a start command's own flags no longer leak into `split-window`/`new-window`/`new-session`** (`f567569`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/panes.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/windows.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/sessions.rs`): the create commands read their flags across the whole line, past `--` and into the start command, so `split-window -t %0 -- sh -c 'echo hi'` took sh's `-c` as the start directory, `-h`/`-b` inside the command changed the split, and `new-session -- ssh -t host` used `host` as the workspace target. Flag reading now stops at `--` or the command's first word (getopt semantics, as `respawn-pane` already had). Also `set-buffer` enters its `-H` hex form only when `-H` is the first word, so a copied payload containing ` -H ` (`set-buffer 'grep -H x'`) is stored instead of failing as bad hex. Pinned by `split_window_stops_reading_flags_at_the_start_command`, `new_session_and_new_window_stop_reading_flags_at_the_start_command`, `respawn_pane_start_command_keeps_its_own_flags`, and `set_buffer_hex_mode_needs_a_leading_flag`.
- **par-mux: four more parser misreads** (`5dc4829`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/sessions.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/worktrees.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/windows.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/panes.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/modal_prompt.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`). `set-environment` scanned the whole line for `-u`/`-t`, so `set-environment -t $0 NAME -u` unset `NAME` instead of setting it to `-u`. Its flags now lead (getopt semantics), and an unknown flag such as `-g` is refused instead of becoming a NAME. `worktree-create` refused `-s 'a -n b'` as the removed `-n` branch flag. The refusal now reads shell words and skips flag values. `rename-window -t @0 -- x` named the window `-- x`. One leading `--` is now dropped, and a quoted target is skipped whole (`-t 'my win' new` used to name it `win' new`). The attach rename prompt sends a guarding `--` only when the typed name's first word is `--`, so older daemons keep receiving the plain form. Finally, `split-window -l 30` ran `30` as the start command. `-l`, `-e`, and `-F` are now refused like `new-window -F`. Pinned by `set_environment_value_shaped_like_a_flag_is_the_value`, `worktree_create_n_refusal_ignores_n_inside_values`, `rename_window_strips_a_leading_double_dash`, and `split_window_refuses_size_env_and_format_flags`.
- **par-mux: the `done` seen rule follows the pane a client shows, not its last size report** (`2e261a6`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/hooks/seen.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/tree/layout_ops.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/client.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): a late `refresh-client -C` against a window the client had just left counted that window as viewed, so a finish there was marked seen (or a `done` cleared) though nobody was looking, and a passthrough attach, which reports its whole window while showing one pane, cleared the `done` of every sibling pane. The daemon now keeps a per-client focused window, separate from the sizing view. The client's own `select-window`, `switch-client -t`, or `select-workspace` (or a shared-selection follow) places it, and a size report naming another window no longer moves a focus the client's own selection placed. Only the zoomed pane, else the active pane, of that window is viewed. A render client viewing a split window now clears only its focused pane's `done`, and a `control-pane` viewer clears its window's active pane. Passthrough attach now selects its shown pane before every size report (the explicit `-t %N` attach and the reconnect included, not only in-session switches), so the active pane the daemon views is the one on screen.
