Pinned by `resync_selects_the_shown_pane_before_the_size_report`, `a_late_size_report_for_a_left_window_does_not_mark_it_seen`, `a_late_size_report_after_a_session_switch_does_not_mark_it_seen`, `a_single_pane_view_does_not_mark_its_siblings_seen`, `a_zoomed_window_views_only_the_zoomed_pane`, and `a_report_after_the_focused_window_dies_moves_the_focus`.
- **par-mux: a flag inside another flag's quoted value no longer redirects the command** (`41133c5`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/mod.rs`): `Args::flag`/`Args::quoted_flag` scanned whitespace tokens, so a flag word inside a quoted value matched as the flag. `new-window -n 'a -t b' -t s1` targeted session `b'`, and `worktree-create -s 'a -c b' -c /r -b x` took repo dir `b'`. The quoted re-read had the same flaw: `new-window -n '-t' -t 'my s'` targeted `-t`. Both helpers now read shell words, skip quoted values whole, and match a flag only against an unquoted word. This covers every target (`-t`/`-s`), name, format, start-dir, and numeric (`-x`/`-y`/`-C`/`-p`/`-S`/`-E`/`-I`) flag read through them. Pinned by `flag_scan_skips_flags_inside_quoted_values`, `target_helpers_skip_flags_inside_quoted_values`, `numeric_flags_skip_flags_inside_quoted_values`, and `select_pane_title_is_not_read_from_a_quoted_target`.
- **par-mux: a valueless flag inside a quoted value no longer fires** (`d5b49be`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/mod.rs`): `Args::has_flag` matched whitespace tokens, so a boolean flag in the middle of another flag's quoted value turned on. Destructive cases: `worktree-remove -t 'x -f y'` forced the removal and `kill-workspace -t 'a -g b'` killed the whole group. `split-window -c '/p -h x'` and `join-pane -s 'a -h b'` split side by side, `resize-pane -t 'a -Z b' -L` toggled zoom, `capture-pane -t 'x -e y'` added escapes, and `list-windows -F '#{window_name} -a …' -t $1` was refused as `-a` with `-t`. `has_flag` now uses the same shell-word scan as `Args::flag`, so a flag inside a quoted value, or quoted as the value (`-t '-f'`), is not the flag. This reverses the refusal the entry above added: `select-pane -t 'a -T b'` now selects the pane and leaves its title alone. Pinned by `boolean_flags_skip_flags_inside_quoted_values` and `mode_flags_skip_flags_inside_quoted_values`.
- **par-mux: parser audit round 3 — flag-shaped values, new-window `-e`, join-pane leftovers** (`d4c367b`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/sessions.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/windows.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/panes.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/buffers.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/worktrees.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`). `set-option`, `rename-session`, `rename-workspace`, and `set-workspace-respawn` dropped every `-t` word and the word after it wherever it appeared, so a key, name, or value that was literally `-t` (bare or quoted) vanished — `set-workspace-respawn -t +1 '-t'` silently became the read-only query. They now read flags only until the first other word or `--`, so a second unquoted `-t` in flag position is a loud duplicate. `set-option` documents the `-t` it accepts and ignores. `new-window -e` was listed as a value flag but never read, so `new-window -e A=1` dropped the variable: it is now refused with a pointer to `set-environment`/`new-session -e` (a pane's environment is its session's, which `respawn-pane` and restore re-seed from). `join-pane` ignored any word it did not read, so tmux's `-l 30` joined at 50%: unknown flags and stray words are refused, the same shape `split-window` uses for `-l`. `new-session -e` and the leading-flag reader (`set-environment`, `respawn-pane`, `wait-output`, the agent verbs, `set-buffer`) matched a quoted `'-e'`/`'-u'` value as the flag; they now compare raw words. An unquoted flag word given as another flag's value is read once: commands declare their value-taking flags, so `worktree-remove -t -f` targets a workspace named `-f` and leaves force off. Pinned by `value_shaped_t_is_a_word_not_the_target_flag`, `new_window_refuses_e_with_a_pointer`, `join_pane_refuses_leftover_arguments`, `quoted_e_value_is_not_an_env_flag`, `flag_spelled_value_is_read_once`, and `leading_flags_read_a_quoted_flag_word_as_the_tail`.
- **Windows: the `mux_attach` test target compiles again and par-mux's Windows-only unused warnings are gone** (`37da2f8`; `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_attach.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/hooks/tests.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/integrations/claude.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/integrations/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/bin/par_mux/main.rs`): a misplaced `#[cfg(unix)]` had left `place_pending` compiled out while its callers were not. Copy mode `e`'s Windows refusal is pinned by `edit_scrollback_refuses_without_a_posix_shell`.
- **par-mux tests: the PTY/attach suites no longer flake under host load** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/test_wait.rs`, `crates/par-mux/tests/common/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_attach.rs`, `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_streams.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/server/`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/host_probe.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/agents_tests.rs`, `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_cli.rs`, `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_restart.rs`, `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_agents.rs`, `src/streaming/mux_factory.rs`, `.config/nextest.toml`; test-only). Several causes, each fixed. The nextest ci profile's concurrency setting never took effect. It used `threads`/`override-threads`, which are not nextest keys, so nextest ignored them and ran one test per CPU. The profile now sets `test-threads = 8` (half of a 16-CPU dev machine; CI's ubuntu/windows-latest runners have 4 vCPUs and macos-latest has 3). Positive waits (the test waits until something happens) used 5-15 s budgets tuned on an idle machine. They now use one shared 90 s ceiling (`test_wait::CEILING`) with fast polling, so a healthy run returns as soon as its condition holds and only a wedged daemon reaches the ceiling. The nextest ci profile now kills a test at 30 s x8, above that ceiling. A click sent after a fixed 600 ms settle could arrive before its target was drawn, so it now waits for the target cell to be painted. Checks that used a quiet window to mean "the repaint landed" now wait for the repaint itself. The render client skips a cell whose glyph has not changed, so `reload config` could arrive as `reload`, a cursor jump, then `config`. Text matching therefore also checks the reconstructed screen. The lib test process inherits a soft fd limit of 256 when launched from launchd or the Dock, and at that limit 104 tests failed with EMFILE. It now raises the limit before any test runs. The host-probe pipe-buffer test dropped a 2 s wall-clock bound that its value assertion already covers. A daemon that is wedged still fails a wait at its ceiling. Pinned by `a_wedged_daemon_still_fails_a_positive_wait_at_its_ceiling`, `output_matcher_reads_a_phrase_split_by_a_skipped_cell`, and `until_fails_at_its_ceiling_with_the_last_state`.
- **par-mux attach: a workspace killed while the render client is still seeding lands instead of exiting 1** (`8c02a79`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/session.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_attach.rs`): the gone-view landing walk ran only in the pump, after the first frame. A target torn down between the seed's resolution and its first frame failed the attach: the daemon refused the size report for the dead pane, and the seed treated the missing `%layout-change` as a pre-layout daemon ("no %layout-change for @N"), or the status refresh found no owning session ("the target's session is gone"). The seed now re-runs the same cascade landing against the daemon's lists, and ends cleanly when nothing is left. A target that does not exist when the seed first asks is still an attach error. Separately, a `pane-info` reply past the 10 s bound no longer falls through to the window probe, which `list-panes -t %N` passes, so a live pane was adopted as its own window id and the seed waited for a `%layout-change %N` that never comes ("no %layout-change for %0" under host load). Pinned by `seed_lands_on_the_survivor_when_the_target_dies_before_its_size_report`, `seed_lands_on_the_survivor_when_the_target_session_dies_mid_seed`, `seed_with_nothing_left_after_the_target_dies_ends_cleanly`, `seed_of_a_target_missing_at_resolution_is_still_an_error`, and `a_timed_out_pane_info_never_adopts_the_pane_as_a_window`. `render_mode_kill_of_the_shown_workspace_lands_on_the_surviving_one` now waits for the client to paint the shown workspace before killing it, because the mouse-mode enable it waited on is written before the seed resolves the target.
- **par-mux: pipe-path `--socket` and bare socket names work on Windows** (`f4b7e1c`, `41689d6`, `34af25d`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/ipc.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/bin/par_mux/main.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/persist.rs`): three defects in the named-pipe path, each measured on the Win11 VM. `to_ns_name` prepends the pipe prefix itself, so a `--socket` spelled with the documented `\\.\pipe\` prefix double-prefixed the pipe and the bind's marker write failed with os error 2 — the daemon never came up; every ipc entry (bind, prepare, both connects) now strips the prefix, so the pipe-path spelling names the same pipe as the bare stem. A daemon started with the prefixed spelling exported `PAR_MUX_SOCKET` still carrying it, but the `.ps1` hook transport passes the env value straight to `NamedPipeClientStream` (which adds its own prefix), so hook connects missed the listener — `run_daemon` strips it once, upstream of bind, the state stem, and the env export. And the bind path wrote the pipe's liveness marker at the literal socket path, so a bare name dropped a regular file named exactly like the socket into the working directory — with `--socket vmbare2 --state-dir <cwd>/vmbare2` it landed on the state dir's own path and every `create_dir_all` under it failed with os error 183, losing all state saves and the registry registration until restart. The marker now lives at the socket path when it names a directory-resident file, else under the per-user temp dir as `par-mux-<name>.sock-marker`, and the stale-marker reclaim in `prepare_socket_path` follows the same resolution. Pinned by `a_bare_socket_name_writes_its_marker_in_temp_not_the_cwd`, `marker_paths_follow_the_socket_path_or_temp`, and `a_shutdown_save_respects_a_state_dir_deleted_mid_run` (all platforms).
- **par-mux: `send-keys` writes to the PTY with the tree lock released** (`08d1c2e`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/panes.rs`, `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_send_keys.rs`): `cmd_send_keys` held the tree mutex across the PTY `write_all`, which blocks once the kernel input queue is full — a client typing into a pane whose child stopped reading stalled every other control command on the daemon. It now snapshots the terminal (for mode-sensitive key encoding) and the input handle under the lock, then encodes and writes with the lock released (the paste-buffer shape), so a stalled write parks only its own connection. Pinned by `send_keys_does_not_stall_other_commands_behind_a_full_pty`.
- **par-mux: post-batch polish — nine small items from the follow-up card** (`47c10ea`; `crates/par-mux/src/bin/par_mux/main.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/`, `crates/par-mux/completions/`): the scrollback editor and the popup commands run their start command inline through the parser's start-command cut, so the launcher-script workaround (and the editor's launcher-deleted-on-error failure mode) is gone; `worktree-remove`'s dirty check is fail-closed — a check that ERRORED now refuses like a dirty tree instead of unwrapping to clean; `sound-player` accepts a quoted program path; the settings dialog's theme row shows `auto` resolved (`auto (default)`) while cycling still writes the raw spelling; the passthrough help dump no longer lists the render-only scroll viewport and its editor keys; `par-mux completion <SHELL>` joins `--print-completions` (herdr's spelling), with the completions regenerated and the generated fish script gaining a `fish -n` syntax gate; the attach handshake comment no longer claims the daemon rejects a target-less size report; and the parser docs (`parse_command`, MUX.md's grammar paragraph) say per-command flag scan. Pinned by a new parse test and the completions check.

### Changed
- **par-mux: `worktree-create`'s start point flag is `--base`** (`554f4f4`; was `-B`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/worktrees.rs`, decision D7 amended): git's `worktree add -B` means create-or-reset, so a short `-B` here collided with that muscle memory and would be read as reset. The flag never shipped in a release; `-B` is refused with a pointer to `--base` (same shape as `-n` → `-b`), and the refusal reads shell words, so a `-B` inside a quoted value or as a value still parses. The agent skill moves to integration v6.
- **par-mux attach: a wheel-scrolled view survives pane output** (`be2cc9f`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/renderer.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the wheel over a pane that does not own mouse mode scrolled the client's view into history, but ANY next output byte from the pane snapped it straight back to the bottom — over a pane that keeps writing (omp's streaming chat, a log tail) the wheel looked dead. A scrolled-off-live view now arms the scroll hold the prefix-`[` viewport uses, and the hold pins the view to its content: new lines push into history under the pinned view (the offset grows by the pushed lines, tmux copy-mode behavior) instead of resetting it. Scrolling back down to the bottom releases the hold, and typing still snaps the view to live. Pinned by `wheel_scrolled_view_holds_against_pane_output` and the end-to-end `render_mode_wheel_forwards_to_a_pane_that_owns_mouse` / `render_mode_scroll_gesture_sends_nothing_but_the_reports`.
- **par-mux: `[client] notify-states` defaults to `["blocked", "done"]`** (was `["blocked", "idle"]`): a background agent finishing now arrives as `done`, so the default keeps announcing finishes. A config that spells `notify-states = ["blocked", "idle"]` explicitly (the settings dialog writes the list) no longer hears background finishes; add `"done"` (or toggle it in the settings dialog's `states` tab).

## [0.62.0] - 2026-10-18

### Added
- **par-mux attach: copy mode** (TUI audit R1, `96372b2`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/modal.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/paint.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/renderer.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the scroll viewport gains tmux's missing half. `v` anchors a reversed line selection the scrolling viewport extends, `y` copies the selected lines as an OSC 52 clipboard write (the host's clipboard policy applies), and `/` searches the pane's scrollback + live text case-insensitively with `n`/`N` walking the matches in view. Pinned by `copy_mode_selects_copies_and_searches`.
- **par-mux attach: tab strip drag-reorder and wheel scroll** (TUI audit R6; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/tabs.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/input_route.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): a press-and-move on the tabs reorders windows through the wire's `move-window` (a release on the origin tab stays a plain click), and an overflowing strip wheels its visible run toward the hidden tabs. Pinned by `tab_drag_reorders_via_move_window` and `tab_wheel_scrolls_an_overflowing_strip`.
- **par-mux attach: the command palette (`C-b :`)** (TUI audit R2; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/config.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/modal_picker.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/panels.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): every management action — plus settings, keybinds, reload config, and detach — in one filterable picker list. `/` filters as you type, Enter runs, esc dismisses; render mode only (passthrough flashes why). Rebinds as `[client] palette`; listed in the help panel and the settings keys tab. Pinned by `palette_chord_filters_and_runs_actions`.
- **par-mux attach: break-pane and join-pane chords** (TUI audit R3; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/config.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/actions.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/navigate.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/modal_picker.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the wire's `break-pane`/`join-pane` were keyboard-only. `C-b !` breaks the focused pane out into its session's new window and lands on it (both attach modes); `C-b J` opens the session/window picker in join mode — the picked window receives the focused pane, and the view lands there (render mode; passthrough flashes that it needs the picker). Both rebind as `[client] break-pane` / `join-pane`, list in the help panel and the settings dialog's keys tab. Pinned by `break_pane_chord_breaks_out_and_lands` and `join_pane_chord_picks_a_window_and_joins`.
- **par-mux attach: a shown pane's death flashes the respawn affordance** (TUI audit F4; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/status_patch.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): under the default `remain-on-exit = false` the held exit cue and `C-b r` respawn never get to show — the reaper strips the pane moments after the `%pane-exited` notice. The death now announces itself: a status-row flash names the pane, the code, and the opt-in, with the live chord spellings. Pinned by `a_shown_pane_exit_flashes_the_respawn_opt_in`.
- **par-mux attach: the settings integrations tab's update button** (Paul, 2026-10-18; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/integrations/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/modal_settings.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): Enter (or `u`) on the integrations tab reinstalls every integration whose installed asset is not the bundled copy (`v1 → v2 outdated`, or `asset missing` under a surviving registration) — never-installed rows are left alone, the outcome flashes on the status row and the rows re-grade in place. The selection core (`update_outdated_with`) is pure and unit-tested; the real dispatchers reuse the shipped installers (the same merge discipline as a fresh install). Pinned by `update_outdated_reinstalls_only_stale_installed_integrations` and `integrations_footer_offers_the_update_outdated_button`.
- **streaming: pointer shape (OSC 22) reaches web clients** (`c241887`; `proto/terminal.proto`, `src/streaming/protocol.rs`, `src/streaming/proto.rs`, `src/streaming/proto_golden_tests.rs`, `src/streaming/server/mod.rs`, `src/streaming/server/send.rs`, `src/streaming/session.rs`, `src/python_bindings/streaming.rs`, `web-terminal-frontend/lib/pointer-shape.ts`, `web-terminal-frontend/lib/terminal-connection.ts`, `web-terminal-frontend/components/Terminal.tsx`, `docs/STREAMING.md`, `docs/API_REFERENCE.md`): the one `TerminalEvent` the streaming mapping still dropped is now on the wire. A new `pointer_shape_changed` message (oneof field 40, `EventType::PointerShape` = 27) carries the requested shape or `None` for a reset; `Connected` grows `pointer_shape` so a connecting client applies the session's current shape without waiting for a change; `send_pointer_shape_changed` joins the Python broadcast surface. The web frontend maps the name (CSS names pass through, the X11 synonyms `sb_h_double_arrow`/`sb_v_double_arrow` map to `ew-resize`/`ns-resize`, `arrow`/`ibeam` included; empty/absent resets to `text`; unknown names keep the current cursor) onto the terminal host's `style.cursor`. Pinned by `test_osc22_event_maps_to_pointer_shape_changed` + `test_osc22_reset_maps_to_none` (the OSC 22 → wire mapping), `test_connect_message_carries_initial_pointer_shape` + `test_connect_message_default_terminal_has_no_pointer_shape` (the `Connected` initial state), the regenerated wire golden (`proto_golden_wire_conversions_are_unchanged`), the parametrized dict-shape rows in `tests/test_streaming_dict_api.py` (`test_server_message_dict_shape`), and `lib/__tests__/pointer-shape.test.ts`.

### Fixed
- **par-mux attach: the initial `attach -t` shades the displayed session's workspace** (TUI audit F1; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/status.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/sidebar.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/pump.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/status_row.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the landing showed the target session's windows while the status segment, side panel highlight, and pass-through bracket followed the daemon-wide active-workspace pointer — the chrome self-contradicted whenever the two differed. The chrome now derives from the displayed session's own workspace, falling back to the daemon's active one when the session carries none. Pinned by `compose_shades_the_displayed_sessions_workspace`.
- **par-mux: a state dir deleted while the daemon ran is no longer resurrected by the final save** (TUI audit F3; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/persist.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the `--stop` reply lands before the process exits and the save-on-exit re-created a dir deleted in that window — a script's `--stop; rm -rf state; start` restored the old tree. A shutdown-family save whose target dir is gone now skips instead of re-creating it. Pinned by `a_shutdown_save_does_not_resurrect_a_deleted_state_dir`.
- **par-mux: a blocked agent's reason now renders** (TUI audit F5; `crates/par-term-emu-core/src/tmux_control/parser.rs`, `crates/par-term-emu-core/src/tmux_control/notification.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/emit.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/hooks/report.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/status.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/notify.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/toast.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/panels.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the hook-reported message rode `list-agents`' `reason=` extra end to end and rendered nowhere. The state push now carries it too — `%agent-state-changed` gains a trailing `msg=<base64>` token (unknown-key-tolerant, so older clients ignore it), the `inside` toast gains the reason as a dim third line, the side panel's `agent-rows` gains a `message` token plus a `verbose` preset, and the Python `TmuxNotification` gains `message`. Pinned by `agent_state_changed_message_round_trips`, `toast_renders_the_blocked_reason_line`, `agent_reason_decodes_into_the_entry_message` and the `msg=` parser tests.
### Changed
- **par-mux: session names are unique** (Paul, 2026-10-18; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/pane.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/tree/lifecycle.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/sessions.rs`, tests, `docs/MUX.md`): `new-session -s`, `new-workspace` (its first session is named after the workspace), and the tree API refuse a name another live session holds (`duplicate session: <name>`), and `rename-session` refuses one too while a session keeping its own name still succeeds — two same-named `$N` sessions made `attach -t <name>` ambiguous (live, 2026-10-18). A nameless `new-session` now takes tmux's lowest unused numeric name instead of a literal `0`. Duplicates from a legacy persisted tree still restore; targeting such a name stays the ambiguous-listing error. Pinned by `duplicate_session_names_are_refused_at_create_and_rename` and `duplicate_session_names_refuse_over_the_wire`.
- **par-mux CLI: the daemon name "server" is reserved** (Paul, 2026-10-18; `crates/par-mux/src/bin/par_mux/main.rs`, `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_cli.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): `par-mux server stop` reads as "stop the server" but clap takes `server` as the daemon NAME and silently narrows to a nonexistent daemon on `par-mux-server.sock` (live, 2026-10-18). Every name position — bare, verb-first, verb-last — now refuses it with the default-daemon spelling in the explanation and `--socket` as the deliberate escape; attach-by-name is untouched. Pinned by `the_reserved_name_server_is_refused_in_every_name_position`.
- **par-mux: the exit cascade default is now close, not hold** (Paul, 2026-10-18; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/server/reap.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/persist.rs`, `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_daemon.rs`, `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_attach.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): a pane whose child exits under the default auto-remove runs the full kill-pane cascade including a workspace's LAST pane — its removal empties and closes the workspace and the view lands on the next one, none remaining ends the client after the daemon's exit-when-empty grace. The keep-one-tab hold survives as the `set-workspace-respawn` opt-in (its respawn and crash-loop guard unchanged), and the restore path (`drop_restored_dead_entries`) follows the same predicate, so a restart no longer resurrects a dead last pane the live daemon would have closed. Flipped red/green: `auto_remove_cascades_a_workspaces_last_pane_on_app_exit`, `render_mode_ctrl_d_on_the_workspaces_last_pane_cascades_and_ends`, `auto_remove_restore_cascades_a_workspaces_last_dead_pane`; the opt-in hold is pinned by `auto_remove_restore_keeps_a_respawn_workspaces_last_dead_pane_held` and the respawn tests.
- **par-mux: the CLI grows a verb surface and the friction findings land** (Paul, 2026-10-17; `crates/par-mux/src/bin/par_mux/main.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/tree/`, `docs/MUX.md`, the bundled skill): the R1-R9 findings from the CLI friction audit, breaking where breaking was better. Details below.
- **R1 — subcommands + the collision guard**: `par-mux start|stop|restart|status|ls|attach|config` are the discoverable front door, each translated into the same handler its flag form (`--stop`, `--restart`, `--gen-config`, …) already used — every flag keeps working (par-term's invocations are untouched). A bare NAME that spells a command word (`par-mux list-sessions`) is now refused with a "command word" hint (exit 2) instead of silently starting a stray daemon; an explicit `--socket` alongside the name stays allowed.
- **R2 — `par-mux status`**: the one-shot orientation block (stamp, workspaces, sessions, the displayed session, the pane count), client-side composition over the existing queries; each section degrades to `?` instead of failing the reply, and no-daemon exits 1 with the start hint.
- **R3 — `new-session` flags are honest**: `-n` (first window's name), `-c` (start dir), `-x`/`-y` (initial grid) are implemented (parity with `new-window`, which already had `-n`/`-c`); `-F`/`-f` are refused loudly instead of being consumed and silently dropped.
- **R4 — trailing start commands on every create (BREAKING)**: `new-session`, `new-window`, and `split-window` accept tmux's positional start command — `new-session -s logs -- tail -f x` — threaded into the spawn the same way `respawn-pane`'s command is. The QA-219 "a start command is not supported; use respawn-pane" rejection is gone.
- **R5 — `send-keys --enter`**: the one-shot type-and-run form (`send-keys -t %0 -l 'make check' --enter` appends the Enter byte); a `--enter` anywhere but the exact leading/trailing position stays payload text.
- **R6 — `has-session` + `-F` formats**: `has-session -t <session>` is the exit-code existence probe; `list-sessions`/`list-windows`/`list-panes` take `-F '<format>'` with tmux-style `#{var}` expansion (session/window/workspace ids and names, `window_active`, `pane_active`, `pane_leaf`; unknown names expand to nothing). The formerly ignored `-F` flag spelling on the list commands is now implemented.
- **R7 — `list-panes -t` takes every scope (BREAKING for error-shape expectations)**: a session target lists its active window's panes, a pane target its owning window's, a name a window then a session — the "$N target answers `no such window`" trap is gone (`MuxTree::resolve_pane_listing_target`).
- **R8 — `show-options`/`set-option`**: dump the applied daemon settings and flip the two live booleans (`exit-empty`, `remain-on-exit`); everything else is refused as restart-required. Volatile by design — the config file stays the durable record and `reload-config` re-reads over a set.
- **R9 — the no-daemon hint**: `--cmd` (and `status`) against nothing running now print "start one with `par-mux` …", matching attach's error.
- Pinned by parser tests (`parses_new_session_*`, `send_keys_enter_flag_*`, `parses_has_session`, `list_panes_accepts_every_target_kind`, …), dispatch tests (`has_session_answers_ok_and_error_by_existence`, `set_option_and_show_options_round_trip_the_live_booleans`, `list_panes_session_and_pane_targets_list_the_owning_window`, `list_format_flags_expand_variables`), and CLI integration tests (`status_subcommand_prints_the_orientation_block`, `ls_subcommand_lists_sessions`, `bare_name_matching_a_command_word_is_refused`, `send_keys_enter_flag_runs_the_line`, `new_session_start_command_runs_in_the_pane`, `has_session_exit_codes_probe_existence`, `set_option_and_show_options_round_trip_via_cli`, `list_sessions_format_flag_prints_fields`, `list_panes_session_target_via_cli`). Gate NOT run yet (held for Paul's all-clear).
- **par-mux: the target-less attach rule is now create-or-single-or-refuse** (Paul, 2026-10-17, `9f23f8b`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/targets.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/session.rs`, `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_attach.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): a bare `par-mux attach` against a daemon with no sessions creates the default tree (`new-workspace -n default` — workspace and session `default`, a window named `1`, one unnamed pane) and attaches to it, so a fresh launch lands in a working session instead of the "no sessions exist — create one first" error; exactly one existing session attaches as-is; several are a refusal that lists every session plus the `par-mux attach -t <session>` hint and exits 1 — the user picks, the client no longer falls back to the displayed/newest-session heuristic (the persisted displayed session still drives the follow broadcasts and a restored daemon's `switch-client`). Both render and passthrough seeds follow the one rule; `-t` attaches are unchanged. Pinned by `render_mode_without_target_creates_the_default_session`, `passthrough_without_target_creates_the_default_session`, `render_mode_without_target_refuses_when_several_sessions_exist` (the former "picks the newest session" test, inverted), and `multi_session_error_lists_every_session_and_the_hint`; the multi-session bare-attach fixtures now pin `-t` explicitly.
- **par-mux: `--list-servers` prunes dead registry entries silently** (Paul, 2026-10-17; `crates/par-mux/src/bin/par_mux/main.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the per-entry `par-mux: pruned a dead entry for …` stderr lines are gone — the enumeration still owns cleanup, but the notes were noise in every caller's output.

## [0.61.0] - 2026-10-16

### Fixed
- **par-mux: the codex session hook reports an id `codex resume` can resolve** (card 01a14584371a721490a1859e2c3379c0, live-reported; `fed47e16`; `crates/par-mux/integrations/hooks/par-mux-codex-session-hook.sh`, `crates/par-mux/integrations/hooks/par-mux-codex-session-hook.ps1`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/integrations/codex.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): codex's hook payload names the THREAD, and codex 0.163's `codex resume` rejected it ("no rollout found for thread id") while the transcript's rollout file existed under a different id that resume does resolve — measured live when a restored pane resumed into an error and fell back to a shell. Both hook assets now derive the reported `agent_session_id` and the `session_resume_argv` from the rollout filename (`rollout-<timestamp>-<uuid>.jsonl`) when it parses and differs from the payload id; the `CODEX_THREAD_ID` fork guard still rules on the payload id. Pinned by `the_hook_reports_the_rollout_filename_id_over_the_payload_thread_id`, which drives the shipped .sh asset end to end (mismatch, match, no-transcript, and refusal scenarios).
- **par-mux: a restored agent that exits cleanly keeps its pane on a shell** (card 01a13d03 criterion 1; `b877dd5c`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/agent_resume.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/persist.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the restored pane's surviving tail fired only on a non-zero resume exit (`|| { …; exec "$SHELL" }`), so an agent exiting cleanly (exit 0) ended its pane into the reaper — the restored screen and identity were lost where the failed-resume path kept them. The tail now fires on ANY exit (`; { if [ $? -ne 0 ] …; exec "${SHELL:-sh}" }`), printing `agent resume failed` or `agent exited` accordingly; the pane, its restored screen/scrollback, and its agent identity survive until an explicit `kill-pane` (unix; Windows keeps its structured-argv path unchanged). Pinned by `a_clean_agent_exit_also_leaves_a_live_pane` and the renamed `render_surviving_appends_the_surviving_tail_on_unix`.
- **streaming: the server's clipboard set request honors `allow_clipboard_write`** (SEC-003 follow-up card 01a140bb8fb3765a81f487dbecb62abb; `2e554b34`; `src/streaming/server/messages.rs`, `src/streaming/server/tests.rs`, `docs/SECURITY.md`, `docs/API_REFERENCE.md`): the `clipboard` set operation stored the text and broadcast `clipboard_sync` without checking the OSC 52 write gate, so a host that set `Terminal.set_allow_clipboard_write(False)` still had an authenticated client's set request mutate the terminal clipboard. It now refuses the store and the broadcast like the OSC 52 path does (silent no-op, with a debug log naming the client), while the get path was already gated by `allow_clipboard_read`. The default flag value is `True`, so no working integration changes behavior. Pinned by `clipboard_set_applies_and_broadcasts_by_default` and `clipboard_set_denied_when_allow_clipboard_write_is_off`.
- **par-mux: `terminal` notifications reach ghostty and iTerm2 when kitty's window id leaked into the attach** (`01df71c0`, `28cc5355`, `8c69ab06`, `6e9c7d40`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/notify.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): kitty detection read `KITTY_WINDOW_ID` (or `TERM=xterm-kitty`) alone, and that id leaks into processes started under a kitty window — a ghostty or iTerm2 launched from a kitty shell, for one — so the escape went out as kitty's OSC 99, which neither ghostty nor iTerm2 parses, and nothing showed. Kitty now needs `KITTY_WINDOW_ID` with `TERM_PROGRAM` unset or `kitty` (a host sets its own `TERM_PROGRAM`); everything else gets OSC 9. An attach inside a par-term or par-mux pane still counts as kitty (those panes export `TERM_PROGRAM=kitty` with the id) and gets OSC 99, which the core parses. Pinned by `host_is_kitty_only_when_the_window_id_is_not_leaked` and `render_mode_terminal_delivery_after_reload_is_osc9_under_a_leaked_kitty_env`.
- **par-mux: two attached terminals of different heights no longer clip the shorter one** (`14c4879f`, `7e9baaf0`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/tree/layout_ops.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/client.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/renderer.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): a window is again sized to the componentwise minimum of the clients displaying it (tmux `window-size smallest`), replacing the unreleased latest-attached-client rule, which sized the window to the newest client and ran a shorter terminal's text off its bottom. A report that does not move the window still answers the reporting client alone, so two differently-sized clients cannot re-report each other into a re-seed loop. A client larger than the window now pads the space past it with dim `.` cells, as tmux does. Pinned by `two_attach_clients_do_not_war_over_the_window_size`, `size_reports_take_the_minimum_and_wake_only_on_a_move` and `a_window_smaller_than_the_frame_pads_with_dots`.
- **par-mux attach render mode: a quiet pump re-asserts the host cursor placement once per second** (`0d26830`, `0725da7`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/session.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/renderer.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/mod.rs`; card 01a12e3c4d4376e09d9ca51518218253): every guarded cursor writer keeps the client's belief equal to the host's state, so a host terminal that lost the cursor state outside those writers (the intermittent joe invisible-cursor report — never reproduced through typing, resizes, two-client stress, or daemon-side keys) never self-healed: the guards re-emit only on a state change, and an idle app never changes one. `WindowSession::frame()` now re-emits the placement when the last write ages past `CURSOR_HEARTBEAT` (1 s; ~16 B/s on an idle host, idempotent when healthy). `PAR_MUX_CURSOR_TRACE=1` logs each placement write and each `focused_cursor` `None` reason (view scrolled, DECTCEM hide, out-of-bounds cell, missing rect/emulator/focus) to stderr, so the next occurrence names its hider. Pinned by `heartbeat_re_emits_the_placement_when_quiet`.

### Changed
- **core: OSC 22 is the pointer shape, not the title-stack pop** (`84144d03`, live-reported during the drag-cursor-shape manual pass; `crates/par-term-emu-core/src/terminal/sequences/osc/pointer.rs`, `crates/par-term-emu-core/src/terminal/sequences/osc/mod.rs`, `crates/par-term-emu-core/src/terminal/sequences/osc/title.rs`, `crates/par-term-emu-core/src/terminal/{state.rs,event.rs,event_fields.rs,mod.rs,title_badge.rs}`, `crates/par-term-emu-core/src/terminal/sequences/osc/tests.rs`, `src/ffi.rs`, `include/terminal_core_layout.h`, `src/streaming/protocol.rs`, `docs/VT_SEQUENCES.md`, `docs/API_REFERENCE.md`): the emulator core parsed OSC 22 as xterm's icon-label stack pop, so a host terminal built on this core (par-term) could never react to the pointer shapes the mux attach client writes for its resize handles — the cursor stayed unchanged there while ghostty/kitty/iTerm2 (which read OSC 22 as the kitty pointer shape) worked. OSC 22 now adopts the named pointer shape (`OSC 22;shape ST`, empty/absent resets, names over 64 bytes or invalid UTF-8 ignored whole), stored as `Terminal::pointer_shape()` and announced as the `pointer_shape_changed` event (`poll_events` + observers; FFI kind `TERM_EVENT_POINTER_SHAPE_CHANGED` = 27; no streaming message yet). OSC 23 remains the title-stack pop; xterm's OSC 22 pop is gone — the breaking edge of this change, aligned with what kitty, ghostty, and iTerm2 actually implement. The X11 cursor-font synonyms the mux client now emits alongside the CSS names (`sb_h_double_arrow`/`sb_v_double_arrow`) are exactly what iTerm2's own OSC 22 map parses. Pinned by `test_osc22_pointer_shape` (rust), `a_shape_is_stored_and_announced` and the pointer module's reset/oversize/UTF-8/idempotence tests, and the python `test_osc22_pointer_shape_event`.
- **par-mux: the daemon forks to the background by default and the launching terminal auto-attaches** (requested, 2026-10-16; `a077a8c4`, `07b78226`; `crates/par-mux/src/bin/par_mux/main.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/client.rs`, `crates/par-mux/tests/common/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_cli.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): `par-mux [name]` and `par-mux --restart` now daemonize (fork + `setsid`, stderr to the daemon log) instead of serving in the foreground, wait up to 10 s for the socket to accept, then exec the attach client in the launching process — herdr's launch-or-attach. `--no-attach` skips the attach; a start whose stdin is not a terminal stays daemon-only by itself; `--foreground` restores the old in-process serving (the debugging/supervision escape, and the shape every programmatic spawner uses — the library's `MuxClient` auto-spawn and the test harness now pass it explicitly, because they kill the child they spawned and a detached grandchild could never be named). The nested-daemon refusal is delivered pre-fork, on the terminal, instead of buried in the daemon log. Windows keeps foreground serving (the fork shape is unix). Pinned by `foreground_and_no_attach_conflict_with_the_one_shots_but_compose_with_restart` and, against the real binary, `the_default_start_daemonizes_and_exits_leaving_a_live_daemon`.
- **par-mux: a settings change reaches every attached client** (card 01a1425582ef7103a61647925496d387; `78a723dd`, `9dec0a8f`; `crates/par-term-emu-core/src/tmux_control/`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/buffers.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/emit.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/navigate.rs`, `src/python_bindings/types/notification.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`, `docs/API_REFERENCE.md`): a successful `reload-config` now broadcasts the new argument-less `%config-changed` notification (Python `notification_type == "config-changed"`), and every attached render client re-reads the config file and re-applies its `[client]` settings through the reload chord's own rebind, flashing `config re-applied`. A change made in one client's settings dialog therefore takes effect in every other client from the same host. The client whose reload caused the broadcast finds its applied section unchanged and does nothing; a follower never sends `reload-config` back. A bare file edit still needs some client's reload. A changed `sidebar-width` follows the dialog's geometry/refit path in every client, while unrelated reloads preserve locally dragged widths. Passthrough clients ignore the cue. Pinned by `reload_config_broadcasts_config_changed_only_on_success`, `config_changed_round_trips`, `config_changed_reapplies_the_file_without_resending_reload`, `render_mode_settings_change_reaches_every_other_attached_client` and `render_mode_config_changed_reaches_a_client_attached_after_a_reload`.
- **par-mux: `notify-delivery = "inside"` also rings the terminal bell** (`1ecf150b`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/notify.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the toast now queues a BEL with the next frame, so the host terminal flags the tab or window unread when the card appears. Pinned by `inside_delivery_shows_a_toast_that_expires_and_clicks_to_the_pane` and `render_mode_inside_delivery_paints_a_toast`.
- **par-mux: an app exit lands on the next pane, tab, or workspace instead of detaching; keep-one-tab workspaces** (card 01a13d03; `8f440d0e`, `6429f18b`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/server/reap.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/server/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/tree/lifecycle.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/persist.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/targets.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/status.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): two rules. **Keep-one-tab (daemon):** under `remain-on-exit = false` the reaper no longer removes a pane that is its workspace's last (the only pane of the only window of the only session); it is held dead as under remain-on-exit — window, session, and workspace stay, only `%pane-exited` is broadcast, and `respawn-pane` (the `C-b r` chord in both attach modes) restarts it. Explicit `kill-*` commands keep their full cascade. A restore under auto-remove keeps such a pane held too, and an attached client now holds an all-dead daemon past the exit-when-empty grace (a daemon with zero sessions still exits under a client). **Landing walks up the tree (render client):** a gone shown session lands on its workspace's landing session's active window, a gone workspace on the daemon's active workspace's; the view ends only when the daemon has nothing left to show or exits. Behavior change: Ctrl+D on a session's last pane used to end every render client showing it; with a sibling session in the workspace they now land there (`render_mode_ctrl_d_on_a_sessions_last_pane_lands_on_the_sibling_session`). Pinned by `auto_remove_holds_a_workspaces_last_pane_on_app_exit`, `auto_remove_removes_down_to_the_workspaces_last_pane`, `remain_on_exit_holds_every_exit_unchanged`, `kill_pane_of_a_workspaces_last_pane_still_cascades`, `render_mode_ctrl_d_on_the_workspaces_last_pane_keeps_the_client`, `render_mode_kill_workspace_of_the_only_workspace_ends_the_client`, `render_mode_kill_of_the_shown_workspace_lands_on_the_surviving_one`, `is_workspace_last_pane_needs_one_pane_window_and_session`, `auto_remove_restore_keeps_a_workspaces_last_dead_pane_held` and `auto_remove_restore_drops_dead_entries_down_to_the_last`.
- **`par-term-emu-derive` bumped to 0.48.0** (audit ARC-006; `derive/src/lib.rs`, `Cargo.toml`): the derive macro now supports named-field structs and a `#[pydict(nested)]` field attribute, so the five streaming stats structs derive their Python dict conversion and the hand-rolled cyclomatic-51 `system_stats_to_py_dict` is deleted. The version bump keeps the next crates.io publish from resolving a registry derive without struct support.
- **Breaking (Rust API): `TmuxNotification::AgentStateChanged` gains a `ws: String` field** (ENH-061; `crates/par-term-emu-core/src/tmux_control/`): the variant is not `#[non_exhaustive]`, so Rust embedders that destructure it without `..` or construct it with a struct literal must add the field (par-term's `mux_drain.rs` and its test helper are the known sites, to be updated when par-term bumps its dependency). Older parsers are fail-closed on the new wire line: a pre-ENH-061 core parser folds `ws=+1` into the state and finds an empty `source`, and par-term's roster then drops the push (`AgentSource::parse()` is None) — the roster stays at its last `list-agents` value rather than corrupting. A new parser against an old daemon reads `ws` as empty and falls back to the refresh path. The next release must be a minor bump.
- **Breaking (par-mux library): `mux::integrations` and `mux::skill_installer` now require the `mux-bin` feature** (ARC-143; `crates/par-mux/Cargo.toml`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/mod.rs`): the agent-integration and skill installers back only the `par-mux` binary's one-shot flags, so they and their `jsonc-parser` dependency moved from `mux` to `mux-bin` (the ARC-106 binary-only rule). A `mux` library consumer (the streaming `MuxSessionFactory`, par-term) no longer compiles about 3,400 lines of installer code or the embedded hook/extension/SKILL.md assets. Rust code that called these modules under plain `mux` must enable `mux-bin`. par-term is unaffected: it enables only `mux`, references neither module, and installs the skill by running the `par-mux` binary.
- **par-mux attach render mode: the agent roster moves from the status bar into the side panel** (`c734266`, `6576eca1`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/status.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/sidebar.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/input_route.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/navigate.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/client.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`; card 01a12e3c917172d68f64c5d80fa90edc): the status bar no longer composes the `agent:state` chips — the side panel gains an agents section under the workspace rows, one `{workspace} · {agent} ({state})` row per roster entry, grouped by workspace name, the focused pane's row inverted, and a click lands on the agent's pane (`select-pane` + a same-window focus move; a cross-window click re-lands the view). The grouping key is a new `ws=<id>` key=value token on every `list-agents` / `client-snapshot` agent row (the pane's owning workspace, `+N`) — trailing extras flow through `AgentRow::parse` untouched, so consumers that ignore unknown keys are unaffected. Pinned by `sidebar_agents_section_groups_by_workspace`, `sidebar_agent_row_click_focuses_the_pane`, the updated wire round-trip and `mux_agents` integration assertions, and the PTY-level `render_mode_side_panel_carries_the_agent_roster` + the flipped no-chip status-bar test.
- **`par-mux` now rejects `--gen-config` combined with `--stop`, `--restart` or `--cmd`** (QA-247; `crates/par-mux/src/bin/par_mux/main.rs`): the twelve one-shot mode flags form one clap `ArgGroup` (`daemon_mode`, at most one per invocation) in place of ten hand-kept conflict lists, which had left those three pairs accepted, with the second flag silently ignored after `--gen-config` wrote the config and exited. Every other pairing already conflicted. `--cmd` still conflicts with `--state-dir`. Pinned by `mode_group_tests`, which checks all 66 pairs.

### Added
- **par-mux: Muse Code agent integration** (card 01a146707059723a86f2fd1bfaafb6e4; `b80ca269`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/integrations/muse.rs`, `crates/par-mux/integrations/hooks/par-mux-muse-session-hook.sh`, `crates/par-mux/integrations/hooks/par-mux-muse-session-hook.ps1`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/hooks/report.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/hooks/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/agent_resume.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/scrape.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/patterns/muse.toml`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/integrations/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/bin/par_mux/main.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): muse (Meta's Muse Code CLI) joins as the fourth config-entry hook agent. muse clears its hook environment down to `MUSE_SESSION_ID`/`MUSE_THREAD_ID` (verified live against 1.4.9 and 1.4.10), so its reporter cannot carry `PAR_MUX_PANE_ID` the way the other agents' do: the new `pane.report_agent_session_cwd` control method takes the payload's cwd and the daemon resolves the pane itself — local panes only, then a pane already claimed by the agent, then an unclaimed pane whose spawn command names the agent as a word, then any unclaimed pane in that directory (the shell pane hosting a hand-typed `muse`), ties to the lowest pane id, no match an error reply the reporter ignores (so fanning to every discovered `default_socket_path`-tier socket, including daemons with no matching pane, is harmless). The reporter assets discover those sockets themselves (`/run/user/<uid>` else the per-UID temp dir; the Windows port derives each pipe from the `par-mux-*.sock` marker files' paths). The hook registers in the user-scope `~/.config/muse/settings.json` (`hooks.SessionStart`, matcher-less, the claude arm's merge discipline; a fresh file bootstraps with muse's required `schema_version: 1`). The resume table gains muse: `muse resume <id>`, falling back to the workspace-scoped `muse resume --last` (CLI flag verified against 1.4.9) when no id was ever reported. The scrape tier bundles herdr's muse pattern set (`patterns/muse.toml`, Apache-2.0 credited) so muse panes roster with state the claude shape delivers — identity by hook, state by scrape. Live e2e (muse 1.4.10 in a par-mux pane): the roster shows `%N muse idle scrape ws=+N`, a daemon `--restart` resumed the exact session (`resumed session <uuid>` from `muse resume <id>`), and quitting the agent left the pane on a shell via the surviving tail. Pinned by the muse installer round-trips, `a_cwd_keyed_report_claims_the_pane_running_in_that_directory`, `a_cwd_keyed_report_never_steals_a_different_agents_claim`, `a_cwd_keyed_report_prefers_the_pane_already_claimed_by_the_agent`, `an_agent_named_spawn_wins_over_an_unclaimed_shell_pane_in_the_same_cwd`, `the_spawn_command_names_the_agent_as_a_word`, `muse_resumes_the_reported_id_through_its_resume_subcommand`, `muse_without_an_id_resumes_last_in_the_workspace` and `muse_rules_read_trust_approval_working_and_idle`.
- **par-mux: version-aware integration status** (card 01a145c10cd77622a630c941522c2644, requested; `07cce00e`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/integrations/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/integrations/claude.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/integrations/codex.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/integrations/grok.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/integrations/extensions.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/skill_installer.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/bin/par_mux/main.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/modal_settings.rs`, `crates/par-mux/skills/par-mux/SKILL.md`, `crates/par-mux/integrations/hooks/` (codex assets bumped to v2), `docs/MUX.md`): every bundled integration asset already carried a `PAR_MUX_INTEGRATION_VERSION` marker, but nothing parsed it and every status surface reported only `installed|absent` — the gap that let a stale codex hook keep failing daemon restores after a par-mux upgrade with nothing able to say so (live, 2026-10-16). The integrations module now grades every installed asset by byte-compare against its bundled copy (`absent` / `current` / `outdated`) and parses the marker on both sides; `--agent-hooks-status` keeps its `installed|absent` second token (par-term's parser) and appends the currency (`v2`, `v1 → v2 outdated`, `asset missing`); a new `--integration-status` reports hooks, the pi/omp extensions, and the skill in one grammar; the settings dialog's integrations section lists all of them with the same values instead of a bare check mark. The codex hook assets are bumped to v2 (their reporting behavior changed today), and the skill carries the marker in an HTML comment. Sister-project note for par-term: the `--agent-hooks-status` second token is unchanged; anything parsing beyond it is new.
- **par-mux: an aggregate cap on the control clients' broadcast queues** (ARC-008 card 01a14068ced87253ba5d39d6847a8f0a; `d68a4b99`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/server/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/server/client.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/server/broadcast.rs`, `docs/SECURITY.md`, `docs/MUX.md`): the per-client queue depth alone let K stalled clients pin K x ~128 MiB in the worst case. Every client now carries a queued-line counter (the fan-out's successful pushes minus the writer's drained lines), and once the sum crosses 8192 lines the push evicts the client holding the deepest queue — the stalled one — while live clients keep draining. A queue under a quarter of the per-client depth is never the victim, so an aggregate that crosses the cap through many shallow queues reads as a healthy crowd, not a stall. The bound is a documented cap in docs/SECURITY.md and pinned by `the_aggregate_cap_evicts_the_deepest_queue` and `the_aggregate_cap_spares_shallow_queues`.
- **par-mux: the pi/omp agent-state extension assets are generated from one template** (ARC-002 card 01a14068c3c7778a9400453b0509f198; `scripts/gen_extensions.py`, `crates/par-mux/integrations/extensions/par-mux-agent-state.ts`, `crates/par-mux/integrations/extensions/par-mux-omp-agent-state.ts`, `Makefile`, `docs/MUX.md`): the two shipped assets were hand-maintained forks of one reporter — the transport half (the socket retry ladder, the send queue, the seq/session-ref plumbing) byte-shared, while fixes landed in one fork and had to be back-ported by hand (a measured 23µs seq-swap race was once fixed twice for exactly this reason). Both now render from a single template: shared sections once, the dialect wiring (agent name, resume-argv shape, nested-session guard, default-export events) as knobs. `make gen-extensions` re-renders, and the new `extensions-check` step in `make checkall` fails when a committed asset is not what the template renders, so the next fix can only land in the template. Rendered behavior is pinned by the live-daemon e2e (`pi_extension_drives_a_live_daemon`, `omp_extension_drives_a_live_daemon`), which passed byte-identical in broadcast counts. The omp fork's queue-race comment is now carried by both dialects, `isAbsoluteSessionPath` is unified (pi's inline copy folded in), and omp's `reportSession` default parameter became explicit `"startup"` arguments at its call sites (wire-identical).
- **par-mux render client: exit cue and respawn chord; the respawn chord is configurable** (card 01a13d03; `9552bf1d`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/status_patch.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/session.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/navigate.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/renderer.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/modal_settings.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/status_row.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/panels.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/config.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): a held pane in the shown window now puts `(exited N — C-b r respawns)` at the head of the render status bar (`(%N exited N)` when another pane is the held one), from a live `%pane-exited` or the registration replay. Prefix `r` sends `respawn-pane -t <focused>` only while the focused pane is held (a live pane gets a `pane is running` flash; never `-k`); `%pane-respawned` clears the cue and resets the pane's emulator before the replay. The chord is a management chord in both modes, `[client] respawn` (default `r`), listed in the settings dialog's keys tab (`respawn dead pane`) and the help panel; both modes' cues spell the live binding. Pinned by `exit_cue_and_respawn_chord_follow_the_held_pane`, `render_mode_held_pane_shows_the_exit_cue_and_the_chord_respawns_it`, `render_mode_pane_held_before_attach_shows_the_exit_cue`, `render_mode_settings_keys_tab_lists_the_respawn_chord` and `settings_keys_section_scrolls_to_every_row`.
- **par-mux: keep-one-tab respawn, a per-workspace option** (card 01a13d03 criterion 4; `417a894f`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/server/reap.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/tree/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/tree/lifecycle.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/pane.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/persist.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/sessions.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/modal_menu.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): `set-workspace-respawn -t <workspace> [on|off|toggle]` (bare = query; replies `on`/`off`) and the side panel's workspace menu row `respawn on exit`. When on, the workspace's last pane restarts in place through `respawn-pane` when its process exits (same pane id, its stored command and cwd; `%pane-exited` then `%pane-respawned`) instead of being held dead. Off by default (the hold-dead keep-one-tab stays the base behavior); a pane that lived under one second is held instead, so an instantly exiting command cannot respawn every reap tick. The flag is saved with the workspace (`respawn_last`, omitted from the state file while off). Pinned by `workspace_respawn_restarts_the_last_pane_in_place`, `workspace_respawn_holds_a_pane_that_dies_at_once`, `workspace_respawn_survives_a_daemon_restart`, `parses_set_workspace_respawn_values_and_query` and `workspace_menu_respawn_row_toggles_and_flashes_the_state`.
- **par-mux attach: the resize pointer covers the side panel's own handles, and the settings dialog's choice rows cycle on ←/→** (`7565bbc3`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/input_route.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/modal_settings.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): `drag-cursor-shape` now shapes the pointer over the side panel's edge column (the width drag, `ew-resize`) and its rule row (the areas' split, `ns-resize`) — the same predicates the press hit-test applies, the chevron's cell excluded — and a live sidebar drag keeps its axis shape with the pointer off the panel, restoring on release (live-report fix: only pane dividers had shaped). The settings dialog's discrete rows (toggles, choice lists like `notify-delivery`) change value on `→`/`←` in place of Enter — forward and back, wrapping, written and applied live — while the prompt-edited rows (numbers, hex, chords) keep the arrows for section switching (live-report fix: the arrows used to jump sections under the value, reading as "cannot change"). Pinned by `sidebar_handles_shape_the_pointer`, `settings_choice_rows_cycle_with_the_arrows` and, PTY-level against the real binary with the user's config shape, `render_mode_settings_notify_delivery_cycles`.
- **par-mux attach: the three live-testing feedback cues** (`13914144`, `2a441732`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/input_route.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/session.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/pointer.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/status_row.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/pump.rs`): an armed prefix paints ` prefix ` on the status row in both modes (clearing on the next key); a raised flash — the reload chord's `config reloaded` (or its error) and every other flash site — paints on the arming tick instead of waiting for an unrelated repaint it can clear before; and `drag-cursor-shape` now shapes the POINTER over divider hovers and live drags through OSC 22 `ew-resize`/`ns-resize` (ghostty/kitty/xterm; terminals without it keep the DECSCUSR steady-block drag fallback), restoring on leave, drag end (the release report itself restores), and detach. The resize names are the axis-pair spellings, not `col-resize`/`row-resize`: ghostty's macOS backend maps only the former pair and silently drops the axis-named ones (live-report fix). Every context menu also gained a subtle hover cue — the action row under the pointer carries its label reversed, driven by no-button motion reports. Pinned by `prefix_indicator_paints_the_row_and_clears_on_the_next_key`, `passthrough_prefix_arms_the_status_row_cue`, `reload_chord_flash_paints_on_the_arming_tick`, `drag_cursor_shape_option_shapes_the_pointer`, `drag_release_restores_the_pointer_shape`, `menu_hover_highlights_the_row_under_the_pointer`, `emit_writes_only_changes_and_restores_with_text` and, PTY-level against the real binary, `render_mode_drag_cursor_shape_writes_osc22_to_the_host`.
- **par-mux attach: the settings dialog exposes every TUI-facing `[client]` key** (`33444e87`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/modal_settings.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/modal_prompt.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/settings_write.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the dialog's sections became one row per config key — `sidebar` (show on launch, width, the `agent-rows` layout as `default`/`compact`/`detailed` presets), `borders` (line style, pane boxes, labels in border, pane gaps, the focus and border `#rrggbb` colors), `general` (attach mode — applied at the next launch, scroll gutter, drag cursor shape, resize step), `notify`, the `states` multi-select, and a scrolling `keys` tab with the prefix, the reload chord, and every management chord. Toggles flip and choices cycle on Enter; numbers, hex colors, and chords open the prompt modal seeded with the value in force and validate before writing — a bad range, malformed hex, or a chord that does not parse flashes and writes nothing, and an empty hex clears the override — with Escape returning to the dialog. `sidebar-width` applies to the live panel like a drag release. Pinned by `tabs_and_rows_fit_the_panel`, `row_sections_show_labels_and_values`, `keys_section_scrolls_and_spells_chords`, `agent_rows_presets_round_trip`, `settings_rows_write_and_apply_live`, `settings_prompt_rows_edit_numbers_hex_and_chords` and `settings_keys_section_scrolls_to_every_row`.
- **par-mux attach: in-app notification toasts, and the settings dialog closes on a click outside it** (`340f86c1`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/toast.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/notify.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/modal_settings.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/input_route.rs`): `notify-delivery` gains herdr's `inside` option (the settings dialog lists `off`, `inside par-mux`, `via terminal`, `via system`): a bordered toast at the pane area's bottom-right with the state's colored dot, `{agent} {state}` and `{workspace} · {agent}`, up 8 s when blocked and 5 s otherwise; a click on it focuses the agent's pane. A press outside the settings dialog closes it. Pinned by `inside_delivery_shows_a_toast_that_expires_and_clicks_to_the_pane`, `render_mode_inside_delivery_paints_a_toast` and `command_menu_settings_opens_the_dialog_and_applies`.
- **par-mux attach: the side panel's command menu opens beside its chip** (`b913ccfb`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/renderer.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/paint.rs`): instead of centering, the ` menu ` chip's menu anchors its left edge just right of the chip and its bottom edge on the chip's row, kept inside the frame. Every overlay now paints and hit-tests through one geometry. Pinned by `menu_chip_opens_the_command_menu` and `anchored_overlay_stays_inside_the_frame`.
- **par-mux attach side panel: the active entry is herdr's subtle band, not a reversed block** (`b703990d`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/sidebar.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/panels.rs`): the active workspace and the focused agent paint on the host background lifted a step (`#1c1c29` over black; darker on a light theme; herdr's `#1e1e2e` when the probe failed), the entry's first line bold in the full foreground, its later lines dim, and its state dot keeping its color. Pinned by `sidebar_active_entry_paints_a_subtle_band`.
- **par-mux attach: the command menu drops its `commands` header row, and a click outside any context menu closes it** (`ef9b13e1`, `cfae4429`, `da0e2346`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/modal_menu.rs`): the box's ` menu ` title already names it, so the rows start at `settings`; the tab and workspace menus keep the name of their target as the header. A press outside an open menu dismisses it without reaching the pane under it, as the settings dialog already did. Pinned by `compose_command_menu_maps_rows_to_actions`, `menu_chip_opens_the_command_menu` and `open_menu_swallows_non_press_pointer_events`.
- **par-mux attach: a settings dialog in the side panel's command menu** (`f70464df`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/modal_settings.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/settings_write.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the command menu's new `settings` row opens herdr's tabbed dialog — sidebar (show on launch), borders (line style), notify (delivery), states (which agent states notify), and integrations (the agent hooks' install state, read-only). Arrows/`j`/`k` select, Tab/`h`/`l` switch sections, Enter/space or a click applies: the key is written into `config.toml` in place through the new `toml_edit` dependency (comments and other keys preserved, an unparsable file left alone), then the reload path rebinds the client and sends `reload-config`. Pinned by `command_menu_settings_opens_the_dialog_and_applies`, `set_client_key_preserves_the_user_file`, `write_client_key_creates_and_updates_the_file` and `render_mode_settings_dialog_opens_from_the_panel_menu`.
- **par-mux attach: notifications on agent state change** (`41ceeaf8`, `3cf082d0`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/notify.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/config.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): a pushed `%agent-state-changed` into a state selected by `[client] notify-states` (default `["blocked", "idle"]`) announces `{workspace} · {agent}: {state}` for an agent outside the shown window (herdr's background-only rule) through `[client] notify-delivery`: `terminal` (default; OSC 9, or kitty's OSC 99, written with the next frame), `system` (`osascript` / `notify-send`, argv only and detached), or `off`. Refreshes and reconnects never notify, a repeat of the same state never notifies, and pane-controlled names are stripped of ESC/BEL/ST before entering the escape. An unknown delivery or state warns and falls back rather than refusing the attach. Pinned by `agent_state_push_notifies_selected_transitions_only`, `render_mode_background_agent_state_notifies_the_host`, `terminal_notification_escapes_are_sanitized` and `notify_keys_resolve_defaults_explicit_and_warn_on_unknowns`.
- **par-mux attach side panel: configurable multi-line agent entries with the project name** (`3f9efa2a`, `859f3e82`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/config.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/panels.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/sidebar.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/host_probe.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/ipc/rows.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): a new `[client] agent-rows` key (herdr's `[ui.sidebar.agents] rows`) lays out each agent entry as one panel line per inner array of tokens (`state_icon`, `state`, `workspace`, `project`, `agent`, `pane`), defaulting to `● {workspace} · {project}` over `{agent}`; an unknown token is skipped with a warning. The project is the basename of the git work tree containing the agent pane's cwd, reported by the daemon's host probe as a new `project` field in the roster's `host_telemetry=` token (no git run; a linked worktree names its main repo). While an agent's project is unknown the client re-queries the roster every 10 s (the probe broadcasts nothing). Every line of an entry is clickable and carries its active block. Pinned by `agent_entry_lines_render_the_token_rows`, `compose_sidebar_lays_out_multi_line_agent_entries`, `sidebar_agent_entries_follow_agent_rows`, `agent_rows_resolve_default_explicit_and_warn_on_unknown_tokens`, `missing_project_polls_the_roster_until_it_arrives`, `project_name_is_the_repo_root_basename` and `agent_row_project_reads_the_host_telemetry_token`.
- **par-mux attach side panel: herdr's split, collapse rail, and persisted panel state** (`8355816f`, `b140811d`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/panels.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/prefs.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/sidebar.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/input_route.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the workspace and agents areas split the panel's rows 50/50 by default (each area keeps at least three rows), and the rule drag now moves that ratio rather than a fixed row. The ` new ` / ` menu ` chips moved from the panel's last row (under the agents) to the workspace area's last row, matching herdr. A `«` chevron at the panel's bottom-right collapses it to a 2-column status rail (workspace and agent indices with their state glyphs, colored by state) and `»` expands it. The dragged width, the split, and the collapse state persist in `client-state.json` beside the config file and override `sidebar-width` at the next attach. Pinned by `compose_sidebar_splits_half_and_ends_the_workspace_area_with_the_chips`, `compose_sidebar_split_ratio_moves_the_rule_and_clamps`, `compose_sidebar_rail_shows_indices_statuses_and_the_expand_chevron`, `sidebar_chevron_collapses_to_the_rail_and_persists`, `prefs_round_trip_and_tolerate_garbage` and `render_mode_persisted_collapsed_panel_launches_as_the_rail`.
- **par-mux attach render mode pipelines its daemon commands** (ENH-049; `6d9df973`, `14cd6cce`; crates/par-mux/src/mux/client.rs, crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/): the pump no longer blocks on a daemon reply per command. MuxClient gains an owed-reply queue (send_nowait/send_forget/poll_replies/outstanding) so async and sync commands share one connection without reply theft; fire-and-forget chords and forwarded keystrokes (send-keys chunks, with a 64-chunk outstanding cap and a stdin backlog for a wedged daemon) are written without waiting; reply-consuming commands and chains (split, new-window, zoom, kill-pane, focus_pane, land_window, switch_* and the picker/menu queries) park continuations that the pump dispatches at its ~16 ms frame tick, with a staleness guard dropping a landing a newer navigation superseded. Startup reads, seed/reseed/resize internals and the status refresh stay synchronous and bounded at the 1 s interactive budget. A wedged daemon now shows the daemon-busy flash instead of freezing the UI. Pinned by slow_daemon_does_not_block_key_forwarding (and its blocking red-proof), the owed-queue unit tests, focus_pane_chain_pipelines_..., and the render_mode_ integration suite.
- **par-mux attach: mouse-forwarding and render-integrity fixes from the input-route pass** (`f3f3484`, `4822383`, `19b5a1d`, `a722e239`, `b40b5d3`; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/input_route.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/session.rs`): mouse reports forward in the dialect the pane negotiated, motion reports reach only panes that requested them, a click no longer re-selects the focused pane, a push burst holds the render frame until it completes, and divider drags claim the drag at pointer speed instead of hijacking edge clicks.
- **`%agent-state-changed` carries the pane's owning workspace** (ENH-061; `4d54a6bf`; `crates/par-term-emu-core/src/tmux_control/`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/emit.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/hooks/report.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/scrape.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/status.rs`): the daemon appends a trailing `ws=+N` token (after `source=`) to every agent-state push, read from the same pane->window->session->workspace map `list-agents` uses, so the attach client's roster stays fully incremental — a newly claimed pane's side-panel row is grouped and labeled correctly on the push itself, with no re-query. The parser treats trailing lowercase `key=value` tokens as metadata (unknown keys ignored, never folded into the state), so a three-token line keeps its `source=hook`-as-state edge case. Pinned by the `par_mux_extension_optional_fields_decode` ws cases, `agent_event_patches_without_a_query` (the ws-carrying insert groups without a re-query), the ARC-138 empty-ws insert still marking the status stale, and the `mux_hooks`/`mux_agents` push-vs-roster agreement asserts.
- **`pinned-tests-check` gate: documentation test citations must resolve** (ENH-057; scripts/check_pinned_tests.py): a new checkall gate checks the snake_case backticked tokens of every pinned clause in docs/*.md and CHANGELOG's [Unreleased] body against the tree's #[test] fns, test-bearing mods, tests/ integration-target stems, and pytest fns; a rename or deletion leaves a dangling citation and fails the gate. Released sections and docs/opus/ stay out of scope; the self-test injects the drift on a synthetic tree and requires the real tree's 25-citation baseline to keep resolving.
- **audit gates: the python API-surface ratchet and the doc-hidden baseline gate** (`744b0d17`, `b289c0d0`, `29b78306`; audit ARC-001 and ARC-005; `scripts/check_api_surface.py`, `scripts/check_doc_hidden.py`): `make checkall` fails when the flat `Terminal`/`PtyTerminal` Python surface grows past its per-class baseline, and when the crates' `#[doc(hidden)]` public set drifts from its DESIGN.md baseline; the doc-hidden check also fails closed on inline and unparseable items.
- **par-mux attach: `PAR_MUX_INPUT_TRACE` diagnostics for the render mouse path** (`d24fe511`, `e0b7ba37`, `589104e4`; `docs/CONFIG_REFERENCE.md`): setting the env var traces the input side (mouse reports, replay/reseed), the output side (frames written), and the reply rows on one stream, so a mis-forwarded click or a stale frame can be diffed against what the daemon actually sent.
- **`mux-docs-check` pins the `list-agents` roster grammar to its emitter** (ENH-056; `scripts/check_mux_docs.py`): check 6 extracts the trailing `key=value` keys from `roster_row` (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/client.rs`) and requires every one (`reason=`, `telemetry=`, `host_telemetry=`, `ws=`) to be named in MUX.md's `list-agents` bullet — which must also keep the consumer rule 'unknown keys are ignored' — and in the embedded agent skill's `list-agents` lines, which must not revert to the pre-ARC-060 free-text `[message]` form. A new extras key in the emitter fails the gate until both docs name it; the self-test injects the drift (plus an empty `roster_row` fail-closed case) alongside the existing ten.
- **`env-docs-check` gate: every env var production code reads is documented** (ENH-055; `4d6e820f`; `scripts/check_env_docs.py`, `docs/CONFIG_REFERENCE.md`): a new checkall gate scans the Rust crates (literal and const-resolved `env::var`/`env::var_os` reads), the shipped agent-integration extensions/hooks (TS/sh/ps1), and the Python package, and diffs the read set against `docs/CONFIG_REFERENCE.md`'s Environment Variables table, MUX.md's pane-env contract, and the streamer's clap `env =` set — in both directions, with test-only reads excluded by path and `#[cfg(test)]` stripping, and failing closed on unresolvable reads. Its first run added seven undocumented rows (`PAR_MUX_OMP_RETRY_GRACE_MS`, `PAR_MUX_SENDLOG`, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `CODEX_HOME`, `GROK_HOME`, `PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR`, `PI_CONFIG_DIR`) and the script's self-test injects one drift per contract, including the fail-closed cases.
- **`ipc_rows` cargo-fuzz target drives the attach client's reply-row parsers** (ENH-060; `fuzz/fuzz_targets/ipc_rows.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/ipc/rows.rs`): the target feeds every typed row parser (`WindowRow`, `SessionRow`, `WorkspaceRow`, `AgentRow`) whole input, per line, and with a snapshot-style prefix stripped, asserting the round-trip invariant — each parser either rejects the line or returns a row whose `Display` parses back to itself. Twelve golden seeds cover the `key=value` extras grammar (`reason=`, `telemetry=`, `host_telemetry=`, `ws=`, unknown keys, empty keys/values, tabs, U+3000 separators, rejects), and a stable-toolchain unit test (`ipc_rows_fuzz_seeds_round_trip`) replays the same seeds so `make checkall` covers the invariant without nightly. All nine fuzz targets now have a `make fuzz-<target>` entry and a CI-matrix slot.

### Security
- **OSC 52 clipboard writes can be refused with `allow_clipboard_write`** (SEC-003; `crates/par-term-emu-core/src/terminal/sequences/osc/clipboard.rs`, `crates/par-term-emu-core/src/terminal/mod.rs`, `src/python_bindings/common.rs`, `docs/SECURITY.md`): the OSC 52 query was gated by `allow_clipboard_read`, but the write path always stored attacker-controlled text as the clipboard content, which hosts that mirror it to the system clipboard turned into an ungated write channel. The new `Terminal::set_allow_clipboard_write` / `allow_clipboard_write` (Python: `Terminal` and `PtyTerminal`) defaults to `true`, so behavior is unchanged; `false` makes OSC 52 writes a silent no-op and survives RIS like the read flag. Pinned by `test_osc52_clipboard_write_denied`, `test_osc52_clipboard_write_flag_survives_ris`, `ris_preserves_host_config`, plus the Python RIS host-config classification probe.
- **par-mux client: daemon reply/notification lines are read with a 4 MiB per-line budget** (ENH-058; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/client.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/server/protocol.rs`): `reader_loop` splits the daemon's stream through the daemon-side `fill_line_bounded` helper instead of `BufRead::lines()`, whose unbounded accumulation let a broken or wedged daemon writing one unterminated line grow every client (`par-mux` CLI, `par-mux attach`, `AttachConn`) without limit toward OOM. A line past `MAX_REPLY_LINE_BYTES` (4 MiB — a healthy daemon's longest line is a `show-buffer` body under the daemon's own 1 MiB input budget, so it never trips on a healthy daemon) closes the connection with a warning; a pending command reports the existing "server closed the connection" (`ConnectionAborted`) mapping. The threat model matches SEC-104: same-user peer, accidental runaway growth, not an adversary. Pinned by `read_daemon_line_strips_lf_and_crlf`, `read_daemon_line_trips_the_budget_without_a_newline`, `read_daemon_line_closes_on_non_utf8`, and `an_unterminated_oversize_daemon_line_aborts_the_pending_command`.
- **The streamer's HTTP responses carry a full Content-Security-Policy** (ENH-059; `src/streaming/server/http.rs`, `docs/SECURITY.md`): the SEC-010 `frame-ancestors 'none'`-only header is replaced by a full policy — scripts same-origin plus `'unsafe-inline'` (Next.js inlines a per-build RSC payload, so hashes would break every other `--download-frontend` version) and `'wasm-unsafe-eval'` (the image addon's decoder), styles/fonts allow the shipped export's Google Fonts and jsDelivr Nerd Font hosts, `img-src` allows `blob:` for the image addon, `connect-src` allows any `ws:`/`wss:` (the UI accepts a user-typed server URL), and `object-src`/`form-action` are locked to `'none'`/`'self'` with `base-uri 'self'`. `csp_allows_every_external_origin_the_shipped_frontend_loads` scans the shipped `web_term/` export and fails until any new external origin is added to the constant in the same change; startup-computed script hashes are a possible follow-up.
- **par-term-streamer `--require-bcrypt` refuses weak HTTP Basic Auth credentials at startup** (SEC-213; `src/bin/streaming_server/cli.rs`, `src/bin/streaming_server/bootstrap.rs`, `docs/SECURITY.md`, `docs/STREAMING.md`): the new opt-in flag (`PAR_TERM_REQUIRE_BCRYPT`, requires `--http-user`) makes the streamer exit with an error when the configured credential — from `--http-password`, `--http-password-hash`, or `--http-password-file` — is a cleartext password, a legacy `{SHA}` / `$1$` / `$apr1$` hash, or any non-bcrypt string. The error names the user and the legacy format but never the password or hash. Off by default, so existing deployments are unchanged; legacy formats will stop being accepted by default in a later minor release. Pinned by the `require_bcrypt_tests` module (runs on Windows too) and `require_bcrypt_requires_http_user`.
