- **Other work in this cycle, cited for traceability** (`2cf0957` pane-border ring and label honor border-active-color / border-color; `693ba68` sidebar-on-launch client option shows the workspace panel at attach (default true); `1e6975b` refresh-client -I declares per-pane chrome; the division sizes PTYs to the interior (card 01a11c5b); `0068dfc` attach tab strip takes herdr's tab-bar look; `b2266a7` emit %client-attached / %client-left lifecycle notifications; `01c89b9` side panel menu chip opens a command menu (keybinds, reload config, detach); `d593b87` multi-client selection sync + smallest-attached-client sizing; `666d3c4` standalone par-mux binaries in GitHub Releases; `7e8e26e` coherent panel-up top row, workspaces-only panel, context menus, right-aligned Help chip; `e0d30b1` refit repaints the strips; herdr default, status toggle, tab +/new-tab prompt, help scrollbar; `9aad739` sidebar herdr treatment - nested windows, inverted active block, compact top row; `f8a0b1c` toggleable workspace side panel (herdr's sidebar); `dca1376` workspace picker, scroll-position indicator, default-on pane labels, modal footer band, shift-arrow feedback; `f568b76` herdr border style, label/swap chords, two-axis pane resize, shaded tab strip; `6a3fc4c` split-window and new-window accept session, window, and pane targets (tmux parity); `5214dd5` render-mode zoom, live rename prompts, border-style cycling, prefix+arrow pane navigation; fix missing…; `45def2c` render mode is the default attach pipeline, --mode validates loudly, tmux-convention divider halves,…; `e33833c` configurable exit-empty — [daemon] exit-empty, live via reload-config; `860b62b` attach picker overlay + herdr parity options (pane-gaps, scrollbar-gutter, drag cursor); `c7472d3` attach workspaces surface — status segment, W/C-w chords, %workspaces-changed staleness; `78c0c62` workspaces — a first-class level above sessions).
### Changed
- **par-mux attach render mode resizes and reseeds in place, replaying only panes new to the window** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/`, ENH-044): a host resize, a sidebar or status-bar refit, and a window reseed no longer throw the `PaneRenderer` away. The new `PaneRenderer::resize` rebuilds only the frame buffers and keeps the pane emulators, focus, titles, side-panel sections, any open overlay, and every display option. Surviving panes reflow in `apply_layout` the same way the daemon's pane terminal does, so only panes new to the renderer send a `refresh-client -t %N` replay. On a 6-pane window a drag-resize step used to cost 6 blocking replays and now costs none. The drain after the size report now keeps every event in order instead of discarding all but the `%layout-change`. `%output` that arrives before the layout change is fed at the old size, and other events go through the normal handler (an `%exit` there ends the view). A pane whose `%output` arrives after the layout change is replayed instead of fed, because the daemon may have applied those bytes at the old size. A parked `%layout-change` from another client replays only new panes as well. Reseeding to a different window still replays every pane. Client-only: no wire or persistence change.
- **par-mux attach render mode patches status from notification payloads instead of re-querying** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/status.rs`, ENH-043): `%agent-state-changed` and `%agent-released` update the agent chips in place, `%window-renamed` updates the tab, and a set `%pane-title-changed` updates the pane's border label and the bar's title, none of them marking the status for a re-query. `%agent-telemetry-changed` no longer marks the status at all, since the bar never renders telemetry. A cleared `%pane-title-changed` still re-queries that pane (the fallback OSC title is not in the payload). The `list-commands` feature-token grammar widened from `[a-z-]+` to `[a-z0-9-]+` to carry `v1`; every existing token stays valid.
- **The core `Terminal` caps its bell queue and its device-query response buffer** (`crates/par-term-emu-core/src/terminal/event_broker.rs`, `crates/par-term-emu-core/src/terminal/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/mod.rs`, `docs/API_REFERENCE.md`, SEC-207). Both queues were unbounded: a pane printing BEL (`yes $'\a'`) raised the par-mux daemon's RSS from 11 MB to 599 MB, and a query flood (`ESC[6n`) grew `response_buffer` the same way. Pending bell events are now capped at `MAX_BELL_EVENTS` (1,024), with the oldest bell dropped first, and `bell_count()` still counts every bell. Replies generated by processed output are capped at `MAX_RESPONSE_BYTES` (64 KiB) pending, with the replies past the cap dropped. Bytes the embedder pushes itself (`push_response`, iTerm2 upload data) are never dropped. Embedders that drain every read (Python, the FFI, par-term, `PtySession`) see no difference; an embedder that never drains now loses the oldest bells and the newest replies instead of growing without bound. The attach client's mirror emulators (render panes and the passthrough shadow) now discard both queues on every feed, because the daemon's PTY session answers for the pane.
- **par-mux attach render mode: the per-pane border ring and its label honor `border-active-color` / `border-color`** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/renderer.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): with `pane-borders = true`, the focused pane's ring and border label took a hardcoded bright cyan + bold and unfocused rings a hardcoded dim, ignoring the `[client] border-active-color` / `border-color` options that already drove the shared dividers. One pair of options now drives dividers, rings, and labels: the focused ring/label takes `border-active-color` (still bold), unfocused rings/labels take `border-color`. Unset options keep the exact previous look. Pinned by `pane_border_ring_and_label_honor_border_colors` (red before the change) and `pane_border_ring_unset_colors_keep_cyan_and_dim`.
- **BREAKING (Rust API): par-mux sizing types carry the declared per-pane chrome** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/tree/`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/layout.rs`; card 01a11c5b, see the render-mode border-ring fix under Fixed). `MuxCommand::RefreshClient` gains `chrome: Option<PaneChrome>`; `MuxWindow` and `ClientView` gain `chrome: PaneChrome`; `MuxTree::set_client_view` takes a fifth `chrome: PaneChrome` argument. None of these types is `#[non_exhaustive]`, so exhaustive patterns and struct literals need the field. **Migration:** add `..` to patterns or `chrome: None` / `chrome: PaneChrome::default()` to literals, and pass `PaneChrome::default()` to `set_client_view` for today's full-rect sizing. New public API: `mux::layout::PaneChrome` (`interior`, `pty_size`, `merge`, `to_wire`/`parse_wire`) and `MuxTree::resize_window_with_chrome`. No known consumer: par-term references none of these.
- **BEHAVIOR CHANGE: plain `par-mux attach` renders** (`crates/par-mux/src/bin/par_mux/main.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/config.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`, `docs/MANUAL-PASS.md`): with neither `--mode` nor a `[client] mode` in the config file, attach runs the render pipeline (the full TUI); `mode = "passthrough"` in the config file still selects the byte pump, and `--mode` beats both. The resolution switched to render in the render-default entry below; the `--mode` help text, the `[client] mode` field doc, the MUX.md attach intro and config example, and MANUAL-PASS.md still said passthrough was the default and now match. Pinned by `attach_mode_defaults_to_render_and_honors_file_then_flag`.
- **Mux tree observer delivery is deferred past the tree lock** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/tree/`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/pane.rs`, `crates/par-term-emu-core/src/pty_session/io.rs`): resize-driven and start-dir-note observer events raised while a `SessionTree` method holds the tree mutex are now parked in `MuxTree::pending_observer_batches` and delivered by `take_observer_batches()` (new public method) after the lock drops — `dispatch_command`, reap, client teardown, and restore all drain it. Direct `MuxTree` callers (a program owning a bare `MuxTree`, not going through the daemon) no longer receive resize events inline: call `take_observer_batches()` where the old inline delivery was relied on. `PtySession` gains `#[doc(hidden)]` `resize_deferred`/`resize_with_pixels_deferred` behind the `mux` feature; the delivering `resize` wrappers are unchanged. The gone-start-dir note a spawn writes into its new pane (`spawn_and_wire`, under the tree lock) goes through the same path, so an observer a pane factory attaches in `create_pane` no longer runs under the tree mutex; `MuxPane::write_note` keeps its signature and now delivers after the terminal's write lock drops. The gone-cwd note restore (`MuxTree::from_persist_state`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/persist.rs`) writes into a restored pane follows suit: its observer events were delivered inline under that pane's terminal write lock and are now parked with the re-fit batches and delivered when restore drains them, before it returns. Delivery order across concurrent commands may interleave where the tree lock previously ordered it. Pinned by `layout_resize_observer_events_arrive_after_the_tree_lock_drops`, `a_direct_tree_resize_parks_observer_events_until_taken`, `start_dir_note_observer_events_arrive_after_the_tree_lock_drops`, and `restore_note_observer_events_arrive_after_the_terminal_lock_drops`; the core dac90e5 lock-discipline regression tests still pass.
- **The terminal core is now the `par-term-emu-core` workspace member** (`crates/par-term-emu-core/`, `Cargo.toml`, `src/lib.rs`, `cbindgen.toml`, ARC-007 option O2, phase 1): `terminal`, `grid`, `pty_session`, `graphics`, `screenshot`, `tmux_control`, `keyboard`, `macros`, `badge`, `coprocess`, `debug`, and the cell/color/cursor/mouse/zone/text/unicode utilities moved (by `git mv`, token-identical apart from the cross-boundary edits listed in the member's `DESIGN.md`) into a new crate that the root re-exports module by module. **No public path changes**: every `par_term_emu_core_rust::…` path, the `debug_*!` macros, the Python `_native` module and its stub, and `include/terminal_core.h` are unchanged. The member without features is the headless (`sim`) profile; root features `screenshot`, `pty_session`, `serde`, `macro-yaml`, `mux`, `ffi`, `python`/`python-test` forward to it. It versions in lockstep with the root (`make core-version-check`, in `checkall`/`release-check`/CI) and must be published to crates.io before the root crate (publish-workflow step still to add before the next release). Embedders that name only `par-term-emu-core-rust` need no change; pure-Rust embedders may depend on `par-term-emu-core` directly for the smaller build.
- **The multiplexer is now the `par-mux` workspace member** (`crates/par-mux/`, `Cargo.toml`, `src/lib.rs`, ARC-007 option O2, phase 2): `mux` (incl. `mux::attach`), the `par-mux` binary, and the eleven mux integration suites moved by `git mv` into a new crate that the root re-exports as `par_term_emu_core_rust::mux` behind its `mux` feature, so every `par_term_emu_core_rust::mux::…` path is unchanged. Root `mux-bin`/`attach` forward to the member's features; par-mux versions in lockstep with the root and both pins are exact (`make core-version-check` now gates root → core, root → par-mux, and par-mux → core). `mux::build_stamp()` comes from the member's own build script. The 31 direct `log::` call sites in the mux tree now log under `par_mux::mux::…` instead of `par_term_emu_core_rust::mux::…` (only matters to a `RUST_LOG` filter naming the old module path). Publish order is derive → `par-term-emu-core` → `par-mux` → root.
- **BREAKING (install): `cargo install par-term-emu-core-rust --bin par-mux` no longer works** — the binary lives in the `par-mux` crate. Install it with `cargo install par-mux --features mux-bin,attach` (from source: `cargo install --path crates/par-mux --features mux-bin,attach`; build: `cargo build -p par-mux --bin par-mux --features mux-bin`). The GitHub Release `par-mux` archives are unaffected.
- **Each grid's evicted-zone buffer is capped at 10,000 zones** (`crates/par-term-emu-core/src/grid/zone.rs`): zones that scroll off the scrollback floor wait in a per-grid buffer until they become `ZoneScrolledOut` events, and nothing bounded that buffer. A grid that is never drained could grow it without limit, for example one embedded directly with no owning `Terminal`. `MAX_EVICTED_ZONES` (10,000, matching `MAX_TERMINAL_EVENTS`) now bounds it, dropping the oldest evicted zones first, so at most 10,000 `ZoneScrolledOut` events surface from one backlog. Pinned by `test_evicted_zone_buffer_is_capped_fifo`.
- **BREAKING (Rust API): `TmuxNotification::Continue` now carries the pane id** (`crates/par-term-emu-core/src/tmux_control.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/emit.rs`, `src/python_bindings/types/notification.rs`): tmux's `%continue %N` names the pane whose output resumes, and the parser dropped it. `Continue` is now `Continue { pane_id: String }`, matching `Pause { pane_id }`. Exhaustive matches over the (non-`#[non_exhaustive]`) enum and constructions need the new field: `Continue` becomes `Continue { .. }` or `Continue { pane_id }`. Python `TmuxNotification.pane_id` is now set for `continue` notifications (it was `None`); the type string is unchanged. Known consumer: par-term's `parser_bridge.rs` matches `CoreNotification::Continue` and needs the same one-token update when it adopts this version.
- **par-mux `new-workspace` names its auto-created first tab `1`** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/tree/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/tree/lifecycle.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/`, `docs/MUX.md`): the first window used to inherit the workspace's name, so a workspace `dev` showed a tab also called `dev`. The session keeps the workspace's name; only the window is named `1`. `new-session` is unchanged (its first window still takes the session name). Pinned by the first-window assertion in `workspace_commands_dispatch_and_broadcast`.
- **par-mux attach render mode: the tab strip takes herdr's tab-bar look** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/tabs.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/`, `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_attach.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): every tab is now a solid block with two columns of padding either side of its name (was one), adjacent blocks sit exactly one column apart (was none), and the whole row carries a dark navy bar background. Colors are truecolor constants in one place in `tabs.rs`, measured from the reference rendering: bar `rgb(23,23,33)`, inactive block `rgb(43,44,60)` with muted text `rgb(97,101,123)` (was DIM text with no fill), active block `rgb(126,171,249)` with dark bold text (was indexed cyan), and the `…` markers and the right-pinned ` + ` paint muted on the bar. The three-stage truncation, the always-visible active tab, the side panel's lead offset, and the ` + ` reservation are unchanged; the stage-2 and stage-3 budgets now count the gaps and each marker's trailing gap. Hit-testing runs through the same layout: a click anywhere on a block, pads included, hits that tab, and gap and marker columns hit nothing. Every strip column/width pin moved to the new geometry (for example the second of `main`/`vim`/`build` now spans cols 9..16 with col 8 a gap), and new pins cover the gap and marker misses (`hit_test_misses_markers_and_their_gaps`) and the palette (`paint_draws_herdr_blocks_on_the_bar`).
- **par-mux client lifecycle notifications fire only for size-reporting clients** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/server/`, `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_daemon.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): `%client-attached` now goes out when a registered client first reports a size (`refresh-client -C`) instead of at registration, and `%client-left` is emitted only for clients that were announced. Every one-shot `par-mux <cmd>` invocation registers as a client on its first control command, so under the registration-time rule each poll by an agent broadcast an attached/left pair to every attached client and forced a status refresh. A client that never reports a size (a one-shot command, a hook-only connection) is now silent in both directions. The joining client still never hears its own attach, and teardown is unchanged (clean disconnect, socket EOF and eviction all still emit for announced clients). Pinned by `a_client_that_never_reports_a_size_causes_no_lifecycle_lines` and the silence assertion added to `client_attach_and_drop_broadcast_lifecycle_lines`.
- **BEHAVIOR CHANGE: render mode is the default `par-mux attach` pipeline; an unknown `--mode` flag value now fails loudly** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/bin/par_mux/main.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/config.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): attach without `--mode` opens the pane renderer (passthrough is the opt-out, `--mode passthrough`, and stays what the test suite pins); the config-file `mode` tier resolves the same default. A typo'd flag value (`--mode rendered`) used to fall back to passthrough silently — it now exits 2 naming the valid modes; an unknown FILE value warns on stderr and uses the default. `AttachOptions.mode` is `Option<AttachMode>` so the flag tier is distinguishable from the default. Pinned by `attach_mode_flag_parses_and_validates` (`crates/par-mux/src/bin/par_mux/main.rs`) and the flipped `AttachMode::default`.
- **BEHAVIOR CHANGE: the passthrough attach client no longer uses the host alternate screen** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_attach.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the client entered the host alt screen once at setup, so a pane app's own `1049h`/`1049l` pair (htop, vim) became the SECOND entry — its leave popped the host back to its main screen. The app-exit screen replaced the pane (the manual-pass report: after exiting htop the pre-attach terminal content showed), every later client draw (the status bar) painted the host main and stayed after detach, and the bar under the app was already lost. The client now clears the screen at attach (`ESC[2J ESC[H`), re-clears on every pane-show replay (switches never mix panes), and clears again on detach; pane apps own the alt screen naturally, their rmcup returns to the pane's screen, and the status row's DECSTBM reserve keeps it safe from app scrolling. tmux control mode never enters the host alt either. Pinned by the rewritten `detach_restores_the_terminal_region` (clear at setup and detach, NO `1049h` ever).
- **BEHAVIOR CHANGE: attached clients no longer hold an empty par-mux daemon** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/server/`, `docs/MUX.md`): exit-when-empty previously required zero CLIENTS and an empty tree, so a daemon whose last session died under an attached client lingered forever — the client sat on a frozen, dead tree with no `%exit` (the manual-pass report: kill the last pane, the daemon never exits). The empty check is now the tree alone (zero sessions, or only dead panes); the last session dying under a client ends the server after the 5 s grace exactly as tmux's `exit-empty` does, and the client receives the broadcast `%exit` and detaches. `[daemon] exit-empty = false` (live via `reload-config`) still holds the daemon indefinitely, client or not. Pinned by `a_connected_client_receives_exit_when_the_tree_empties` and `exit_empty_false_keeps_an_empty_persisting_server_alive` (now with an attached client) in `crates/par-mux/src/mux/server/`.
- **BEHAVIOR CHANGE: a par-mux daemon pane whose child exits is now AUTO-REMOVED by default** (`[daemon] remain-on-exit`, default flipped from the always-on hold to off; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/config.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/server/`, `docs/MUX.md`): previously a pane whose process exited on its own was always HELD dead (frozen screen, `(exited N)` cue, `respawn-pane` restart) and nothing auto-closed. With the new default, the death removes the pane at the next reaper pass (within 250 ms) through the kill-pane cascade — a window whose last pane died closes (`%window-close`), an emptied session (and workspace) goes with it, and the daemon exits-when-empty once no clients remain, exactly as if the pane had been `kill-pane`d. `respawn-pane` on a removed pane answers "no such pane". Set `[daemon] remain-on-exit = true` in the config file (live via `reload-config`) to restore the old hold behavior — including the attach client's `(exited N — C-b r respawns)` cue, which only appears for held panes.
- **par-mux attach: the status refresh is bounded and batched** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/status.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/`, ARC-125): the refresh deduplicated its queries (one `list-workspaces` reply feeds both the status row and the side panel; pane titles re-query only when stale on `%pane-title-changed` or an OSC title change), and every status query runs under a 500 ms timeout, keeping the previous view and marking status dirty for the next tick on timeout — previously a wedged daemon could block the single UI thread up to 10 s per query. The at-most-6-round-trips bound holds once pane titles are known; a cold first refresh costs one `pane-title` per unseen pane.
- **Scrollback storage: one boxed row per line instead of one flat growing `Vec<Cell>`** (`crates/par-term-emu-core/src/grid/mod.rs`, `crates/par-term-emu-core/src/grid/scroll.rs`, `crates/par-term-emu-core/src/grid/erase.rs`, `crates/par-term-emu-core/src/grid/snapshot.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/persist.rs`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/ADVANCED_FEATURES.md`, `docs/API_REFERENCE.md`): the scrollback buffer was a single flat `Vec<Cell>` in row-major order behind a rotated ring head — a 10k-line × 330-col scrollback held one ~183 MB allocation (with a ~2× doubling transient while growing) and every width resize rebuilt the whole buffer cell by cell. Storage is now `Vec<Box<[Cell]>>` (one small allocation per retained line at its own width) with a plain oldest-first queue; eviction drops one small allocation, and **resize no longer touches scrollback at all** — width changes reflow only the visible screen, stored lines keep the width they scrolled off at, and the cost of a resize is bounded by the viewport rows. Behavior notes: (1) scrollback lines are no longer re-wrapped on width change — renderers were already padding short lines (`ffi.rs copy_row`, mux attach renderer, par-term `push_line_from_slice`), so history renders correctly, it just keeps its original line shapes; (2) `GridSnapshot.scrollback_cells` keeps its flat one-line-per-`cols` payload for byte-compatible mux persistence round-trips, but lines are now always stored oldest-first — the `scrollback_start` field is gone (it serialized as `0` in every real snapshot, so old state files restore correctly and no `FORMAT_VERSION` bump is required); (3) `Grid::scrollback_line`/`row`/`row_mut` signatures are unchanged. Pinned by `scrollback_storage_is_per_line_rows` and `resize_with_large_scrollback_preserves_row_allocations` (asserts per-row allocation pointer identity survives both resize paths), plus `scroll_eviction_drops_one_row_not_a_buffer_rebuild` and `snapshot_round_trips_per_line_storage` (`crates/par-term-emu-core/src/grid/tests.rs`).
- **par-mux attach render mode: resize mode hides the host cursor** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/`, ARC-127): entering the sticky resize mode now hides the cursor with the other modals (the `Modal` enum's `hides_cursor` covers prompt, menu, help, picker and resize), and only one modal can be open at a time — opening any modal from the scroll viewport exits the viewport.
- **`Cell` slimmed from 56 to 40 bytes** (`crates/par-term-emu-core/src/cell.rs`): fg/bg/underline colors are packed into `u32` words (2-bit variant tag + compact payload: named = 4-bit index, indexed = 8-bit, RGB = 24-bit; the underline `Option` shares its top bit as a presence flag) and the combining-mark store moved from an inline `SmallVec<[char; 4]>` (24 bytes in every cell) to a null-optimized `Option<Arc<Vec<char>>>` (8 bytes; cells with combining marks now share the mark buffer on clone instead of deep-copying it). All `Cell` accessor signatures and the public `Color`/`CellFlags` types are unchanged, so the rlib API stays semver-minor — the par-term frontend compiles against the new core with only a version-pin bump. Scrollback memory for a 10k-line × 80-col buffer drops from ~46MB of `Cell` storage to ~32MB (56B → 40B per cell), with combining-mark sequences paying one small heap allocation each.
- **`Cell`'s serialized shape is unchanged, but is now produced by manual serde impls** (`crates/par-term-emu-core/src/cell.rs`): the derive on the struct was replaced by an `into`/`from` wire-format mirror (`CellSerde`) so packing does not alter the JSON — mux persistence files and replay snapshots stay byte-compatible in both directions with 0.58.0 output (verified field-for-field against the pre-change derive). Treat any hand-written deserializer that matched the old derive output as still-valid; none needed.
- **par-mux workspaces: a first-class level above sessions** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/tree/`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/server/`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/persist.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`). The hierarchy is now daemon > workspace > session > window > pane. A session belongs to exactly one workspace for its life; each workspace tracks its own active session, and the daemon tracks an active workspace. New commands: `new-workspace [-n name]` (creates and selects, replying the `+N` id), `list-workspaces` (`+N: name` lines, the active one suffixed ` active`), `select-workspace -t <workspace>` (resumes that workspace's previously active session), `rename-workspace -t <workspace> <name>`, and `kill-workspace -t <workspace>` (kills every session, window, and pane in it). `new-session` gains `-t <workspace>`; a bare `new-session` lands in the active workspace, lazily creating a default one named `main`. `list-sessions` gains `-t <workspace>` and its reply shape is now workspace-aware: each line is `+W: wname: $N: name` (bare = every workspace). Workspaces use the `+` id sigil. A workspace dies when its last session dies; exit-when-empty is unchanged in substance — empty means no sessions across all workspaces, so an empty workspace never keeps the daemon alive. A new argument-less `%workspaces-changed` broadcast (the `%sessions-changed` convention) fires on workspace add, kill, rename, select, and on any session cascade that removes an emptied workspace; `list-commands` carries the new rows with the `workspace` feature token on `new-session`/`list-sessions`. Persistence: `FORMAT_VERSION` is now **3** — the save carries workspaces, their membership, and the active pointers, plus a fourth id counter. NOT backward compatible by design (workspaces are first-class, no migration): pre-workspace (v2) state files are quarantined and the daemon starts fresh. Pinned by the workspace tree tests (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/tree/tests.rs`), dispatch/broadcast tests (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/server/`), persistence round-trip and v2-quarantine tests (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/persist.rs`), and the live-daemon tests `workspace_commands_over_the_wire` (`crates/par-mux/tests/mux_daemon.rs`) and `workspaces_survive_a_restart` (`crates/par-mux/tests/mux_restart.rs`).

### Deprecated
- **Deprecated: `par_mux::mux::persist::save_to_with_origin`** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/persist.rs`, QA-237): it runs the whole save, fsync included, while the caller holds the tree, so a caller keeping the tree behind a mutex stalls every command for the disk write. Use `save_off_lock` (ARC-119), which captures under the lock and writes off it. The function stays public and unchanged since v0.58.0 exposed it; nothing in the workspace calls it.

### Fixed
- **A long SGR mouse report no longer overflows `u32`** (ENH-046; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/input.rs`): `ESC[<` followed by eleven or more digits multiplied the field accumulators into an overflow panic under debug assertions and a silent wrap in release. Digit accumulation saturates now; the existing downstream clamps bound the field. Pinned by `sgr_mouse_huge_fields_saturate_instead_of_overflowing`.
- **par-mux attach passthrough mode: a session/window/workspace switch now moves the daemon's displayed session** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/navigate.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/actions.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/targets.rs`, ARC-124; the passthrough half of card 01a11bd1). Passthrough landed with a bare `select-window`, which never moves the daemon's persisted displayed-session pointer, so after a daemon restart the next no-target attach showed the session passthrough had left. It also ignored a refused select and moved its local view anyway. Passthrough now lands the way render mode does (`switch-client`, falling back to `select-window`) and changes its local window/session only after the daemon accepts. Both modes now share one set of navigation helpers. Pinned by `passthrough_session_landing_survives_a_restart_for_the_next_attach` (red before the change) and `passthrough_refused_landing_leaves_local_state_put`.
- **par-mux attach render mode honors `--prefix`** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/mod.rs`, ARC-123). Render mode, the default, seeded the detach prefix at `C-b` and ignored `--prefix`, so `par-mux attach --prefix C-a` behaved as `C-b`. Both attach modes now consume one resolution (`resolve_client`: CLI over the config file over the built-in defaults), made once before either mode touches the terminal, so a malformed chord reports the same `invalid --prefix …` error in either mode and no longer leaves the host in the alternate screen. Pinned by `render_mode_prefix_flag_rebinds_the_detach_chord` (red before the change) and the `resolve_client_*` unit tests.
- **par-mux attach passthrough mode: output after the pane scrolls no longer collapses onto the status row** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/status_row.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/pump.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`; card 01a11d62). Under the `python -m rich` demo, most of the colored paragraphs were missing from both the host screen and its scrollback. No bytes were lost: a byte-exact diff of the pane's PTY reads against the client's host writes matched (151,763 bytes, fast and slow hosts). The cause was cursor placement. The pane's PTY is the host grid minus the status row, but the passthrough client's shadow emulator was sized to the full grid, so once the pane scrolled the shadow tracked its cursor one row too low, on the status row. Every status draw closes by moving the host cursor to the tracked cell, which put it below the `1;rows-1` scroll region, where line feeds do not scroll. Each later line then overwrote the bottom row and never reached the host's scrollback (199 of 202 draws in the rich capture closed on row 24). The shadow emulator, its per-draw re-fit, and the `refresh-client -C` size report now share one pane-grid helper, `pane_grid`. Pinned red-to-green by `status_draw_keeps_the_cursor_inside_the_content_region_after_a_scroll` (headless) and `passthrough_output_after_a_scroll_reaches_the_host_scrollback` (40 printed lines, all missing from the host's screen and scrollback before the fix). The transport is guarded by the byte-exact `passthrough_forwards_every_byte_of_a_rich_like_flood` and `passthrough_forwards_every_byte_to_a_slow_host`, which pass before and after. `status_draw_tracks_the_cursor_under_output_flood` now also bounds the placement row.
- **par-mux attach render mode: TUIs no longer lose their bottom row and right column under the pane-border ring** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/layout.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/client.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/tree/`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/`, `docs/MUX.md`; card 01a11c5b). The render client paints each pane's content inset by the border ring (`pane-borders`, on by default through the herdr style), the `pane-gaps` band, and the `scrollbar-gutter` column, but its size report told the daemon only the full content grid. The daemon sized every pane's PTY to its full rect, so a full-screen program's last rows and columns fell in the cropped band. The client now declares its per-pane chrome on the size report: `refresh-client -t <pane> -C WxH -I border=B,gap=N,gutter=G`, advertised by the new `chrome` feature token on `refresh-client`'s `list-commands` row. The daemon reserves it (tmux's reserve-border-lines model): pane rects and the layout string keep their full geometry, and each pane's PTY is the rect less the declared chrome, so `stty size` in a bordered single pane on an 80x24 host now reads `20 78` (was `22 80`). A pane too small to carry a ring (2x2 or smaller after the gap) keeps its full rect, matching the paint. Several viewers reserve the union of their declarations. A chrome-only change (the border-cycle chord, a config reload) re-declares and re-fits. One computation, `PaneChrome::interior`, drives both the daemon's PTY sizing and the client's paint/mouse/cursor inset, so they cannot drift. **Compatibility:** a report without `-I` declares no chrome, so older clients get exactly the previous full-rect sizing. The client sends `-I` only when the daemon advertises `chrome`, so a new client against an older daemon also keeps the old behavior (with the crop). `pane-info`/`list-panes` sizes for a bordered render view now report the interior. Pinned red-to-green by `render_mode_border_ring_keeps_the_tui_last_row_visible`, plus the division tests (`declared_ring_sizes_stacked_panes_to_the_interior`, `…side_by_side…`, `declared_gaps_and_gutter_compound_with_the_ring`, `a_pane_too_small_for_the_ring_keeps_its_full_rect`, `a_chrome_only_report_refits_and_undeclaring_restores_full_rects`, `viewers_chrome_merges_to_the_union`, `a_zoomed_pane_reserves_the_chrome_too`) and the invariant `content_view_area_equals_the_pty_grid_in_every_combination`.
- **par-mux attach render mode: a shell exit that closes the shown tab no longer disconnects every attached client** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/mod.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`; card 01a11c3a). Under `remain-on-exit = false` (the default), Ctrl+D in a window's only pane auto-removes the pane. The daemon closes the window (`%pane-exited`, then `%window-close`) and the session keeps its other windows. Every render client showing that window then re-queried status and could not find the window in any session. That `SessionGone` result ended the view, so each client exited 0 while the session survived. The pump now ends the view only when the session itself is gone. When just the window is gone, the client lands on the session's active window (tmux semantics). The daemon's reap broadcast was already correct and is unchanged. Pinned red-to-green by `render_mode_ctrl_d_closing_a_tab_keeps_both_clients_in_the_session`. `render_mode_ctrl_d_on_the_last_pane_ends_both_clients` keeps the session-gone teardown, and `render_mode_ctrl_d_in_a_pane_keeps_both_clients_on_the_survivors` covers a pane exiting inside a window that survives (that case already passed).
- **par-mux attach render mode: a stale displayed session no longer shows a single pane on the first attach, reverts the other client, or hides its new tabs** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/sessions.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/tree/lifecycle.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/mod.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`; card 01a11bd1). Three landing rules disagreed inside a multi-session workspace: a no-target attach took the globally newest session id, a workspace landing took the workspace's first session, and the daemon's follow broadcasts named the workspace's active session. Landing on a sibling session only sent `select-window`, which is silent for a background session, so the daemon's displayed session (persisted, and read by every no-target attach) kept naming the session last displayed. After a restore the first attach showed that session's single pane, a workspace switch away and back fixed only the client that did it, and the follow it triggered moved the other client back. The bug is in the pointer, not the restore: it reproduces on a fresh tree, and a restart only serves the stale pointer back. The new `switch-client [-t <session|window|pane>]` command writes the pointer: with a target it makes that session the displayed one and its workspace active (a window/pane target also selects the window), persists, and broadcasts `%client-session-changed` (plus `%workspaces-changed` when the workspace moved) so other render clients follow. A target inside the already-displayed session behaves exactly like `select-window`, so the `%session-window-changed`/`%window-pane-changed` tab-switch cues that par-term and other control-mode consumers track still fire for tab switches made in a render client. Bare, it answers the displayed `$N`, read-only. Flags other than `-t` are rejected. The render client lands every user-initiated switch (window/session/workspace switches, tab and picker clicks, new-window landings, the closed-tab survivor) through `switch-client -t <window>`. Follow re-seeds never write it. The no-target seed and workspace landings resolve the displayed session, falling back to the old rules against a daemon without the command. Separately, render and passthrough clients now refresh their tab roster on `%window-add`/`%window-close`: `new-window` sends no `%sessions-changed`, so a tab another client opened never appeared. Pinned red-to-green by `render_mode_no_target_attach_lands_on_the_displayed_session`, `render_mode_session_landing_moves_the_display_and_the_other_client_follows`, `render_mode_landing_survives_a_restart_for_the_next_attach`, `render_mode_new_window_in_another_client_reaches_this_client`, and `render_mode_window_add_elsewhere_refreshes_the_tab_strip`, plus `switch_client_queries_and_moves_the_displayed_session` and `switch_client_mutates_only_with_a_target`.
- **`ZoneScrolledOut` events now reach observers and filtered polls** (`crates/par-term-emu-core/src/terminal/mod.rs`): the events were created inside `poll_events()` from the grids' evicted-zone buffers and drained in the same call, so `TerminalObserver::on_zone_event` never saw them and `poll_subscribed_events()` with a filter set never returned them. Evicted zones are now pushed onto the event queue at the end of `process()`/`process_deferred()`, before observer dispatch, and before a filtered `poll_subscribed_events()`. `Terminal::apply_action()`/`apply_actions()` (`crates/par-term-emu-core/src/terminal/action.rs`), which drive the parser without `process()`, now run the same tail, so observers receive the events they queue (zone evictions included) before the call returns. Previously those events waited for the next `process()`. `Terminal::resize()` (and `ptec_terminal_resize`) runs the same tail for zones a narrowing reflow pushes out of scrollback; `PtySession::resize()`/`resize_with_pixels()` deliver them after dropping the terminal write guard, as the PTY reader thread does. `poll_events()` still flushes too, for zones evicted by a `Grid` mutated directly. Pinned by `test_zone_scrolled_out_reaches_observers_via_process`, `..._via_process_deferred`, `..._via_apply_actions`, `..._via_apply_action`, `..._via_resize`, `test_resize_eviction_reaches_observers_without_write_lock`, and `test_zone_scrolled_out_reaches_filtered_poll`.
- **par-mux `paste-buffer -t <missing pane>` with an empty buffer now names the pane** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/dispatch/`): the empty-buffer check ran before the pane lookup, so a typo'd target answered `no buffers` and hid the real mistake. The pane is checked first. Pinned by `paste_buffer_reports_the_bad_target_before_the_empty_buffer`.
- **par-mux attach render mode: switching to a workspace with fewer tabs left the old tabs on the strip** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/tabs.rs`): `TabStrip::invalidate()` reset its diff baseline to a BLANK row, and the diff emits only cells that differ from the baseline, so the new strip's blank cells were never written. The reseed, resize, and sidebar-toggle paths invalidate without clearing host row 0, so the previous workspace's extra tabs stayed on screen wherever the new strip was blank. The invalidated baseline is now filled with a sentinel glyph no paint writes, so the next diff repaints every column, blanks included. `StatusRow::invalidate` does not share the flaw: its padding is styled REVERSED, so every column already differs from a blank baseline. Pinned red-to-green by `invalidate_repaints_every_column_including_blanks`.
- **par-mux attach render mode: every client's ` Z ` zoom cue follows the daemon's per-window zoom truth** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/`, `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_daemon.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the status row's ` Z ` cue was per-client state flipped only by the client's own zoom chord, so a second attached client never showed another client's `resize-pane -Z` (and a select-away unzoom left a stale cue on the zooming client until its next chord). The daemon already broadcasts zoom truth per window as the `Z` raw flag of `%layout-change` from every site that changes it (`resize-pane -Z` both ways, `select-pane` away, every layout mutation) — no new notification. The render client now sets the cue from that flag for the shown window in its event handler (outside the geometry guard, so a one-pane window's same-rect zoom still flips it), and in the seed, reseed, and host-resize paths that read the triple directly; another window's zoom never touches it, the chord still flips it locally for instant feedback, and passthrough keeps chrome-dirtying on `%layout-change`. Pinned red-to-green by `layout_change_flags_drive_the_zoom_cue_for_the_shown_window_only`, and over the wire by the two-client `a_zoom_and_its_select_away_unzoom_reach_the_other_client`.
- **par-mux attach render mode round 7: the context menu's clicks were one panel width left of its items** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/`, `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_attach.rs`): the menu modal centers over the HOST width (the pane layout already carries the strip offset), but its mouse guard mapped clicks through the panel's strip rebase — with the panel up every action row was unclickable (the manual-pass report; the same defect class as round 5's unclickable ` + `). The guard takes the raw host column like the picker's (red-to-green pinned by `panel_up_menu_click_maps_raw_host_columns` and the panel-up end-to-end tab-menu test), and the strip's active-tab block is pinned never to bleed into a neighboring tab's columns with or without the lead segment (`active_block_never_bleeds_into_other_tabs`).
- **par-mux `attach` render mode mis-decoded bracketed paste as keystrokes** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/input.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`; feature `attach`): the render client's `InputParser` had no bracketed-paste handling — a host left in DECSET 2004 mode by an earlier app sends `ESC[200~ … ESC[201~` around every paste, and the parser silently dropped both markers while SCANNING THE BODY as keystrokes: embedded `C-b` fed the prefix chord scanner, embedded escape sequences decoded as key events (pasting shell text containing `ESC[3~` pressed Delete in the pane), and a paste split so its leading ESC arrived as a lone one-byte read fragmented further. The parser now treats a `ESC[200~`-opened paste as an opaque byte run: the body passes through verbatim as plain bytes (text the user meant to insert, not keystrokes to decode), the markers are consumed, an unterminated paste stops holding the input stream after 1 MiB (the OSC 11 give-up shape, resized), and paste state persists across feeds so a paste split over any read boundary reassembles untouched. Passthrough mode never parsed stdin and was never affected. Pinned by `paste_pair_decodes_to_one_opaque_run`, `paste_body_split_across_feeds_reassembles_untouched`, `byte_at_a_time_decode_follows_the_design`, `paste_marker_splits_at_every_byte_boundary`, `burst_interleaves_paste_chord_and_plain_runs`, and `unterminated_paste_flushes_at_the_cap` (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/input.rs`).
- **`par-mux --restart` can no longer restore nothing** (intermittent "fresh daemon came up empty"; `crates/par-mux/src/mux/server/`, `crates/par-mux/src/bin/par_mux/main.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/persist.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the old daemon unlinked its socket inside the accept-loop teardown, BEFORE `run_persisting`'s final state save — so `--stop`/`--restart`'s wait for the socket to stop accepting could return while the save was still in flight, and the fresh daemon's restore read a missing or stale state file. The socket is now released only after the final save lands (`MuxServer::run_persisting` unlinks via the new `release_socket_path` after `save_off_lock`; embedded `run()` unlinks immediately after its loop), making the documented handoff contract ("the state save the stop just completed is what it restores") actually enforced. A restart whose restore will be empty is now LOUD on both sides: the pre-fork peek (`persist::peek_state`, a read-only mirror of `load_or_quarantine`'s conclusions including the last-good fallback) tells the caller on the terminal whether the fresh daemon will restore nothing — and why (no saved state / previous daemon saved an empty tree / unreadable state), and the detached fresh daemon's stderr is routed to `<state file>.log` beside the state file (created `0600`, appended across restarts, parent dir auto-created) instead of `/dev/null`, so a post-fork startup failure (bind error, restore failure, exit-when-empty) leaves evidence on disk; the empty restore itself logs `restored tree is empty; daemon exiting (exit-when-empty)` in that log, and every exit-when-empty logs `tree empty; daemon exiting (exit-when-empty)`. On Windows, where restart serves in-process with the caller's stderr intact, only the pre-fork announcements and the exit-when-empty log line apply. Pinned by `cli_restart_restores_a_populated_tree_five_trials` (five real-daemon restarts), `cli_restart_after_emptying_announces_the_empty_restore` (both loud lines asserted), and `cli_restart_surfaces_a_fresh_daemon_startup_failure` (a post-fork bind failure is readable in the daemon log) in `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_restart.rs`.
- **par-mux `send-keys -l` multi-token behavior documented and pinned, not changed — it already matches real tmux** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/`, `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_send_keys.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the card reported `send-keys -t %0 -l echo LEFT` typing `echoLEFT` as a bug, but a probe against real tmux 3.7c shows tmux does the same (`send-keys -t 0 -l echo LEFT` types `echoLEFT`; quoted `-l 'echo LEFT'` types `echo LEFT`; default mode `abc def` types `abcdef`). Tokens join with NOTHING in every mode. par-mux already implements this exactly; the card closes on documentation instead of a code change: MUX.md's `send-keys` row and bullet now state the measured joining rule with the quoted-run and `-H` hex forms as the lossless paths, and the contract is pinned by `send_keys_literal_tokens_join_with_nothing_like_tmux` (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/command/`) and the live-daemon tests `literal_multi_token_payload_drops_the_separator_like_tmux` / `quoted_literal_run_keeps_its_space_like_tmux` (`crates/par-mux/tests/mux_send_keys.rs`).
- **par-mux `attach` passthrough raced its status draw against pane output** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_attach.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`; feature `attach`): the passthrough status draw wrapped itself in ESC7/ESC8 (cursor save/restore) and relied on the restore for the post-draw cursor position. Pane output flowing while the draw ran broke that: a pane scroll landing between save and restore left the restored position one line off, and every later output painted over the wrong row (observed on Terminal.app as a fresh prompt overwriting the middle of the previous line). The passthrough session now keeps a shadow emulator of the pane (the render mode's `PaneEmulator`, seeded by the `refresh-client` replay in `resync()` and fed every `%output` chunk in `handle_event`, re-fit on host resize), and the draw keeps the ESC7/ESC8 wrap (which still protects against output interleaved WITHIN the draw) but CLOSES with an absolute CUP at the emulator's tracked cell (`ESC[<row>;<col>H`) — a scroll landing between the draw and the placement cannot make a fresh absolute position wrong. The DECSTBM reserve semantics are unchanged (the content region stays reserved after the draw). Over a held-dead pane the tracked cell is the frozen screen's cell, so placement still targets the right spot; on resize the emulator re-fits and its clamped cursor is what placement reads. Pinned headless by `status_draw_places_the_cursor_at_the_tracked_cell` and `status_draw_tracks_the_frozen_cell_when_the_pane_is_dead` (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/mod.rs`: the tracked-cell CUP closes every draw, the wrap and DECSTBM reserve survive, the dead pane's frozen cell is placed), and PTY-level by `status_draw_tracks_the_cursor_under_output_flood` (`crates/par-mux/tests/mux_attach.rs`, unix: a scrolling flood runs while the client's status draws land, every captured draw block closes with its placement CUP — a bare-ESC8 close is the raced shape — and an echo typed after the flood round-trips intact).
- **par-mux attach: the host terminal is restored when the render client errors out** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/mod.rs`, ARC-129): alternate screen and mouse capture are undone by a RAII guard declared after the raw-mode guard, so a panic or a config error mid-setup can no longer leave the host in the alternate screen with mouse reporting on; the manual end-of-run restore is gone.
- **par-mux attach render mode: three sidebar/label defects from the manual pass** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/`, `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_attach.rs`): toggling the side panel now refits the grid through the pump's `resize_to`, whose `repaint_all` erases the region the old layout vacated — before, only changed cells repainted, so ghost pane contents stayed at the pre-toggle positions beside a dead margin the width of the strip (the chord parks a `pending_grid_refit`; the pump performs it and re-queries the strip's sections, which the refit's renderer reconstruction drops). Border labels now paint the daemon's `pane-title` value — the user `-T` label (prefix `$`) when set, else the OSC title — re-queried on seed and every throttled status refresh; before, the painter read the shell's OSC title only, so user labels never showed (pinned by `render_mode_border_label_paints_the_user_title`, which sets labels daemon-side and asserts they paint in the herdr style). And only the ACTIVE workspace's sidebar row carries the full-width inverted block; the shown window's row brightens instead (two blocks read as two actives).
- **par-mux `attach` passthrough printed the help dump on the cursor's line and let later keystrokes paint over it** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/mod.rs`): the prefix-`?` dump started at the cursor's current position — the first category header landed on the prompt row — and the pane's cursor never advanced, so every later keystroke mixed into the help text. The dump now leads with a blank line, bolds the category headers (SGR 1, the render modal's accent rows), and advances the pane past the text with empty Enters (~2 rows each, half the dump's row count) so the shell lands below the help; the text stays in the pane's scrollback. Pinned by `passthrough_help_dump_leads_with_a_blank_line_and_bolds_headers` and `help_chord_advances_the_pane_past_the_dump` (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/mod.rs`).
- **par-mux `attach` passthrough left switched-to windows at their restored size** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/mod.rs`): the handshake's target-less `refresh-client -C` sizes only the newest session's active window, and pane switches sent select + replay without a size report — a window restored at 80x24 stayed 80x24 in a tall host, and its apps ran at the stale height (the manual-pass htop report; render mode's switch path always reported). Passthrough `resync` now size-reports the target pane's window at the content region (`cols x rows-1`, the status row stays reserved) before every replay. Pinned by `resync_size_reports_the_target_window_before_the_replay` (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/mod.rs`).
- **Styled exports replayed default-colored text through the host's palette** (`crates/par-term-emu-core/src/grid/export.rs`, `crates/par-term-emu-core/src/grid/tests.rs`, `crates/par-term-emu-core/src/terminal/tests/terminal_tests.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/server/`, `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_reattach.rs`): every styled export (the attach replay's `export_screen_restore_sequence`, `capture-pane -e`, `export_styled_buffer`) emitted the DEFAULT colors as explicit palette entries (`37` fg white / `40` bg black — `Cell::default()`'s values) in each SGR run, so a reattached client's host painted visible grey bands around replayed prompt rows whose true appearance was the pane host's own default background (palette-0 on a themed host is not black). The exporter now omits fg `Named(White)` and bg `Named(Black)` — the default sentinels — from every SGR run, so replays paint default-colored text with the host's own defaults, exactly as the live pane did. Documented conflation: an explicit `ESC[37m`/`ESC[40m` exports as default too (the cell model cannot distinguish them). Pinned by `styled_export_omits_default_colors` (`crates/par-term-emu-core/src/terminal/tests/terminal_tests.rs`), the updated `test_styled_exports_golden_bytes` (`crates/par-term-emu-core/src/grid/tests.rs`), and the updated `-e` capture and reattach assertions.
- **par-mux `attach` passthrough attached with the cursor one row below the prompt** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/mod.rs`): the `resync()` seed appended a trailing newline to the daemon's screen-restore byte stream before feeding the shadow emulator — but the stream ends with the pane's real cursor CUP, so the shadow (and every status draw's absolute placement with it) parked one row low until the shell's next repaint. The feed is now the verbatim stream. Pinned by `resync_feeds_the_shadow_the_restore_stream_verbatim` (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/mod.rs`).
- **par-mux `attach` passthrough mixed pane content with the terminal's prior screen** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_attach.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): the startup `refresh-client` replay was written verbatim over whatever the host terminal was already showing. The passthrough client now enters the alternate screen as part of terminal setup (in `TerminalGuard`, right after raw mode, before the replay is written) and leaves it on every exit path — detach, `%exit`, socket close, error, panic unwind — before the DECSTBM restore. The status row's DECSTBM reserve applies within the alt screen unchanged. Render mode already entered its own alt screen; `LeaveAlternateScreen` being idempotent means the guard's leave after the session's own is harmless. Pinned by the PTY-level `detach_restores_the_terminal_region` (now asserting `ESC[?1049h` at startup, its ordering before the DECSTBM reserve, and `ESC[?1049l` in the detach tail on unix; Windows ConPTY re-encodes 1049 switches, so those bytes stay unix-asserted per the existing cfg-split conventions).
- **par-mux `attach` flooded the screen over a held-dead pane** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_attach.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): after the focused pane's shell exited (remain-on-exit), every keystroke was forwarded as `send-keys`, which both echoed the bytes into the pane's frozen screen and provoked the daemon's pinned NotStartedError reply per keypress. The client now drops plain stdin bytes while `exited` is set (the flag it already tracks from `%pane-exited` / `pane-info` for the status cue); prefix chords keep working, so detach and respawn stay reachable, and prefix `r` (`respawn-pane`) clears the flag and resumes forwarding on `%pane-respawned`. The status row's `(exited N)` cue now names the chord: `(exited N — C-b r respawns)`. Pinned headless by `dead_pane_takes_no_stdin_bytes_but_prefix_keys_still_route`, `status_line_names_the_respawn_chord_when_the_pane_is_dead`, and `respawn_chord_resumes_forwarding_after_revival` (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/mod.rs`), and PTY-level by `dead_pane_takes_no_typing_and_prefix_r_respawns` (`crates/par-mux/tests/mux_attach.rs`, unix: the typed burst never echoes, no error text renders, prefix `r` round-trips a fresh echo through the revived pane).
- **par-mux `attach --mode render` with no `-t` failed to resolve a session** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/tests/mux_attach.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): `list-sessions` replies `$N: name`, and the render client's target-less resolution took the whole whitespace token (`$0:`) — the daemon's id parser rejects the trailing colon, so `list-windows -t $0:` errored and the client exited 1 with "the session has no windows" instead of the documented default. The resolution now parses the id at the colon (a shared `parse_session_line` helper, the same rule status.rs and passthrough's own re-query used) and picks the NEWEST session (highest id — the deterministic newest stand-in), then its active window's active pane. The same colon-bearing tokenization in both `switch_session` implementations (passthrough and render) is fixed too: their `$N:` tokens could never match the colon-less `session_id` the status view records, so `prefix (` / `)` broke with two or more sessions. Pinned by `target_less_resolution_takes_newest_session_without_colon` (scripted daemon, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/`), `parse_session_line_strips_the_sigil_colon` (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/mod.rs`), and the PTY-level `render_mode_without_target_attaches_the_newest_session` + `render_mode_without_target_picks_the_newest_session` (`crates/par-mux/tests/mux_attach.rs`). Commit `6a6008d`.
- **`PtySession`'s reader thread never signaled a quiet child death and could spin on a dead pty** (`crates/par-term-emu-core/src/pty_session/reader.rs`): the EOF arm stored `running = false` and notified waiters but bumped no generation counter, so a frontend sleep-polling `update_generation()` never observed a child that exited without producing output; and a terminal read error was logged and retried, so on Linux (where a dead pty's master read returns EIO, not EOF) the reader looped on the error forever. Both arms now exit through one `reader_dead` helper — `running = false`, condvar notify, then the generation bump (notify first, so a blocked `wait_for_update` waiter wakes on the running flag; the bump after is what a generation-poll observer sees on its next poll) — and a `read_error_is_terminal` classifier treats BrokenPipe and EIO (unix) as fatal while keeping WouldBlock/Interrupted/UnexpectedEof retryable. The Windows ConPTY contract is untouched (liveness still comes from `try_wait` polling; the pipe never EOFs). Pinned by `quiet_child_death_advances_the_generation`, `injected_terminal_read_error_stops_the_reader_without_spinning`, and `read_error_classifier_treats_broken_pipe_and_eio_as_terminal` (`crates/par-term-emu-core/src/pty_session/reader.rs`). Commit `188210e`.

### Security
- **Streaming server warns on weak Basic Auth credentials** (`src/streaming/config.rs`, `src/streaming/auth_hash.rs`, SEC-201/SEC-206): configuring a cleartext password or a legacy `{SHA}` / `$1$` / `$apr1$` htpasswd hash now logs a `warn!` naming the format and pointing at `htpasswd -nB`. The message never contains the secret; verification is unchanged.
- **Streaming server warns when `--allow-api-key-in-query` is active** (`src/streaming/server/`, SEC-202): startup logs a `warn!` when query-string API keys are accepted, since they land in proxy logs and browser history.
- **`--download-frontend` verifies the archive's SHA-256** (`src/bin/streaming_server/frontend_download.rs`, `.github/workflows/deployment.yml`, SEC-203): releases now ship a `par-term-web-frontend-v<version>.tar.gz.sha256` sidecar, and the download checks it before the web root is touched. A mismatch or unusable sidecar aborts. Releases without a sidecar download with a warning.
- **Web frontend `dev`/`start` scripts bind to `127.0.0.1`** (`web-terminal-frontend/package.json`, SEC-205): LAN testing now needs an explicit `-H 0.0.0.0`. `.gitignore` also covers `.env`, `.env.local`, and `.env*.local` (SEC-204).
- **Web frontend dependencies bumped past `bun audit` advisories** (`web-terminal-frontend/package.json`, `web-terminal-frontend/bun.lock`, `web_term/`, SEC-210): `next` and `eslint-config-next` 16.3.6 → 16.3.8, `sharp` 0.35.4 → 0.35.5, `source-map-js` 1.2.1 → 1.2.2. This clears 8 of 9 advisories (3 of 4 high). The production bundle is a static export, so the exposure was build and dev time only. One high advisory remains: `braces@3.0.3` (GHSA-vfj7-8cjw-p6xm) has no patched release, and it is reachable only through the dev-only `eslint-config-next` → `fast-glob` → `micromatch` chain, against repo-authored glob patterns.
- **par-mux attach: paste bodies are forwarded opaque and re-wrapped for bracketed-paste panes; chords no longer fire on pasted text** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/input.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/render/`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/mod.rs`, SEC-208). Render mode built a fresh input parser on every stdin pump tick, so a paste or an escape sequence that spanned two reads lost its state: a pasted `C-b x` could kill a pane and a split mouse report leaked into the pane as text. The parser now lives on the session, and a bracketed-paste body becomes a `Token::Paste` that skips the prefix and chord scan, cancels a pending prefix, has any embedded `ESC[201~` stripped, and is re-framed with `ESC[200~`/`ESC[201~` when the pane's emulator has DECSET 2004 on (bare otherwise). Passthrough mode tracks the paste markers across bursts and skips the prefix scan inside a paste. A paste larger than the 1 MiB hold cap streams out in successive paste chunks and stays opaque until its terminator, instead of falling back into the chord scanner.
- **par-mux strips control characters from user titles and session, window and workspace names** (`crates/par-mux/src/mux/mod.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/pane.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/tree/lifecycle.rs`, `crates/par-mux/src/mux/attach/status_row.rs`, SEC-209). `select-pane -T`, `new-session`/`new-window`/`new-workspace` names and the `rename-*` commands stored raw C0, DEL and C1 characters, which the passthrough status row and `par-mux -c` echoed to the viewer's terminal (screen clears, title spoofing, OSC 52 clipboard writes). The setters now drop control characters at entry instead of rejecting the command, so existing scripts keep working. The passthrough status row also strips them from OSC 0/2 pane titles at display time. `break-pane` window names, a new workspace's first-window name, and names restored from a save file are stripped the same way, and the effective pane title (the `pane-title` reply `par-mux -c` prints, falling back to the program's OSC 0/2 title) and render-mode border labels drop control characters.

## [0.58.0] - 2026-10-01

### Added
- **par-mux preserves held-dead panes across a daemon restart** (ARC-114; `src/mux/persist.rs`, `src/mux/pane.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): a pane held dead at save time is now restored held dead instead of being respawned as a fresh shell. `PersistPane` gains `dead` and `exit_code` (both `serde(default, skip_serializing_if)`, so `FORMAT_VERSION` is unchanged and older files and older daemons degrade to the previous respawn behavior). `MuxPane::mark_dead_with_code` records a persisted code without asking the (absent) child, and `PaneFactory::create_dead_pane` builds the processless pane (**breaking for external `PaneFactory` implementors**: no default impl, because a pane's session is non-optional). A restored dead pane keeps its exit code, spawn command, agent identity and frozen screen (re-hung without `restore_for_new_process`'s mode and alternate-screen reset), reports `exited=<code>` in `pane-info`, replays `%pane-exited` at registration, is never re-announced by the reaper, and restarts with `respawn-pane` without `-k`. Live panes restore exactly as before. Pinned by `a_held_dead_pane_round_trips_as_held_dead` and `a_save_file_without_the_dead_fields_loads_alive` (`src/mux/persist.rs`), and the dead-pane restart tests in `tests/mux_restart.rs`.
- **par-mux `pane-exited-replay`** (ENH-042; `src/mux/command.rs`, `src/mux/dispatch.rs`, `src/mux/server.rs`): a read-only control command that replays the held panes' `%pane-exited %N [code]` lines on demand to the issuing client only, queued ahead of an empty reply block, framed exactly like registration replay. It reuses the new `replay_pane_exited_lines` helper that registration replay now also calls, so the two cannot drift. Pinned by `pane_exited_replay_reaches_only_the_issuing_client` and `pane_exited_replay_with_no_held_panes_is_empty` (`tests/mux_daemon.rs`).
- **FFI scroll-aware damage (ENH-038, ABI v5 additive)**: `TermScrollDelta` + `ptec_terminal_scroll_delta_since` / `ptec_terminal_content_dirty_ranges_since` let a renderer blit on scroll and redraw only content-changed rows. `TERM_CORE_ABI_VERSION` is now 5; see FFI_GUIDE's Scroll-Aware Damage section for why `total_lines_scrolled` was rejected as a damage source.
- **par-mux `clear-history`** (`9b1b1ed`; `src/mux/command.rs`, `src/mux/dispatch.rs`): `clear-history -t %N` clears a daemon pane's scrollback and wipes its visible screen through the emulator's own ED 3 path (graphics teardown and the `ScreenCleared` event included). It is classified structural (its success saves state), because the cleared scrollback is what a restart-restore replays — a content classification would resurrect the history on restart. Pinned by `clear_history_wipes_scrollback_and_screen` (`src/mux/dispatch.rs`).
- **par-mux `list-commands` and registration-time held-state replay** (ENH-037; `80ba80a`, `d92febf`; `src/mux/command.rs`, `src/mux/dispatch.rs`, `src/mux/server.rs`). The new read-only query answers one `name [feature …]` line per dispatchable command, generated from the `COMMANDS` table, whose rows carry the feature tokens (`resize-pane zoom absolute`, `split-window before start-dir`, `respawn-pane kill start-dir`, `pane-info cmd`, `refresh-client cell-pixels`, `capture-pane escape`), plus a daemon-level `features replay-held-state` line — a client learns a daemon's abilities without probing command by command. On registration a client now receives `%pane-exited` per held pane and `%layout-change` (raw flags `Z`) per zoomed window, queued ahead of its first command's reply; the snapshot and the registry join happen under one tree-lock hold (documented lock order: `tree` then `clients`), and a held pane's `%pane-exited` may repeat where the snapshot overlaps an in-flight reap broadcast — idempotent by contract. `%layout-change` rendering is shared between broadcast and replay through `render_layout_change`, so the two forms cannot drift. Pinned by `list_commands_reply_lists_every_command_exactly_once` and `every_command_feature_key_is_a_command_name` (`src/mux/command.rs`), `held_state_replay_reports_held_panes_and_zoomed_windows` (`src/mux/server.rs`).
- **`InlineImage` is constructible and exported from Python** (QA-220; `src/python_bindings/types/graphics.rs`, `python/par_term_emu_core_rust/__init__.py`). The inline-image store's methods were all bound, but the class had no `__init__` and was not re-exported from the package root, so Python could never create the first image the store holds. It now has a positional constructor (`protocol` through `display_rows`, trailing `id=None`) and joins the `par_term_emu_core_rust` exports. Pinned by `tests/test_inline_image.py`.
- **FFI `ptec_terminal_scrollback_total_scrolled`** (part of C ABI v4; it first landed as `terminal_scrollback_total_scrolled` while `TERM_CORE_ABI_VERSION` still read 3, and the v4 bump subsumes it): total lines ever pushed into scrollback, so an embedder mirroring the scrollback window (ParDeck's history view) derives head evictions and tail appends from the `total`/`count` pair instead of re-reading the whole buffer — the count alone freezes at the ring cap. Pinned by `ffi_scrollback_total_scrolled_tracks_the_window` (invariant `count == min(total, cap)`, cap freeze, alt-screen active-grid reads, ED 3J reset). Note for ENH-038: this counter is a window cursor, not a scroll-damage source — the four failure modes listed there are exactly why.
- **par-mux `pane-info` reports held panes** (audit ARC-095; `src/mux/dispatch.rs`): an optional trailing `exited=<code|?>` token, so a client that connects after `%pane-exited` can render remain-on-exit state. `?` means the exit code was unreadable. A live pane's reply is unchanged.
- **`PtySession::terminal_write()`** (QA-195): exclusive terminal access as a `TerminalWriteGuard` that republishes the geometry mirror when dropped, for callers that cannot use the `with_terminal_mut` closure. **`MuxPane::with_terminal_mut`** is the pane-level equivalent.
- **`TmuxNotification.exit_code`** for `pane-exited` (QA-190; `src/python_bindings/types/notification.rs`): the held pane's exit code as an `int`, `None` when it was unreadable (signal death) and for every other notification type. Python clients no longer parse it out of `name`.

- **par-mux opt-in per-pane hook-only endpoints** (ENH-039; `src/mux/server.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`). `par-mux --pane-endpoints` (default off) gives every pane its own socket beside the control socket that accepts only the four hook-report methods bound to that pane: a report naming another pane is refused, a report omitting `pane_id` is filed for the bound pane, and any control command is answered with a hook-only error before the connection closes. `PAR_MUX_SOCKET` then names the pane's endpoint; the full control socket is exported only as `PAR_MUX_CONTROL_SOCKET` under `--expose-control-socket` or `PAR_MUX_CONTROL=1`. Pinned by the daemon-level env-contract matrix in `tests/mux_daemon.rs`.
- **par-mux fuzz targets run in CI** (ARC-121; `fuzz/`, `.github/workflows/fuzz.yml`). The `mux_parse_command` and `mux_hook_report` targets existed but never ran; they join the fuzz matrix, and `make fuzz-all` runs all seven.
- **`make stub-drift`** (QA-204). Rebuilds the extension, regenerates `_native.pyi` and fails on a diff — `stub-check` never regenerated, so stub drift went unnoticed.
- **`make release-check`** (ENH-036; `scripts/check_release_notes.py`). Every `feat`/`fix` commit since the top CHANGELOG section's base tag must be cited there by SHA or card ID, with a `Changelog: skip` trailer as the escape hatch; `--write` maintains the top compare links idempotently.
- **Dependency-audit gate** (ENH-040; `deny.toml`, `make audit-deps`, weekly `deps-audit.yml`). cargo-deny advisory/source/ban policy plus bun and pip-audit runs with fail-closed tool guards; needs network, so it stays out of `checkall`.
- **Offline doc link gate** (ENH-035; `make doc-links-check`). lychee `--offline --include-fragments` over the repo docs fails on broken intra-repo links and GitHub-slug heading anchors; wired into `checkall` and the CI lint job.
- **The FFI surface doc gate grew to five fail-closed checks** (ENH-034; `scripts/check_ffi_surface.py`): exported functions, header typedef names, `TERM_*` constant coverage, ABI-table version agreement, and no stale "hand-written header" claim about the cbindgen-generated `include/terminal_core.h`.
- **The streaming `Connected` message seeds `initial_screen` with the pane's scrollback** (`9b68492`), so a client attaching to a mux-backed streamer sees the pane history from its first frame.
- **Agent roster wire surface + streamer roster watcher** (`6b6a723`..`ed7f3cb`, redial/decode hardening `a2d61a6`; ParDeck RQ5/Q6): the streaming server can now serve the par-mux agent roster to clients. `proto/terminal.proto` grows `AgentEntry`/`AgentRoster`/`AgentStateChanged` (ServerMessage oneof 38/39; `ClientMessage` untouched — the roster is push-only, full snapshot at connect, deltas after). `src/streaming/roster.rs` parses `list-agents` output (tokens 1–4 positional with source validated `hook|scrape`, `reason=` base64-decoded, unknown keys ignored, malformed lines skipped — a malformed line can never panic or emit a half-parsed entry) and translates notifications to deltas on the daemon's real grammar (`%agent-state-changed <pane> <agent> <state>[ source=<hook|scrape>]` — source is an optional key=value tail token, reason is never carried on deltas; `%agent-released`/`%pane-exited` → release). `roster_watcher.rs` runs one notification-driven watcher per streamer (no timers; the only wait is reconnect backoff), keeps a mutex-guarded roster cache, sends the snapshot to every client right after its `Connected` handshake on both connect paths (subscribe-before-snapshot, so no delta is missed), broadcasts deltas server-wide via the new `StreamingServer::broadcast_all` (the dead `Broadcaster` is untouched), and after a daemon loss reconnects, re-snapshots, and broadcasts a fresh roster so no ghost agent survives a daemon restart. A lagged client (broadcast channel overflow) is resynced with a fresh snapshot instead of silently missing deltas. `py_convert.rs` covers the two new variants for the Python side. Read-only daemon access (`list-agents` + notification listen); par-mux still owns every pane.
### Security
- **par-mux restored dead panes keep their OSC 7 host** (SEC-128 follow-up to ARC-114; `src/mux/persist.rs`, `src/mux/pane.rs`, `docs/MUX.md`): a held-dead pane persisted only its OSC 7 directory, so after a daemon restart `respawn-pane` saw a host-less report, treated it as local, and restarted the pane in the remote shell's directory whenever that path existed on this machine. `PersistPane` gains `cwd_host` (`serde(default, skip_serializing_if)`, so `FORMAT_VERSION` is unchanged and files stay compatible in both directions), captured only when the cwd came from the OSC 7 report and re-seeded with the cwd on restore, so the existing remote-host rejection applies. Pinned by `a_dead_panes_remote_osc7_host_survives_restore`, `a_save_file_without_cwd_host_loads_as_before` (`src/mux/persist.rs`) and `respawn_of_a_restored_dead_pane_ignores_its_remote_osc7_cwd` (`tests/mux_restart.rs`).
- **Trigger results, highlights and bookmarks are bounded** (audit QA-197; `src/terminal/trigger.rs`, `src/terminal/semantic_snapshot.rs`). Trigger output could grow without bound: `Notify`, `MarkLine` and `SplitPane` action results ignored the `max_action_results` cap (only `RunCommand`/`PlaySound`/`SendText` enforced it), trigger highlights were never evicted, and a `duration_ms == 0` highlight never expires (`0 = permanent` by design), so a trigger firing on every written line grew memory for the life of the session; `add_bookmark` — also fed by `MarkLine` actions — grew with it. All six result variants now go through one `TriggerState::push_action_result` helper that keeps the already-capped variants' drop-newest-when-full behavior; highlights evict the oldest beyond `max_action_results` (100); bookmarks cap at `MAX_BOOKMARKS` (1000, matching `max_matches`), evicting the oldest. Pinned by `test_trigger_highlight_cap_evicts_oldest`, `test_trigger_markline_result_cap_drops_newest` and `test_add_bookmark_cap_evicts_oldest`.
- **par-mux `pane-info` no longer serves a program-controlled `cmd=` name unfiltered** (audit SEC-129; `src/mux/foreground.rs`). The foreground command name is the basename of argv[0], which any program sets (`exec -a`), so a pane could hand a mirroring client ANSI escapes, a spoofed name, or an ARG_MAX-sized token for its close-confirmation prompt. `ProcessTable::foreground_command` now applies the `git_branch` rule: a name that is empty, over `MAX_FOREGROUND_NAME_LEN` (128 bytes, new caps-table row), or carries a control character is served absent, never truncated or stripped. The name stays a display hint, not an identity. Pinned by `foreground_command_rejects_control_and_oversize_names`.
- **The Python debug log is no longer a shared-temp symlink target** (audit SEC-131, recurring SEC-120; `python/par_term_emu_core_rust/debug.py`). With `DEBUG_LEVEL` set, `DebugLogger` opened the fixed path `<tempdir>/par_term_emu_debug_python.log` with `open(..., "w")`: on a shared `/tmp` another local user could plant a symlink there and have the log truncate and overwrite any file the victim can write, and the log was created world-readable under the default umask. It now matches `src/debug.rs`: the file is `par_term_emu_debug_python_<pid>.log`, created `0600` through `os.open` with `O_NOFOLLOW`, and a planted symlink fails the open, which disables logging (fail closed). **Behavior change:** tools tailing the old fixed filename must glob `par_term_emu_debug_python_*.log`. `debug.DEBUG_FILE` still names the active path. Pinned by `tests/test_debug_log.py`.
- **Kitty `t=t` reads and deletes only the file that passed the temp gate** (`src/graphics/kitty.rs`, audit SEC-130, CWE-367). The gate checked the canonicalized path, but the file was opened and later deleted through the original path, and `O_NOFOLLOW` guards only the final component, so a parent directory swapped for a symlink in between redirected both. The read now opens the canonical path, re-runs the temp-root and filename gate on the path the kernel reports for the open handle (`/proc/self/fd` on Linux, `F_GETPATH` on macOS), and pins the pending delete to the handle's `(dev, ino)`: a file swapped in at that path after the read is left in place. Behavior change: on Linux without `/proc` mounted, `t=t` now fails closed.
- **`cargo audit` is clean** (`Cargo.toml`; SEC-134). The `image` dependency's `rayon` feature is dropped — nothing in this crate used it — so the unmaintained `paste` proc-macro (RUSTSEC-2024-0436) and its chain (`pulp`, `rav1e`, `exr`, `ravif`) leave Cargo.lock entirely.

### Changed
- **par-mux commands are one declarative table** (ARC-094b; `src/mux/command.rs`, `scripts/check_mux_docs.py`). Each `COMMANDS` row is now `(name, parser, feature-tokens)`, so a command's parser and its `list-commands` metadata live on the same row instead of two parallel name-keyed lists; the ENH-033 doc gate's table parser follows the row shape.
- **C ABI v4: the breaking FFI revision** (audit decision D4; ARC-101, ARC-112, ARC-114; **breaking for C/Swift embedders**). `TERM_CORE_ABI_VERSION` and `ptec_terminal_abi_version()` are now 4. One revision instead of three; migration notes are in `docs/FFI_GUIDE.md` ("ABI Version").
  - **Every export is prefixed `ptec_`** (ARC-112): `terminal_create` → `ptec_terminal_create`, and so on for all 22 functions, so the C surface cannot collide with another library's `terminal_*` symbols in one process. Types (`SharedCell`, `TerminalObserverVtable`, …) and `TERM_*` constants keep their names.
  - **`SharedCell` colors are display colors** (ARC-101). They used the fixed xterm table, so after `OSC 4;1;rgb:00/00/ff` SGR 31 still read back as (128,0,0). They now resolve like the Python snapshot: the live ANSI palette for the 16 base colors, the OSC 10/11 default for a default cell, and bold brightening. `attrs` gains `TERM_ATTR_DEFAULT_FG` (bit 12), `TERM_ATTR_DEFAULT_BG` (bit 13) and `TERM_ATTR_HAS_COMBINING` (bit 14).
