Default is recognized by value: a color equal to the unstyled color or the current OSC 10/11 value. An explicit SGR 37/40 (or `38;5;7`/`48;5;0`) is flagged the same way, and a cell written under an earlier OSC 10/11 value keeps that color unflagged once the default moves again. Short rows are padded with a resolved default cell instead of fixed white-on-black. The `SharedCell` layout is unchanged.
  - **New `ptec_terminal_read_cell_grapheme`** (ARC-101) returns a cell's full UTF-8 grapheme cluster. `SharedCell.text` holds only the base character, so `x` + U+0301 read back as `x`.
  - **Structured events: `on_event_v2`** (ARC-114). A new `TerminalObserverVtable` slot before `user_data` (the vtable grows from 48 to 56 bytes on LP64) receives a `TermEvent { kind, _pad, payload_len, payload }`: one `TERM_EVENT_*` code per event kind, and a length-delimited UTF-8 JSON payload whose keys are the Python event-dict keys. The five text slots still deliver `Debug` text, which remains diagnostic only.
  - **Fixed: text-slot events whose `Debug` text contained a NUL were silently dropped** (`CString::new` failed). The text slots now replace the NUL with U+FFFD, and `on_event_v2` carries it JSON-escaped.
  - Still true from ARC-112: the C surface compiles only with the `ffi` feature, so Python wheels and Rust embedders export no C symbols; C/Swift embedders build `--features rust-only,ffi` (`make xcframework` does).
- **Python per-cell colors follow the palette and the default colors** (ARC-101; **behavior change**). `get_fg_color`, `get_bg_color`, `get_line_cells`, `scrollback_line` and `create_snapshot` resolve through one core resolver, `Terminal::resolve_cell_colors`: the live ANSI palette (OSC 4 / `set_ansi_palette_color`), the OSC 10/11 defaults for default cells, and bold brightening. Only `create_snapshot` used the palette before; the other four returned the fixed xterm table, so SGR 31 read back as (128,0,0) where the snapshot reported `get_ansi_palette()[1]`. On the default theme `create_snapshot` output is unchanged except after OSC 10/11 or `set_default_fg`/`set_default_bg`, which now recolor default cells. `get_underline_color`, `default_fg()` and `default_bg()` also resolve through the palette: `default_fg()` on a fresh terminal returns palette slot 7 (229,229,229) instead of (192,192,192), matching what `get_fg_color` reports for a default cell. Rust gains `Terminal::resolve_cell_colors` / `ResolvedCellColors` beside the existing `Terminal::resolve_color` (the 16-slot palette resolver).
- **The `par-mux` binary needs the new `mux-bin` feature** (ARC-106; **breaking for `cargo install` / `cargo build --bin par-mux`**). `mux` no longer pulls `clap`, so library embedders (par-term) stop compiling it; `mux-bin = ["mux", "clap"]` mirrors the `streaming`/`streaming-bin` split. Install with `cargo install par-term-emu-core-rust --no-default-features --features mux-bin --bin par-mux`. The integration tests that exec the daemon (`mux_cli`, `mux_daemon`, `mux_nested`, `mux_agents`, `mux_hooks`, `mux_restart`) now fail to compile without `mux-bin`: cargo sets `CARGO_BIN_EXE_par-mux` even when the bin is skipped, so a plain-`mux` run would otherwise exec a stale binary. `make check-features` asserts clap is absent from `rust-only,mux`.
- **`Macro` YAML save/load needs the new `macro-yaml` feature** (ARC-116; **breaking for Rust embedders on `rust-only`/`sim`/`mux`**). `Macro::{save_yaml, load_yaml, to_yaml, from_yaml}` are compiled only with `macro-yaml`, which `python`, `python-test` and `streaming-bin` enable, so the Python `Macro` API and `par-term-streamer --macro-file` are unchanged and slim builds no longer compile `serde_yaml_ng`. tokio's `test-util` moves out of the runtime dependency; the dev-dependency already provides it to tests.
- **Wheels have one feature set** (ARC-105): `streaming` moves into `pyproject.toml` `[tool.maturin] features`, so CI, TestPyPI and PyPI wheels and `make dev` builds all include the streaming classes (TestPyPI and CI wheels previously lacked them, and `make dev` silently dropped them from stub regeneration). The per-command `--features streaming` flags are gone; `make dev-streaming` and `make build-streaming` remain as aliases of `make dev` / `make build`. `make dev` now compiles the tokio/axum/rustls stack, so it takes longer.
- **`Cargo.lock` is committed; CI and release builds use `--locked`** (ARC-107). Every `cargo build`/`test`/`clippy` step and every maturin wheel build in the workflows fails instead of re-resolving when the lockfile is stale, and jobs that build through `make` or a script (the feature matrix, FFI drift, xcframework and bench gate) assert it first with `cargo fetch --locked`. Released wheels, streamer binaries and the xcframework are now built from the reviewed dependency set rather than whatever resolved on the day. The `fuzz/` workspace's lockfile stays untracked.
- **Core diagnostics reach the `log` facade** (ARC-111; `src/debug.rs`). Every `debug_error!`/`debug_info!`/`debug_log!`/`debug_trace!` record and `debug::log_*` helper is now emitted through `log` under the target `par_term_emu_core_rust` (`debug::LOG_TARGET`), formatted `[CATEGORY] message`, at the level the installed logger enables. The `DEBUG_LEVEL` temp-file log is now an optional second sink with the same format, except that a `DEBUG_LEVEL=4` buffer snapshot is written as a normal `[ts] [TRACE] [BUFFER_SNAPSHOT] …` record instead of a bare banner block. **Visible effect for embedders:** par-mux's stderr logger (Info and above) now prints core Error/Info records such as `PTY_SPAWN` command lines. `par-term-streamer` prints them through its tracing subscriber, whose `tracing-log` bridge is on by default: Info records by default, and with `--verbose` every Debug record as well, including per-sequence `CSI`/`OSC`/`ESC` dispatch (client keystrokes take the queued raw-writer path and are not logged as `PTY_WRITE`; only `--macro-file` playback is). par-term's log bridge records Info and above to its log file. Filter the `par_term_emu_core_rust` target to silence core records. With no `DEBUG_LEVEL` and no logger, the per-sequence level check is still two atomic loads. Pinned by `tests/debug_log_facade.rs`.
- **`ClientMessage::Mouse.event_type` is a `MouseEventType` enum** (QA-213; `src/streaming/protocol.rs`; **breaking for Rust embedders constructing `ClientMessage::Mouse` or calling `ClientMessage::mouse`**). `Press`/`Release`/`Move`/`Scroll`, with `as_str()`/`parse()` for the wire names. The protobuf wire format is unchanged (`MouseInput.event_type` stays a string); an unknown name decodes as `Press`, as before, when every name but `"release"` counted as a press. The Python dict API still reads and writes the lowercase name and defaults to `"press"`, but an unknown name now comes back as `"press"` instead of verbatim. serde JSON output is unchanged for the four names, and deserializing JSON with any other name is now an error.
- **The 17 internal `impl_terminal_*!` binding macros are no longer exported at the crate root** (ARC-117). They were `#[macro_export]`ed into the public API and docs.rs although they expand to crate-private items and could not be used outside the crate; they are now `pub(crate) use` re-exports from `python_bindings::common`.
- **The Python stub carries docstrings and documented types** (DOC-116; `scripts/generate_stubs.py`, `_native.pyi`). Every class, method, function and property in `_native.pyi` now has its binding docstring for IDE hover help. Return and parameter types come from the Google-style `Returns:`/`Args:` sections where those name a concrete type; the rest stay `Any`.

- **`SpawnContext` gains an `output` sink** (ARC-103; **breaking for Rust embedders**, `src/mux/pane.rs`). A par-mux pane's output sink now rides the spawn context as `Option<&OutputSink>` and `ShellPaneFactory` installs it on the PTY before the child spawns, so the first bytes a new pane prints (`new-session`, `new-window`, `split-window`, `respawn-pane`) reach clients. Before, the sink was installed only after the pane was inserted into the tree, and anything printed in between reached the daemon grid but no client. Struct literals of `SpawnContext` need `output: None` or `..SpawnContext::default()`. A custom factory that builds its own `PtySession` should install `context.output` before spawning; the dispatcher re-installs the sink after the insert, so a factory that ignores it keeps today's early-output gap. With the sink live from the first byte, `%output %N` can precede the command's reply and `%window-add`/`%layout-change`; clients drop output for a pane they have not mapped, as before. The four creation commands now share one `spawn_and_wire` helper instead of four copies of the wiring block.
- **par-mux `new-window`/`new-session` reject trailing command text instead of ignoring it** (audit QA-219; `src/mux/command.rs`). `new-window sleep 5` replied success and opened a shell, silently dropping `sleep 5`. A positional word (or anything after `--`) is now an error pointing at `respawn-pane`. tmux's value flags consume their value (`new-session -x 80 -y 24` still parses), quoted values stay one word, and unknown flags stay tolerated. Neither par-term sender passes a command.
- **Behavior: Python `Terminal.encode_key` option-key modes default to ESC on both sides** (audit ARC-093/QA-189, `src/python_bindings/terminal/input_api.rs`; **breaking for Python callers relying on the old default**). `left_option`/`right_option` defaulted to 0 (Normal passthrough), so Alt+f encoded as `b'f'` from Python but `b'\x1bf'` from C `terminal_encode_key` and Rust `KeyEncodeOptions::default()`, contradicting the documented byte identity. The default is now 2 (ESC prefix); pass `left_option=0, right_option=0` for the old passthrough.
- **par-mux `send-keys` and macro playback encode keys through the shared encoder** (audit ARC-093; `src/mux/command.rs`, `src/mux/dispatch.rs`, `src/macros.rs`, `src/terminal/macros.rs`; **breaking for Rust embedders matching `MuxCommand::SendKeys`**). There were three named-key tables, and neither par-mux nor macros honored the application's modes, so arrows sent to an app in application-cursor mode got `ESC [ A` instead of `ESC O A`.
  - **send-keys**: `Up`/`Down`/`Left`/`Right`, `Home`, `End`, `PageUp`/`PgUp`/`PPage`, `PageDown`/`PgDn`/`NPage`, `Insert`/`IC`, `Delete`/`DC`, `F1`–`F12` and `BTab` are encoded against the target pane's DECCKM and kitty keyboard state, as tmux does. The control-byte names (`C-x`, `C-Space`, `Escape`, `BSpace`, `Space`, `Enter`, and the new `Tab`) stay raw bytes, because par-term sends already-encoded keystrokes in that form and re-encoding them would double-encode. **Behavior change:** `Tab` and every navigation/function name above except the four arrows were written as literal text before and are now keys. Key names match inside quoted runs too, as in tmux, so a par-term keystroke chunk that spells exactly one of these words now sends the key; send literal text with `-l`. `MuxCommand::SendKeys.keys` is now a `SendKeysPayload` of `SendKeysPart::Bytes`/`Key` parts, encoded at dispatch with `SendKeysPayload::encode`; the terminal is read only when a `Key` part is present.
  - **Macros**: `MacroEngine::tick_macro`, Python `PtyTerminal.tick_macro` and `par-term-streamer --macro-file` encode through the new `KeyParser::encode_key(key, &Terminal)`, so replayed keys follow DECCKM, kitty flags and modifyOtherKeys like a real keypress (under kitty flags, `enter` is `CSI 13u`). `KeyParser::parse_key` keeps its signature and encodes against a freshly reset terminal. **Behavior change:** modifiers on named keys now take effect: `alt+f4` is `ESC[1;3S` (was `ESCOS`), `ctrl+alt+del` is `ESC[3;7~`, `shift+enter` is LF, and `ctrl+alt+a` is `ESC ^A` (was `^A`, Alt dropped). Non-ASCII keys (`é`, `alt+é`) are still sent as their literal bytes.
- **par-mux pane telemetry and hook sequence stamps are typed pane fields** (audit ARC-113; `src/mux/pane.rs`, `src/mux/hooks/`, `src/mux/host_probe.rs`). The `agent_telemetry`, `agent_host_telemetry` and `agent_seq_by_source` metadata keys were JSON strings re-parsed on every `list-agents` read and every hook report, under the tree lock. They are now `MuxPane` fields: the hook telemetry's roster token is base64-encoded once when the report is accepted, the host probe's sample is stored typed and rendered to the same canonical JSON at serve time, and the per-source `seq` stamps are a map. Wire bytes are unchanged (pinned by test). The three keys no longer appear in `MuxPane::metadata()`, which only a Rust embedder reading those raw keys would notice. Nothing is persisted differently: all three were already display-only or volatile.
- **`GridSnapshot` lives in the grid layer** (ARC-108; `9709bc4`; `src/grid/snapshot.rs`). It was defined in `terminal::replay_snapshot`, so the grid layer depended on terminal; it moved verbatim (same serde derives and field order — the par-mux on-disk format) and `terminal::replay_snapshot` re-exports it, so every existing path still resolves.
- **The par-mux shutdown save runs off the tree lock** (ARC-119; `4a7b77e`; `src/mux/persist.rs`). `save_off_lock` takes the lock only for the field capture, then serializes, fsyncs and renames without it, so a wedged filesystem during shutdown no longer stalls the reap tick and late client commands; `save_to_with_origin` keeps its signature for direct callers.
- **The par-mux build stamp ignores a git repo that is not this crate's** (ARC-120; `df747eb`; `build.rs`). A crate vendored inside another repository, or a crates.io tarball extracted in one, stamps the source digest (`src-<digest>`) instead of the outer repo's commit.
- **`emit()`'s daemon-unreachable notifications are one explicit arm** (ARC-094; `9329b96`; `src/mux/hooks/report.rs`). The `'_ => String::new()'` wildcard meant a new `TmuxNotification` variant compiled silently and emitted nothing; the never-constructed variants are now listed explicitly, so adding one fails to compile until the emit decision is made.
- **`checkall` runs a non-mutating `lint-check` gate** (ARC-115; `f51472e`; `Makefile`). `cargo fmt --check`, clippy `-D warnings`, `ruff format --check`, `ruff check` and pyright — the gate reports drift instead of rewriting the tree; `make lint` / `make lint-python` remain the fix targets.
### Removed
- **Legacy stringly-typed event methods removed — breaking (Python API)** (`src/python_bindings/terminal/mod.rs`, `src/python_bindings/observer.rs`; DOC-120). `poll_events_legacy()` and `poll_subscribed_events_legacy()`, the 0.50.0 migration bridge, are gone. Use `poll_events()` / `poll_subscribed_events()`, which return native Python types.
- **`Grid::erase_rectangle`** (audit QA-218; **breaking for Rust embedders**, none known). It was reachable only from a `CSI … $ {` arm in the window handler that the CSI router never calls: DECSERA goes to the terminal-level `handle_decsera`. Use `Terminal::erase_rectangle` (selective, what DECSERA and the Python binding use) or `Grid::erase_rectangle_unconditional`. The dead arm is removed too; DECSERA behavior is unchanged.
- **Four unused Python debug helpers** (audit QA-218; `par_term_emu_core_rust.debug`): `log_snapshot`, `log_terminal_state`, `log_textual_event`, `log_get_line_cells_call`. No caller in this repo, par-term-emu-tui-rust or par-term. The five helpers par-term-emu-tui-rust imports stay.

### Deprecated
- **`streaming::Broadcaster`** (ARC-112). `StreamingServer` never used it (clients are managed per session), and it has no users outside its own tests. It will be removed in a future release.
- **`TmuxNotification.name` carrying the `pane-exited` exit code** (QA-190). Read `exit_code` instead; `name` stops carrying the code for `pane-exited` in 0.59.0.
- **`ServerMessage::connected_full`** (QA-212). Use `ServerMessage::connected_builder()`, the single edit site for `Connected` fields, like the other deprecated `connected_*` constructors.

### Fixed
- **`PtyTerminal.paste()` no longer deadlocks on the terminal lock** (QA-221; `src/python_bindings/pty.rs`). It read the bracketed-paste sequences under `terminal.read()` and kept that guard across `PtySession::write`, which takes `terminal.write()` to record input — a read-to-write request on the same non-reentrant parking_lot RwLock, so every paste self-deadlocked before writing a byte. Beyond the deadlock, holding any guard across the blocking PTY write starves the reader thread once the PTY input buffer fills (the child's echo stops draining, the child stops reading, the write never completes). The sequences are now snapshotted under a short read lock and all writes run with no guard held. Pinned by `test_paste_large_content_output_processing_continues` (tests/test_pty.py), which bounds a regressed paste with `faulthandler.dump_traceback_later(exit=True)` because the GIL-starved pytest-timeout watchdog cannot fire.
- **Python `detect_urls`/`detect_file_paths`/`detect_semantic_items` return correct `row`/`col`** (QA-220; `src/python_bindings/terminal/search_api.rs`). The bindings destructured the core's `DetectedItem(text, col, row)` tuples as `(text, row, col)`, so every `DetectedItem.row`/`.col` pair Python saw was swapped. Pinned by `tests/test_detect_items.py`, which asserts positions where row != col.
- **`check_activity()` is no longer a no-op stub** (QA-220; `src/terminal/notification.rs`, `src/terminal/mod.rs`). The body was an empty `if`; with `activity_enabled` set it now queues an `Activity`/`Visual` event when `update_activity()` was called since the previous event, rate-limited to at most one per `activity_threshold` seconds. Rust tests live beside the implementation in `src/terminal/notification.rs`; Python tests in `tests/test_check_activity.py`.
- **A PTY spawn no longer panics on a non-UTF-8 parent environment** (QA-196; `src/pty_session/`). The inherited-env drop read `std::env::vars()`, which panics on a variable whose name or value is not valid Unicode; it now reads `vars_os()` once.
- **RIS keeps every embedder setting** (audit ARC-100, `src/terminal/mod.rs`): the OSC 1337 transfer cap, bold brightening, window position/iconified state and the mouse/inline-image/command/cwd history caps now live in a `HostConfig` that `reset()` carries whole; the conformance level and bell volumes restore to the host-configured baseline (a program's DECSCL/DECSWBV/DECSMBV still resets). Before this, one `ESC c` from any program restored the 50 MiB OSC 1337 transfer cap after an embedder lowered it. Rust gains `Terminal::set_conformance_level`/`set_warning_bell_volume`/`set_margin_bell_volume`; Python `set_conformance_level`/`set_warning_bell_volume`/`set_margin_bell_volume` now set that baseline instead of feeding the escape (`c1_mode` is accepted and ignored, as the DECSCL handler never read it). A classification test (`tests/test_ris_host_config.py`) fails on any new `set_*` until it is declared host config or VT state.
- **Snapshot restore onto a live terminal reports the whole screen dirty** (audit ARC-092, `src/terminal/replay_snapshot.rs`). `restore_from_snapshot` could make the lower-generation grid visible (an alt-screen snapshot restored over a busy primary screen) without syncing the two grids' damage generations, so `dirty_rows_since(g)` for a generation captured before the restore returned no rows and a damage-driven renderer (FFI readback, ENH-025) kept drawing the old screen. Every wholesale change of the visible screen (alt-screen enter/exit, RIS, both snapshot restores) now goes through one `invalidate_visible_screen()` funnel that syncs generations before marking rows. Only Rust embedders restoring onto a live terminal were affected; par-mux and `SnapshotManager` restore onto fresh terminals.
- **Triggers match rows that scroll off the screen before the next scan** (audit ARC-091, `src/terminal/mod.rs`, `src/terminal/trigger.rs`). Pending trigger rows were visible-row indices, and a row that left the screen between scans was dropped unscanned: an `ERROR` line followed by 10 lines in one feed on a 5-row terminal produced no match, so Notify/RunCommand/MarkLine triggers missed matches in bursty output such as build logs, depending on the OS read size. Every Terminal-level region scroll, IL and DL now goes through a tracked wrapper that scans the pending rows about to leave (into scrollback, off a DECSTBM region, off the alternate screen, or off the bottom via SD/RI/IL) before the grid moves them. Only written rows are scanned, each exactly once (the ARC-064 rule). A match found this way reports the visible row the text occupied just before it scrolled, and its actions now run inside `process()`. Also fixed: the wide-character wrap at the bottom row scrolled without moving pending rows, so the wrapped row's pending index pointed at the new blank line and it was never matched.
- **Kitty key encoding reports non-BMP codepoints whole, and unknown keys encode to nothing in both regimes** (audit ARC-098, merges QA-209; `src/keyboard.rs`). The kitty (`CSI u`) encoder held the key code in a `u16`, so Ctrl+U+1D54F encoded as `CSI 54607;5u`; it now reports `CSI 120143;5u`. `TermKey::Unknown` (or any raw key value that is not a `TERM_KEY_*` code) encoded as `CSI 0u` under kitty while producing nothing under legacy; it is now empty under both, matching the "empty output means the key has no encoding" contract. Under kitty, a `Char` whose codepoint is not a Unicode scalar value (e.g. a surrogate) is likewise empty instead of `CSI 0;…u`.
- **`get_stats()` reports non-zero estimates**: `estimated_memory_bytes` and `hyperlink_memory_bytes` were always 0 (QA-206; `src/terminal/metrics.rs`). Both are now computed lower bounds: cells times `size_of::<Cell>()`, and per-URL bytes including the hyperlink map key and `String` header.
- **A reaped child's PID is never signalled again** (audit SEC-125; `src/pty_session/`). A held (remain-on-exit) par-mux pane, or a Python `PtyTerminal` after `try_wait()`, kept its reaped child's PID. Later `kill-pane`/`respawn-pane -k` SIGHUPed it through portable-pty's raw `kill(2)`, and every resize sent SIGWINCH to it and its process group. Once the OS recycled the PID, a same-user process could be terminated. `PtySession` now records the exit under a lock the first time `try_wait`/`wait`/`kill` (or a respawn's cleanup) reaps the child. Every signal path, including the reader thread's alt-screen pulse, checks that record while holding it. As a result, `pane-info cmd=`, the host probe, scrape agent liveness and respawn's cwd fallback no longer read a released PID. **Behavior change:** `PtySession::child_pid()` / Python `child_pid()` return `None` once the exit has been observed. `kill()` after the exit returns `Ok`/`None` instead of raising `OSError` (ESRCH).
- **`respawn-pane` no longer reads flags from inside the command** (audit SEC-126, merges QA-183; `src/mux/command.rs`). The parser scanned the whole line for `-k` and `-c`. So `respawn-pane -t %0 sh -c 'echo X; sort -k 1 f; sleep 600'` killed a live pane without `-k`, took the inner `-c` as the start directory, and ran the fragment `X; sort -k 1 f; sleep 600'`. It still replied success and broadcast `%pane-respawned`. Also fixed: `-c '/a b'` produced the command `b' …`, `-c /tmp -t %0 top` produced `-t %0 top`, and runs of whitespace inside quotes collapsed. Flags (`-t`, `-c`, `-k`, any order) are now read only up to the first command word or `--`, getopt-style. The command is the raw rest of the line, passed to the pane's shell with its quoting and whitespace intact. **Behavior change:** an unknown leading flag (`-x`), a repeated `-t`/`-c`, combined flags (`-kt`), or an empty `-c ''` is now an error. A command starting with `-` must follow `--`.
- **`respawn-pane`'s default cwd no longer trusts a remote OSC 7** (audit SEC-128; `src/mux/pane.rs`, `src/mux/tree/`). With no `-c`, respawn restarted in the pane's OSC 7 cwd. OSC 7 is program output: a remote shell over SSH, or anything printing the escape, chose the local directory the new process ran in. The new `MuxPane::respawn_cwd` uses OSC 7 only when its host is absent, `localhost`, or this machine's hostname (full or short form, case-insensitive), and only when the directory exists. Otherwise it uses the live child's kernel-reported cwd (the `-k` case). Failing both, it defers to the factory's pane cwd, then `$HOME`, where a fresh pane lands. It no longer uses the daemon process's own working directory. A held dead pane has no child cwd (SEC-125), so it never falls back to a reaped PID. This matches SEC-115's rule for the host probe. Persistence/restore still prefers OSC 7 (`persistence_cwd` is unchanged).
- **The par-mux control socket's 1 MiB line budget now holds for a line that never ends** (audit SEC-127, recurring SEC-118; `src/mux/server.rs`). The budget was checked only after `read_line` returned, and `read_line` never returns without a newline, so any same-user program (including one inside a pane via `$PAR_MUX_SOCKET`) could stream bytes with no newline and grow the daemon until it OOMed, taking every session with it (reproduced: 256 MiB in, RSS 12 → 279 MiB, connection still open). The read loop now accumulates raw bytes through a bounded `fill_buf` loop that checks the budget per chunk, so an unterminated stream is answered with the same `%error` block and closed near 1 MiB. The same change fixes a silent data loss: a recv-timeout wake that split a multi-byte UTF-8 character made `read_line` discard the partial it had already consumed, so the rest of the line was answered with `%error`. Bytes now stay raw until the line completes and UTF-8 is decoded once.
- **The par-mux control-command debug summary no longer records typed input or clipboard content** (audit SEC-132, recurring SEC-123; `src/mux/server.rs`, `docs/SECURITY.md`). `summarize_line` kept the first 120 bytes of every control line, so at `DEBUG_LEVEL>=1` (rejected and unparseable lines) and `>=3` (every command) the log captured `send-keys` input, including passwords typed into a pane, and `set-buffer` clipboard content. Those two commands are now logged as name, leading `-t` target and byte count only, whether or not the line parses. `set-environment` and `new-session -e` values are still logged verbatim, and at `DEBUG_LEVEL>=3` the PTY write log still hex-dumps every byte delivered to a pane; SECURITY.md gains a "Debug Logging" subsection that says both.
- **The par-mux host probe can no longer deadlock on git output or hang the daemon on a wedged filesystem** (audit SEC-133, merges QA-193, recurring SEC-124 + QA-155; `src/mux/host_probe.rs`, `src/mux/server.rs`). `run_git` read stdout only after git exited, so a repo whose `ls-files --others` output passed the 64 KiB pipe buffer blocked git until the 5 s kill and `git_dirty` served nothing. Each pane's `statvfs` and three git runs had no combined bound, so one sweep could take 25 s, and shutdown joined it unconditionally, so a hung NFS/FUSE mount could block `par-mux --stop` forever. Now: stdout is drained on its own thread and capped per call (1 byte for the dirty check, just past the branch limit for the branch), so a chatty git fails its next write instead of blocking; killed children are reaped on a detached thread; each pane's probe runs on a detached worker the sweep waits for at most 6 s, and a pane whose probe timed out is skipped until its cwd changes; the shutdown flag is checked before each pane and each git run; and the daemon waits at most 1 s for the probe thread at shutdown before detaching it. At most 4 probe threads may be outstanding before the sweep stops starting new ones.
- **par-mux `resize-pane` while zoomed no longer rewrites the hidden layout** (audit ARC-090; `src/mux/tree/`). `resize-pane -x/-y/-L/-R/-U/-D` never cleared the zoom, so it moved the divider behind a zoomed pane. Splitting an 80-column window 40/40, zooming `%0`, sending `resize-pane -x 79` and unzooming restored a 79/1 layout. par-term sends this on every divider drag. A successful resize now ends the zoom first, as tmux does (`server_unzoom_window`). A rejected resize (no divider on that axis) changes nothing, including the zoom. `-x` and `-y` together are all-or-nothing. Before, when one axis moved and the other spanned the window, the first change stayed applied and the terminals were never re-fitted. Every layout mutation (split, swap, break, join, kill, resize) now goes through one `MuxTree::mutate_layout` choke point that ends the zoom and re-fits the panes, instead of seven hand-written `zoomed = None` sites. A same-window `join-pane` now re-fits each pane once, to the final layout. **Behavior change for par-term:** a divider drag in a zoomed par-mux window now unzooms it.
- **par-mux `respawn-pane -k` no longer leaks the dying process's output into the respawned pane** (audit ARC-089; `src/mux/pane.rs`, `src/mux/tree/`, `src/mux/dispatch.rs`). The replacement reuses the pane id. The old process kept its `%output` sink through portable-pty's SIGHUP grace and the reap, so a SIGHUP handler's parting bytes arrived as `%output %N` beside `%pane-respawned %N`. A TUI's alt-screen exit and cursor restore landed on the fresh screen in every client. The new `MuxPane::detach_output` stops forwarding and returns only once no sink call is in flight. It runs synchronously before every detached kill (`kill-pane`, `kill-window`, `kill-session`, respawn and failed spawns), inside `MuxPane::kill`, and for `respawn-pane -k` before the off-lock spawn. If that spawn fails, the still-live pane resumes forwarding. The killed process's exit output is no longer broadcast, including to a `kill-pane` id that no longer exists. While a `-k` spawn runs, the old process's bytes still reach the daemon's terminal but not clients. After a failed `-k`, a mirror rebuilt from `%output` lacks them.
- **par-mux `refresh-client` sizes are capped, and pane pixel extents no longer overflow** (audit QA-182; `src/mux/command.rs`, `src/mux/pane.rs`, `src/pty_session/`, `src/python_bindings/pty.rs`). `-C` is capped at 1000×500 (the streaming server's viewer bounds, so a mux-backed streamer's forwarded resize is never refused) and `-p` at 512×512 per cell; a larger report is an error. Before, any size up to 65535×65535 was accepted, and a grid that size could abort the daemon on allocation. Separately, `cols * cell_w` was computed in `u16`, so 2000 columns of 40 px cells panicked; PTY pixel extents now saturate at `u16::MAX`. Python `PtyTerminal.resize`/`resize_pixels` raise `ValueError` for a value past 65535 instead of silently truncating it.
- **`PtyTerminal` methods that mutate the terminal from Python, and par-mux restores and client-color reports, now republish the geometry mirror** (audit QA-195; `src/pty_session/`, `src/python_bindings/pty.rs`, `src/mux/pane.rs`, `src/mux/tree/`, `src/mux/persist.rs`). They wrote through the raw terminal lock, which skipped the wait-free `(cols, rows, cursor)` mirror, so `cursor_position()`/`size()` stayed stale until the next PTY output. They now go through `PtySession::terminal_write()` or `MuxPane::with_terminal_mut`, which publish on release.
- **The streaming server's input/write paths are load-hardened** (QA-191 `4b46b55`, QA-192 `60b67f3`, QA-198 `bec391c`, QA-184 `63835bc`; `src/streaming/session.rs`, `src/streaming/server.rs`). Input on a writer-less session is dropped and counted instead of closing the WebSocket (the bundled frontend's retry looped forever); the PTY input drain parks in `recv_timeout` instead of polling at ~100 wakes/s and bounds its writer wait; blocking PTY and daemon writes move off tokio workers via `spawn_blocking`; mux session creation gets a per-reply deadline instead of unbounded blocking on the WebSocket accept path.
- **Python PTY writes release the GIL while blocked** (QA-224; `9cdb74e`; `src/python_bindings/pty.rs`). `paste`/`write`/`write_str`/`tick_macro` held the GIL across `PtySession::write`, which can block on a full PTY buffer, starving every other Python thread; all four route through one `py.detach` helper.
- **par-mux operational fixes**: `paste-buffer` snapshots and writes off the tree mutex (QA-225; `a93f167`); the stale-socket probe re-probes for 150 ms before refusing a teardown ghost (QA-223; `7ffdb35`); a failed pane resize is logged instead of silently discarded (QA-188; `c703ad9`).
- **Restored panes clamp hostile persisted geometry** (`9135598`, `665ba2f`; the ARC-113 restore-hardening family; `src/mux/persist.rs`). A state file claiming a 65535×65535 window or degenerate grid dimensions now clamps to the streaming-layer bounds (2..=1000 cols, 1..=500 rows) instead of driving the allocation.
- **Production `unwrap()`s removed from server, file-transfer, macro and export paths** (QA-216; `145a31c`).
- **Every production unsafe block states its invariant, and undocumented ones are linted** (QA-201; `7952272`; `#![cfg_attr(not(test), warn(clippy::undocumented_unsafe_blocks))]`).
- **`SharedState.cell_count` derives from its allocation** (QA-215; `7a20994`), removing the duplicated count `Drop` rebuilt the slice from.
- **The Windows `TOKEN_USER` query buffer is correctly aligned** (QA-194; `f9b489d`): a `Vec<u64>` sized in 8-byte units instead of a `Vec<u8>` viewed as a pointer-bearing struct.
- **The unneeded unsafe `Send`/`Sync` impls on the Python observer wrappers are gone** (QA-210; `0cc12dd`) — pyo3 already implements them for `Py<T>`.
- **Dead `TerminalDebug.tsx` removed** (QA-211; `55359f8`): nothing imported it and it carried an ungated `console.log`.
- **Color and attribute tests assert real palette values** (QA-217; `cd3bf92`) instead of `is not None`.
- **The `check_mux_docs` cfg(test) stripper skips string and char literals** (QA-229; `b1e482d`), so a test fixture with unbalanced braces can no longer shift the cut point.
- **Unix-only PTY reader bindings are cfg-gated for Windows `-D warnings`** (`914ac15`).
- **`check_release_notes.py` no longer derives its argparse description from `__doc__`** (`97d0290`), which pyright flags as `str | None`.

## [0.57.0] - 2026-09-29

### Added
- **par-mux pane zoom** (`b42e1e0`): `resize-pane -Z` zooms a pane to fill its window and restores the exact prior layout on unzoom; zoom state broadcasts `%layout-change` with the zoomed layout.
- **par-mux pane move family** (`37be711`): `break-pane`, `join-pane`, `move-window`, and `swap-window` — panes and windows can be reorganized across sessions/windows, closing the gap with tmux's layout commands. Wire tests and `docs/MUX.md` rows included.
- **par-mux dead-pane hold and respawn** (`4437667`, `0b58dc3`, `4dde9e5`): a pane whose child exits is held with its exit code (remain-on-exit semantics) instead of being torn down immediately; `respawn-pane -t %N [-k] [-c dir] [command]` replaces the command in a live or held pane. `%pane-exited`/`%pane-respawned` notifications are mapped in the Python `TmuxNotification` converter.
- **par-mux `split-window -b` and a `pane-info` foreground-command token** (`1c4c479`): `split-window -b` places the new pane before its target — left of it under `-h`, above it in the default direction — keeping exactly its `-p` share. `pane-info` replies append an optional last `cmd=<base64>` token (standard base64 of the pane's foreground command name, tmux `#{pane_current_command}`), absent on Windows or when the process argv is unreadable. The fixed `%N @W COLSxROWS` prefix is unchanged, so existing parsers keep working.

### Changed
- **ENH-026: allocation-free FFI dirty ranges** (`bab1b27`): `terminal_dirty_ranges` coalesces runs in place on the caller-owned buffer instead of building an intermediate allocation, cutting per-frame readback cost.
- **ENH-031: pre-sized nibble-table hex encoder** (`d827666`): the streaming `send_keys_line` hex path no longer formats through the generic formatter.
- **ENH-029: typed telemetry schema** (`fa03f60`): the par-mux telemetry validator is replaced by a serde-typed `TelemetryV1` schema, deleting the hand-rolled field-by-field validator.
- **ENH-032: third-party GitHub Actions pinned to commit SHAs** (`5740f90`, `1f0b446`) across all workflows.

### Fixed
- **QA-156: par-mux rejects future-dated telemetry** (`aa6286b`): `sampled_at_unix_ms` ahead of the daemon clock is rejected instead of poisoning the agent roster.
- **Real constructor signatures in the stub, and constructor/property coverage in the API_REFERENCE checker** (ENH-030; `scripts/generate_stubs.py`, `scripts/check_api_reference.py`). PyO3 0.29 sets the class-level `__text_signature__` from `#[new]`/`#[pyo3(signature=…)]`, so the stub generator now renders every `__init__` from it — all 13 `*args/**kwargs` constructors became real signatures, with the opaque form kept only as a fallback for classes exposing no signature. The generator refuses to run against a non-streaming build (placeholder `StreamingConfig` has a `'()'` signature) with a message pointing at `make dev-streaming`. `check_api_reference.py` now also validates the first fenced `ClassName(args)` block or bullet per class section against the stub's `__init__` (names + default presence, DOC-064 rules), and checks `### Properties` sections bidirectionally — documented names must be stub properties and every stub property must be documented (empty escape-hatch allowlist). First catch: `StreamingConfig.kitty_file_media` was missing from both the constructor signature and the Properties section (audit DOC-073) — documented now with its SEC-101 semantics.
- **The shared key encoder now covers modifyOtherKeys and the macOS option-key modes** (ENH-028; `src/keyboard.rs`, `src/ffi.rs`, `src/python_bindings/terminal/input_api.rs`). `keyboard::encode_key` grows into the single encoder par-term, ParDeck and Python all call, replacing the modes that previously lived only in par-term's `key_encoding.rs`: text keys with Ctrl or Alt held encode as `CSI 27;mods;codepoint~` under modifyOtherKeys mode 1/2 (one rule set, matching par-term exactly — Shift-only exempt, ASCII bases only), and `encode_key_with(ev, term, &KeyEncodeOptions{left_option, right_option})` selects Normal/Meta/Esc option-key handling per side, picked by the new `modifiers::ALT_RIGHT` side bit (bit 6, above the kitty-order bits; ignored by every parameter field). Also par-term parity: Shift+Enter sends LF, and the legacy modifier parameter counts Shift/Alt/Ctrl only, so Super held alone no longer fabricates `CSI 1;N` parameters. Defaults (ESC both sides) keep `encode_key`/`terminal_encode_key` byte-identical to the previous wire behavior. New surface: FFI `terminal_encode_key_ex` + `TermKeyOptions` (Rust `KeyEncodeOptions`) (TERM_OPTION_MODE_* constants, ABI 3); Python `Terminal.encode_key(key, modifiers, codepoint=0, left_option=0, right_option=0)`. Data-driven conformance tests derive from par-term-input's suite (~360 rows plus a 1350-case no-truncation sweep), red-proofed against the old encoder first; two deliberate divergences are noted in the test module (Home/End keep SS3 under DECCKM, Alt+Space runs the option transform). Kitty protocol levels 2-4 remain out of scope (level 1 unchanged, pinned by tests).

## [0.56.0] - 2026-09-29

### Removed (breaking for Rust embedders)
- **The 29 deprecated `Terminal` macro/trigger forwarding methods are gone** (pinned by 0.54.0's deprecation schedule, removed on it). The 16 macro forwarders (`load_macro`, `get_macro`, `remove_macro`, `list_macros`, `play_macro`, `stop_macro`, `pause_macro`, `resume_macro`, `set_macro_speed`, `is_macro_playing`, `is_macro_paused`, `get_macro_progress`, `get_current_macro_name`, `tick_macro`, `get_macro_screenshot_triggers`, `recording_to_macro`) and the 13 trigger forwarders (`add_trigger`, `remove_trigger`, `set_trigger_enabled`, `list_triggers`, `get_trigger`, `poll_trigger_matches`, `process_trigger_scans`, `get_trigger_highlights`, `clear_trigger_highlights`, `clear_expired_highlights`, `poll_action_results`, `trigger_registry`, `trigger_registry_mut`) — use `terminal::MacroEngine` and `terminal::TriggerEngine` (stateless services over a borrowed `Terminal`). The in-file forwarder parity tests are removed with them. The Python surface is unchanged — the bindings call the services directly and keep every method name; par-term has zero Rust call sites (checked at the 0.54.0 move).

### Security
- **The Kitty graphics path is bounded end to end against decompression bombs** (audit SEC-116, recurring SEC-109; `src/graphics/kitty.rs`, `src/terminal/apc_filter.rs`). A small APC carrying a large zlib payload used to inflate without bound before `decode_pixels` ever saw the dimensions — `cat` of a crafted file, a hostile SSH host, or a tailed log could OOM the embedding process, and under par-mux or the streamer one pane took every session down. Three caps now stack, each with a `/// cap:` doc and a caps-table row: `decompress_zlib` reads through `take(limit + 1)` so an over-limit stream errors *during* inflation (the limit is the transmission's own geometry — `s`×`v`×bpp when known, `MAX_IMAGE_PIXELS * 4` otherwise); accumulated decoded chunks are capped at `MAX_KITTY_PAYLOAD_BYTES` (64 MiB, overflow resets the parser); and the wire-side APC accumulator is capped at `MAX_KITTY_APC_BYTES` (96 MiB — base64 of the payload cap plus params; an oversized APC is dropped whole at ST with the filter reset, both 7-bit and 8-bit terminators). **Behavior change:** `KittyParser::get_data` now returns `Result` and propagates decompression failures — the old raw-bytes fallback fed compressed bytes to the pixel decoder, and callers that treated corrupt-zlib-as-raw must now handle the error (a Rust-embedder-visible signature change). SECURITY.md no longer claims `MAX_DECOMPRESSED_SIZE` covers this path (it caps streaming wire frames only). Fuzzers re-run clean under `-rss_limit_mb=512`.
- **FFI string lengths can no longer name bytes the C string does not hold** (audit SEC-117, `src/ffi.rs`, `src/terminal/sequences/osc/`). `SharedState::from_terminal` computed `title_len`/`cwd_len` from the source string, then built the C string with `CString::new(..).unwrap_or_default()` — an interior NUL (OSC 7 percent-decodes `%00` to U+0000) failed the conversion, produced an empty 1-byte string, and left the length reporting the full source size (reproduced via ctypes: `cwd_len` 4003, `strlen(cwd)` 0). A C or iOS consumer honoring the length (`Data(bytes:count:)`) over-read the heap, driven by remote shell output. Fixed at both ends: a `to_c_string` helper replaces interior NULs with U+FFFD and always computes the length from the C string's own bytes, so `*_len == strlen(*)` is an invariant for any source; and `parse_osc7_url` plus the iTerm2 `CurrentDir` path reject decoded paths containing NUL or control characters outright, keeping them out of the cwd state (and every downstream consumer) entirely. The C header now documents the invariant.
- **The par-mux host-probe never runs repo-configured code** (audit SEC-115, `src/mux/host_probe.rs`, `src/mux/pane.rs`). The 30 s host-telemetry sweep ran `git` in a cwd that pane output controls through OSC 7, so a repo whose `.git/config` names a `core.fsmonitor` hook — an extracted tarball, `npm pack`, an embedded `core.worktree` — executed arbitrary commands as the daemon's user (reproduced end-to-end by the audit). Three layers now stack: the sweep probes only the pane child's kernel-reported cwd (`process_cwd`, never the OSC 7 value — a pane whose child cwd cannot be read is simply not probed, and SSH panes stop being probed as local paths); every git invocation runs with `core.fsmonitor=false`, `core.hooksPath=/dev/null`, `safe.bareRepository=explicit`, `protocol.ext.allow=never`, `--no-optional-locks`, `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0`, `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` and `GIT_DIR`/`GIT_WORK_TREE` cleared; and the dirty check is `diff-index --cached` (HEAD vs index), which runs no clean filter. **Behavior-affecting, documented narrowing:** `git_dirty` no longer flips on unstaged-only edits to tracked files — detecting those requires worktree content reads, which execute the repo's clean filter. Staged changes and untracked files still read dirty; persistence/session-restore cwd semantics (OSC 7 first) are unchanged.

### Added
- **par-mux `rename-session` and `kill-session`** (`src/mux/`; c607942, command reference in `docs/MUX.md` by 3d32e22). Session-level lifecycle was missing — a session only ended as a side effect of kill-window emptying it, and its name was frozen at creation. `rename-session -t $N <name>` takes the shell_split grammar (same as set-environment) and updates `MuxSession.name`, which every later spawn exports as `PAR_MUX_SESSION` (running panes keep the spawn-time name per the fixed-at-spawn contract); it broadcasts `%session-renamed` (a previously unemitted notification variant, wire shape matching the existing parser: id then rest-of-line name). `kill-session -t $N` removes the session and every window/pane in it (`kill_detached` per pane), broadcasting `%window-close` per window then `%sessions-changed` — the same line order kill-window's cascade produces. par-term's End-session flow (close last tab) consumes this.
- **Per-consumer damage generations replace the shared dirty bitset** (ENH-025; `src/grid/`, `src/terminal/`, `src/ffi.rs`, Python bindings). The Grid now owns the damage state and its mutators (`get_mut`/`set`, scrolls, erases, rect ops) stamp the rows they change with a monotonic generation counter — every screen mutation marks damage structurally, closing QA-150's deferred DECSERA gap by construction and deleting the handler-level and write-path dirty marks that duplicated it (phases 2a–2d). Independent consumers each capture `damage_generation()` and ask `dirty_rows_since(gen)` — one consumer's `mark_clean` can no longer hide damage from another (the ARC-088 destructive-shared-clear class). New surface: Python `damage_generation()`/`dirty_rows_since(gen)`; FFI `terminal_damage_generation` and `terminal_dirty_ranges_since` (ABI bumps to 2, additive — a generation consumer is isolated where `terminal_mark_clean`'s built-in consumer is not). A damage-completeness property test (phase 1) pins the contract; its first shrink found the DECCRA underflow below.
- **C FFI on-device embedding surface** (`src/ffi.rs`, `src/keyboard.rs`, `include/terminal_core.h`; 9c2cf0b). A full embedding API for C and Swift renderers, not just snapshots: `terminal_create`/`terminal_free`/`terminal_feed`/`terminal_resize` lifecycle, `terminal_dirty_ranges` (the dirty-row bitset coalesced into inclusive row ranges over a caller-owned buffer, cap/return-total protocol), pinned-buffer cell readback via `terminal_read_row`/`terminal_read_scrollback_row` (no allocation, no full-grid copy per frame), per-frame state via `terminal_get_cursor`/`terminal_get_modes`, and `terminal_encode_key` — key-event-to-PTY-bytes encoding (xterm legacy plus kitty level-1 disambiguate, DECCKM-aware) backed by the shared `keyboard::encode_key` encoder so frontends never reimplement key translation. The header pins the `repr(C)` layout with `_Static_assert`s mirrored by Rust layout tests, and an FFI round-trip test replays seeded VT through the C surface and matches the core's own screen state byte-for-byte with every changed row covered by a dirty range.
- **`make xcframework` builds `TerminalCore.xcframework`** (`scripts/build-xcframework.sh`, `deployment.yml`; c638c00, 610a030). Device and simulator slices for iOS embedding, built from the headless `rust-only` profile via `cargo rustc --crate-type staticlib` (everyday `make dev`/`make checkall` builds are untouched), verified with Apple-linker probes. Each slice ships a `Modules/module.modulemap` (gated by a `swiftc` typecheck probe), so a Swift embedder just does `import TerminalCore` — no bridging header. Release CI attaches `TerminalCore.xcframework.zip` to releases.
- **`ffi_readback` benchmark** (`benches/ffi_readback.rs`; 99ebac8). Criterion bench measuring the per-frame cost of dirty-range readback over 60-frame render loops on a 120×40 terminal: ~17 µs/frame against ~25 µs/frame for the full-grid snapshot control on an always-scrolling stream (~31% faster), and ~3.8 µs/frame on status rewrites (~85% faster), allocation-free.

### Changed
- **Triggers observe written text, not render damage** (audit ARC-064; `src/terminal/write.rs`, `src/terminal/trigger.rs`; c2a1f51, b2798dd). `mark_row_dirty` no longer feeds `pending_trigger_rows`: the printable write path marks rows via the new `mark_row_written`, and the wrap/newline scrolls inside the write path shift pending rows with their content (`shift_pending_trigger_rows`), so a line written at the bottom and scrolled in the same batch is still scanned exactly once, at its landing row. Two observable bugs fixed by the reclassification, both pinned by new tests: alt-screen enter/leave re-fired every trigger whose text was still on screen (mark-all-rows meant a full rescan), and SD did the same for visible rows; erase/attribute/resize damage no longer rescans at all — erased text is not new output.
- **`include/terminal_core.h` is generated by cbindgen and drift-gated** (ENH-027; `cbindgen.toml`, `Makefile`, 959135f). `make ffi-header` regenerates the header from the annotated FFI surface; `ffi-header-check` and `ffi-surface-check` (both in `make checkall`) fail on hand-edits or exports missing from `docs/FFI_GUIDE.md` — the header can no longer silently diverge from `src/ffi.rs`.
- **The `list-agents` blocked reason is one `reason=<base64>` token, not a free-text column** (audit ARC-060, `src/mux/dispatch.rs`). The row grammar is now `%N <agent> <state> <source>` followed by zero or more whitespace-free `key=value` tokens (`reason=`, `telemetry=`, `host_telemetry=`; unknown keys ignored): consumers parse positions 1-4 positionally and split each remaining token on its first `=`. The old free-text reason was ambiguous — par-term's shipped parser takes the last token as the source, so every row carrying a reason (since v0.50.0) or a telemetry token (v0.55.0) failed `AgentSource::parse` and was dropped. **Behavior change:** rows with a reason changed wire shape (the reason is now base64-encoded); rows without one are byte-identical.
- **Behavior: the `par-mux` CLI honors `$PAR_MUX_SOCKET` as the fallback target socket** (`src/mux/ipc.rs`; 1367050). `--cmd`/`--stop`/`--restart` typed inside a pane always resolved to the unnamed default socket and failed with "no daemon running" — the one context these flags are most often used from — because the pane env contract's `PAR_MUX_SOCKET` was never consulted. Socket resolution now follows one precedence (`resolve_socket_path`): `--socket` > positional `NAME` > `$PAR_MUX_SOCKET` > default, with an empty env value counting as unset; the positional `NAME` lost its `default` clap default so "given" and "absent" stay distinguishable. **Behavior change:** `--stop`/`--restart` inside a pane now target that pane's own daemon (killing the daemon that owns the pane the command was typed into).
- **Behavior: input for a session with no live PTY writer is dropped loudly and the connection closed** (`src/streaming/server.rs`; beffc93). Input arriving for a session whose writer never attached, or was detached by session shutdown, was silently discarded — no log, no metric, and the WebSocket stayed open, so a client kept typing into a void (the mux path was worst: `close_session` never detached the writer, so input kept queueing for a dead control connection). Input-bearing messages (Input/Paste/Mouse/FocusChange) are now checked for a live writer before dispatch: the drop is logged, counted in `metrics.dropped_messages`, and the connection is closed so the client reconnects. Read-only viewers and non-input messages stay connected; `shutdown()` detaches the session's writer so post-shutdown input takes the same loud path.

### Fixed
- **DECCRA destination-row arithmetic no longer underflows on inverted rects** (`src/terminal/sequences/csi/`; 2bb0401). `CSI 2;0;0;0$v` (pb < pt) made `src_bottom - src_top` wrap on `usize`, panicking debug builds inside `mark_rows_dirty`. The height saturates; an inverted source rect copies nothing, so the destination range is empty either way. Found by the ENH-025 damage proptest (first shrink).
- **Documentation accuracy sweep** (DOC-065, DOC-067–071): the crates.io install lines need `--no-default-features` (README); `screenshot_to_file` has no `html` format — use `export_html()` (README); `TmuxNotification.notification_type` values are hyphenated and all 32 are documented (API reference); `FFI_GUIDE.md` is rewritten around `terminal_core.h` and the shipped embedding surface, with the `out=NULL` sizing-call contract on `read_row`/`read_scrollback_row`/`encode_key` stated honestly; RUST_USAGE, API_REFERENCE and README point at the shipped FFI surface.
- **An arbitrary `uint16_t` in `TermKeyEvent.key` is handled, not undefined behavior** (audit QA-151, merging ARC-061/SEC-122; `src/keyboard.rs`, `src/ffi.rs`). The FFI key-event struct carried a `#[repr(u16)]` Rust enum field that C and Swift fill with a bare `uint16_t` — materializing a reference to a struct whose enum field holds an invalid discriminant is immediate UB in the shipped xcframework, whether or not the field is ever matched (a key code added on the Swift side first, or a typo). The field is now `u16` in Rust too, read through `TermKey::from_raw` (generated by a macro over the variant list, pinned by a round-trip test) which maps unknown values to `Unknown` — an invalid key encodes to zero bytes instead of UB. **Breaking for Rust embedders:** `TermKeyEvent.key` changed type from `TermKey` to `u16`; use the new `TermKeyEvent::key()` accessor (or `TermKey::from_raw`) for the validated enum. No ABI change — the C header layout is untouched (comment only).
- **Every screen mutator now honors the damage contract** (audit QA-150, merging ARC-059; `src/terminal/sequences/csi/`, `src/terminal/replay_snapshot.rs`). ICH (`CSI @`) and DCH (`CSI P`) — which readline, vim and tmux emit constantly — DECFRA, DECCRA, DECERA, DECCARA and DECRARA changed screen content without marking any row dirty, so damage-driven renderers (the iOS `TerminalCore.xcframework` loop via `terminal_dirty_ranges`) left stale cells; `restore_from_snapshot` and `restore_for_new_process` had the same hole after a mux pane restore. All of them now mark the rows they change — DECCRA marks the destination rows only, never the untouched source — and the restore paths resize the dirty bitset to the snapshot's row count before marking every row (restore swaps grid contents directly, bypassing the resize path that normally keeps `dirty_rows` sized). `ffi_round_trip_matches_core_state` gained frames for each sequence plus a post-scrollback RIS damage check, so the FFI boundary pins the contract per frame. The structural rework this entry deferred to landed in the same release — the Grid owns damage marking now (see Added, ENH-025), which closes the DECSERA gap by construction.
- **RIS resets the terminal, not the embedder's configuration** (audit ARC-058, `src/terminal/mod.rs`). `Terminal::reset()` (ESC c, and `PyTerminal.reset()`) used to rebuild the whole `Terminal`, silently reverting every host-set policy to its default — one escape from a remote program turned a hardened embedder's `disable_insecure_sequences` off, re-enabled `accept_osc7`, restored the 1 MiB OSC cap after the host lowered it, dropped the answerback string, detached all observers and triggers, and reverted file-media/clipboard/sixel limits. All of that now survives RIS: security policy, kitty file-media mode and temp-file retention, graphics and clipboard limits, answerback, theme colors (OSC 4 palette drift resets to the *configured* palette, xterm-style, via a new `configured_palette` copy written by `set_ansi_palette_color`), unicode config, observers and event subscriptions, the trigger registry, macros, notification config, the badge format, active recordings, tmux control flags, pixel dimensions, and profiling. (Incomplete in 0.56.0: the OSC 1337 transfer cap, bold brightening, conformance level, bell volumes, window position/iconified state and the mouse, inline-image, command and cwd history caps still reverted — fixed in a later release, ARC-100.) RIS also now marks every screen row dirty (the damage contract). **DECSTR (CSI ! p) is a real soft reset** instead of a screen-and-scrollback-erasing full reset: it resets modes (DECTCEM/IRM/DECOM/DECAWM/DECCKM/DECKPAM/DECSCA/DECSDM/DECSACE), SGR, charsets, margins, the cursor (home, visible), pushed colors, and the DECSC saved state, while screen content and scrollback are untouched. This is a behavior change for any consumer that relied on DECSTR clearing the screen — xterm/VT510 semantics are the contract now.
- **The dirty-row damage contract covers erase, resize, scrolls, alt-screen switches, IL/DL and the graphics scroll** (`src/terminal/`; aa6edf1, 32b922f). Writing marked rows dirty, but ED/EL/ECH/DECERA, resize, region scrolls (SU/SD/IL/DL, wrap/IND/NEL/RI), alt-screen switching and the graphics-placement scroll changed cells while reporting nothing dirty — a damage-driven renderer (the iOS FFI readback loop) left stale pixels on exactly those operations. `mark_rows_dirty(top, bottom)` centralizes region marking; every scroll site marks its region, the erase operations mark their affected rows, resize marks the whole new screen, and screen switches mark everything. Found by the FFI round-trip test feeding a seeded VT replay; completed for every remaining mutator by QA-150 above.
- **The streaming PTY input path no longer parks queued frames, and every input drop is attributable** (`src/streaming/`, `src/pty_session.rs`; 932283a, 444be93, e818a37, 6828c63). Keystroke-sized frames could stall 10–60 s between enqueue and PTY write, released only when an unrelated thread unparked the drain: the per-session input queue's drain task parked on primitives the enqueue side failed to wake (tokio `blocking_recv` from `spawn_blocking` first, then every replacement park — the drain now polls `try_recv` + sleep on a named plain thread and takes the writer mutex by `try_lock`-with-retry, so any wait is bounded to one poll interval). Teardown could hang on top: `MuxSessionFactory` and each session's daemon-connection drain thread held the `StreamingServer` strongly while the server holds the factory — both back-references are now `Weak`. Every remaining silent drop site on the streamer→daemon path is now visible: daemon-rejected send-keys reply blocks and unparseable lines are logged, and oversize, rate-limited, and mid-dispatch writer-loss drops are counted in `metrics.dropped_messages`. The stress repro is un-ignored — its residual "deschedule" was the test's own EOF write queue-jumping ahead of the frames it followed (EOF now takes the same enqueue queue).

## [0.55.0] - 2026-09-27

### Removed (breaking for Rust embedders)
- **The deprecated `Terminal` screenshot/benchmark forwarders are gone** (pinned by 0.54.0's deprecation schedule). Six `#[doc(hidden)]` methods removed: `Terminal::screenshot`/`screenshot_to_file` (use the `crate::screenshot::render_terminal`/`save_terminal` free functions) and `Terminal::benchmark_rendering`/`benchmark_parsing`/`benchmark_grid_ops`/`run_benchmark_suite` (use `terminal::TerminalBenchmarks`). Parity tests removed with them. The Python surface is unchanged — the `screenshot`/`benchmark_*` bindings call the free functions/services directly and stay; par-term has zero call sites (verified). The 0.56.0-pinned `Terminal` macro/trigger forwarders are untouched.

### Added
- **par-mux hook endpoint `pane.report_agent_telemetry`** (`src/mux/hooks.rs`; card 01a0e3f11c367e62873a8ada8833e6f8). A fourth hook-report method accepting one versioned, bounded telemetry object — `version` (currently 1), data `source`, `sampled_at_unix_ms`, and optional fields (`model` ≤ 128 chars, `effort` ≤ 32, `thinking_enabled`, `context_used_percent`/`context_remaining_percent` and the five-hour/seven-day rate-limit percents 0-100 rounded, `*_resets_at_unix_ms`) — mirroring the hub's normalized telemetry shape (par-remote-herd `status_telemetry.py`), so a hook tailing the agent harness's status file can push it and the daemon never parses transcripts. Stored as one canonical JSON blob in pane metadata (display-only: never touches roster state, never persists — the save format copies named identity keys only, so the format version is untouched). Freshness is enforced at the door: a sample older than 55 minutes (the hub's `STATUSLINE_FRESHNESS_SECONDS`), a report at or below the last accepted per-source `seq`, a sample older than the one already stored, or a report whose `agent` is not the pane's current label (telemetry attaches to a claim, never takes one over) are each a silent ok no-op — absent beats stale. Malformed payloads (unknown version, out-of-range percent, over-cap string, control characters) are error-replied with nothing written, and `pane.release_agent` plus the liveness sweep clear the blob with the claim.
- **Telemetry serves on the roster and pushes `%agent-telemetry-changed`** (`src/mux/dispatch.rs`, `src/tmux_control.rs`, `src/mux/emit.rs`; card 01a0e3f11e287f038dadcf332a1af961). `list-agents` rows carry a fresh sample as one final whitespace-free `telemetry=<base64>` token — standard base64 of the canonical JSON, so string values with spaces ride the space-split row intact; stale or absent telemetry adds nothing and the row keeps its exact pre-telemetry shape. An accepted report broadcasts the new `%agent-telemetry-changed %N <agent>` notification: identity only, no values — clients re-query `list-agents`, the same shape `%sessions-changed` teaches. The variant round-trips through `TmuxControlParser` (serialize-then-parse conformance test, the T5.5 pattern) and maps to `PyTmuxNotification` (`notification_type: "agent-telemetry-changed"`). The `%` notification lines are the part older clients ignore by construction; the roster row itself is **not** backward compatible for every consumer — a positional parser that treats the last token (or the rest of the line) as further data must handle the new token, and rows carrying a blocked reason were already ambiguous for such parsers (corrected for 0.56 by encoding the reason, see ARC-060 in Unreleased).
- **Host telemetry probe: disk-free percent and git state per pane cwd** (`src/mux/host_probe.rs`; card 01a0e3f1205371619309073eb5f803d6). A dedicated daemon thread sweeps rostered panes every 30 s (whole-sweep deadline 10 s, per-git deadline 5 s) measuring what no hook can: `statvfs` disk headroom (Windows serves a graceful nothing for now) and `git symbolic-ref`/`diff-index`/`ls-files` state, mirroring HerdDeck's hub probe (par-remote-herd `_probe_cwd`) — a failing repo serves no verdict, never "dirty". Never on the roster poll: `list-agents` reads only what the sweep wrote, the invariant the hub keeps by snapshot cadence. Results land in pane metadata with per-field `sampled_at_unix_ms` and `source: "host-probe"` and serve as the roster row's `host_telemetry=<base64>` sibling token, aged per field against the same 55-minute window — a stopped sweep fades the token field by field. Display-only like hook telemetry (never persisted), cleared with the claim, and broadcast-free: `%agent-telemetry-changed` stays the hook's signal.

## [0.54.0] - 2026-09-27

### Breaking (Rust embedders)
- **`sim` no longer implies `screenshot`** (ENH-024; `Cargo.toml`, `scripts/check_features.sh`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`). The headless `sim` profile dropped the implied `screenshot` feature, so `cargo build --no-default-features --features sim` no longer compiles `crate::screenshot` or pulls the swash font-shaping stack and the ~700 KB of embedded fonts. **Migration:** add `features = ["sim", "screenshot"]` to keep `crate::screenshot` (verified: `cargo check --no-default-features --features sim,screenshot` compiles and `cargo test --lib --no-default-features --features sim` passes 1786 tests). The sim CI guard and `make check-features` now assert `swash` never reaches `sim`. No first-party consumer uses `sim` today, so the blast radius is zero.

### Security
- **The Windows named-pipe client verifies the server's identity before connecting** (`src/mux/ipc.rs`; audit SEC-112, commit aea292b — omitted from these notes at publish time). Named pipe names share one machine-wide namespace, and the daemon's owner-only DACL governs only pipes it created — it says nothing about who created the pipe a *client* connects to, so another local user's process serving the derived pipe name would receive this client's keystrokes and clipboard as its par-mux server (the Unix side has refused a foreign server's euid since 0.52.0). `connect_local_stream`'s Windows arm now fails closed with `PermissionDenied` unless the server process's token user SID equals the client's own: the pipe handle names the server (peer-credential pid), `OpenProcess`/`OpenProcessToken` compare the SIDs, and every lookup failure (server exited, pid recycled, access denied) refuses — unprovable is not same-user, mirroring the Unix euid check. `prepare_socket_path` inherits the refusal, so a daemon never steals a foreign server's path either. Operator-visible behavior is unchanged when connecting to a same-user daemon; `windows-sys 0.61` is a new Windows-only optional dependency under the `mux` feature. Details: [docs/SECURITY.md](docs/SECURITY.md#socket-permissions).
- **The Kitty file-media gate is reachable from every embedder path** (`src/python_bindings/`, `src/streaming/server.rs`; audit SEC-112). `PtyTerminal` — the class that runs untrusted programs — had no `set_allow_file_media`/`get_allow_file_media`, and `StreamingServer::with_config` never applied its `kitty_file_media` config to the caller-supplied terminal (only the session-factory path did), so an embedder hardening a single-session server with `"off"` kept `"temp_only"` silently. Both methods now exist on `PtyTerminal` too (emitted from one shared definition with `Terminal`), and `with_config` applies the configured mode to the default session's terminal. **Behavior-affecting:** a terminal passed to `StreamingServer::new`/`with_config` now carries the configured mode — including the `temp_only` default — instead of keeping whatever mode it had.
- **API_REFERENCE.md parameter lists now match the stub** (audit DOC-039). 39 documented signatures were wrong (phantom arguments, missing required ones, wrong keyword names/shapes); all corrected against `_native.pyi`, with a new `scripts/check_api_reference.py` wired into `make stub-check` that fails on any doc-vs-stub parameter drift (495 signatures checked).

### Fixed
- **`PtyTerminal` is documented as what it is: not a `Terminal` subclass** (audit DOC-040). The reference claimed it "inherits all Terminal methods"; at runtime 222 of `Terminal`'s 386 public methods have no `PtyTerminal` counterpart. The false claims are replaced with a generated Method Availability section (full list from `_native.pyi`), and SECURITY.md's Kitty section now names the `PtyTerminal` way to set the file-media mode.

### Changed
- **`par-term-streamer` rate-limits client input by default** (ENH-021; `src/bin/streaming_server/cli.rs`). `--input-rate-limit` defaulted to `0` (unlimited), leaving the QA-131 memory-growth scenario reachable out of the box. The default is now 1048576 (1 MiB/s per client connection — well above human typing and normal pastes; the 256 KiB paste cap clears in under a second). **Behavior-affecting:** operators who need unlimited input pass `--input-rate-limit 0` or `PAR_TERM_INPUT_RATE_LIMIT=0`.
- **Macro and trigger logic moves off the `Terminal` god object onto services** (ARC-039; `src/terminal/macros.rs`, `src/terminal/trigger.rs`; follows the `TerminalBenchmarks` pattern from ARC-021 phase 1). The macro library/playback state machine (16 methods) and the trigger scan/action lifecycle (13 public methods) are now stateless services operating on a borrowed `Terminal`: `terminal::MacroEngine` and `terminal::TriggerEngine`. `Terminal` keeps `#[doc(hidden)]` deprecated forwarding methods so existing Rust callers compile; the Python bindings, the PTY reader path, and the integration tests call the services directly, and parity tests pin each forwarder to its service's output. The Python surface is unchanged (verified by `make stub-check`). par-term call sites checked before the move: zero.

### Deprecated
- `Terminal::screenshot`/`screenshot_to_file` and `Terminal::benchmark_rendering`/`benchmark_parsing`/`benchmark_grid_ops`/`run_benchmark_suite` — deprecated since 0.53.0; their removal is now pinned to **0.55.0**. Use `screenshot::render_terminal`/`save_terminal` and `terminal::TerminalBenchmarks`.
- The 16 `Terminal` macro forwarding methods (`load_macro`, `get_macro`, `remove_macro`, `list_macros`, `play_macro`, `stop_macro`, `pause_macro`, `resume_macro`, `set_macro_speed`, `is_macro_playing`, `is_macro_paused`, `get_macro_progress`, `get_current_macro_name`, `tick_macro`, `get_macro_screenshot_triggers`, `recording_to_macro`) — use `terminal::MacroEngine`; removed in 0.56.0.
- The 13 `Terminal` trigger forwarding methods (`add_trigger`, `remove_trigger`, `set_trigger_enabled`, `list_triggers`, `get_trigger`, `poll_trigger_matches`, `process_trigger_scans`, `get_trigger_highlights`, `clear_trigger_highlights`, `clear_expired_highlights`, `poll_action_results`, `trigger_registry`, `trigger_registry_mut`) — use `terminal::TriggerEngine`; removed in 0.56.0.

### Added
- `par-term-streamer --kitty-file-media` / `PAR_TERM_KITTY_FILE_MEDIA` (ENH-021) — the standalone streamer hardcoded `kitty_file_media: FileMediaMode::default()`, so operators could not turn Kitty file media fully `off` (or `all` for trusted local use). The flag takes the same vocabulary as `StreamingConfig.kitty_file_media` (`off | temp_only | all`, default `temp_only`).

## [0.53.0] - 2026-09-26

### Security
- **Kitty graphics file media is gated (`t=f`/`t=t` no longer reads or deletes arbitrary paths)** (`src/graphics/kitty.rs`). Terminal output is untrusted input, but a single `t=t` APC naming an absolute path deleted that file after reading it — before even checking it was an image (audit SEC-101, CWE-73/CWE-22; also closes the SEC-102 `t=f` read and the SEC-103 symlink race). The new `FileMediaMode` gate defaults to `temp_only`: `t=t` loads only a file whose canonicalized path sits under an allowed temp root (`$TMPDIR`, `/tmp`, `/dev/shm`) **and** whose filename contains `tty-graphics-protocol` (kitty's own spec rule), opens it with `O_NOFOLLOW`, and deletes it only after the payload decodes as an image. `t=f` now requires opting in. **Behavior-affecting/breaking:** tools relying on unrestricted `t=f`/`t=t` must opt in via `Terminal::set_allow_file_media` / `Terminal.set_allow_file_media("all")` in Python, or the new `kitty_file_media` field on `StreamingConfig` (`"off"` / `"temp_only"` / `"all"`, default `"temp_only"`) which the streaming server applies to every session terminal it creates. Sixel and iTerm2 media are unaffected (they take no paths).
- **par-mux bounds its adversarial control-protocol inputs** (`src/mux/server.rs`, `src/mux/hooks.rs`; audit SEC-104/SEC-105). A client connection could accumulate an unbounded partial control line before any length check ran (the 4 KiB command cap applied only to complete lines), so one socket write of tens of megabytes pinned that much daemon memory. Line accumulation is now capped at 1 MiB and the connection is closed when exceeded. Separately, the free-text values in agent hook reports (report, note, detail) passed the JSON parse without a length bound and were stored, broadcast, and persisted verbatim — a rogue pane could inflate daemon memory and every subscriber's socket. Those values are now truncated at 4 KiB at the validation boundary.

### Added
- `Terminal.set_allow_file_media(mode)` / `Terminal.get_allow_file_media()` — Python bindings for the Kitty file-media gate, with `StreamingConfig.kitty_file_media` for streaming session terminals.
- **PyPI wheels now ship with the streaming server compiled in** (ENH-017; `.github/workflows/deployment.yml`). Wheels were built without the `streaming` feature, so `StreamingServer`/`StreamingConfig` were constructor-raising stubs while `_has_streaming` still reported `True` (audit ARC-025). The publish pipeline now builds every wheel with `--features streaming` (wheel size 7.6MB → 12.0MB on macOS arm64; job timeouts widened for the extra dep closure). New native constant `par_term_emu_core_rust._native.HAS_STREAMING` reports the real capability, `_has_streaming` derives from it instead of an ImportError probe that never fired, and non-streaming source builds now export `None` for the streaming names instead of unusable stubs. `scripts/generate_stubs.py` handles `bool` module constants.
- **cargo-fuzz targets cover the par-mux control protocol** (ENH-018; `fuzz/`). Two new targets over the daemon's adversarial-input surface: `mux_parse_command` (whole-input and per-line, since the control socket is line-based and `%-notification`/`{-hook}` shapes ride it) and `mux_hook_report` (JSON parse, header validation, and the SEC-105 value caps against an empty tree). First fruits: the send-keys leading-whitespace panic above, found within the target's first 60 seconds.
- **Screenshot rendering is callable without `Terminal`, and a slim build can drop it** (ARC-021 phase 1; `src/screenshot/`). The bodies of `Terminal::screenshot`/`screenshot_to_file` moved into `screenshot::render_terminal`/`save_terminal` free functions over `&Terminal`; the `Terminal` methods remain as `#[doc(hidden)]` deprecated forwarders with unchanged signatures, so the Python surface is untouched. A new `screenshot` cargo feature (enabled by `python`/`python-test`, and by `sim` today) gates the ~700 KB of embedded fonts out of embedder builds that don't render images; mux/rust-only builds compile without the module.

### Changed
- **Hot-path debug logging is lazy** (QA-112; `src/debug.rs`, `src/terminal/`, `src/mux/`). `log_debug` calls in the parse and graphics paths formatted their arguments even when debug logging was off; the level check now happens first (an atomic load, off the logger mutex) and the format closures run only when the log will be emitted.
- **par-mux persist capture runs off the tree lock** (ARC-032; `src/mux/persist.rs`, `src/mux/pane.rs`, `src/mux/dispatch.rs`, `src/mux/server.rs`). A save collected pane handles and captured each pane's screen while holding the tree mutex, stalling every client command and the reaper for the duration; the capture now collects the (cheap) handle list under the lock and captures each pane's state outside it.

### Fixed
- **par-mux `send-keys` no longer panics on leading whitespace** (`src/mux/command.rs`; fuzz-found by ENH-018's new `mux_parse_command` target within its first 60 seconds). `parse_command` finds the command name after any leading whitespace, but `parse_send_keys` stripped the name from byte 0 of the raw line — a control line like `" send-keys -t %0 Enter"` (or tab-led) panicked the per-connection handler with `the command name prefixes the line`. The strip now runs on the trimmed line, matching the tokenizer's own view.
- **Build stamps no longer degrade to `+unknown` for crates.io builds** (`build.rs`). Without `.git`, the stamp fell back to `unknown`, so a daemon and client built from different code at the same crate version were indistinguishable and clients' stale-daemon checks stayed silent. The fallback is now `src-<16hex>`: an FNV-1a digest over the sorted crate source (`src/**`, `Cargo.toml`, `build.rs`, CR-normalized), deterministic for identical source and different the moment any of it differs, with `rerun-if-changed` on the hashed inputs so in-place edits re-stamp. Git checkouts keep the sha-based stamp.
- **Streaming input order is guaranteed: one per-session PTY writer** (QA-110; `src/streaming/session.rs`). Each `Input`/`Paste` previously spawned its own blocking writer and `Mouse`/`FocusChange` wrote from the async task, all racing for the PTY mutex with no arrival-order guarantee — back-to-back keystrokes could reach the shell reordered, and a blocked write stalled a tokio worker. All four input kinds now enqueue onto a per-session channel drained by a single blocking task holding only a `Weak` to the session (it exits when the session drops), so channel order is byte order on the PTY; bracketed pastes are sent as one frame so markers cannot split from their payload.
- **par-mux `connect_or_spawn` probes the pre-0.52 socket path** (`src/mux/client.rs`). The 0.52 per-UID socket directory left a 0.51 daemon serving the legacy path invisible to the new default, so a client silently spawned a second daemon for the same name and stranded the old one with its live sessions (observed in the wild from par-term). The client now attaches to whatever answers on the legacy path instead of spawning, so the existing version check surfaces the stale-daemon mismatch instead of hiding it.
- **par-mux removes the socket file when the daemon exits** (`src/mux/server.rs`). `--stop` waited for the socket to stop accepting but left the file on disk, so `ls par-mux-*.sock` could not tell live daemons from stopped ones. Every deliberate exit path (kill-server, SIGTERM, exit-when-empty, listener fault) now unlinks the bound path; a crash skips it and the next bind reclaims the remnant as before.
- **par-mux spawns panes off the tree lock** (ARC-022; `src/mux/tree.rs`, `src/mux/dispatch.rs`). `new-session`/`new-window`/`split-window` held the tree mutex across fork/exec and the reader-thread start, so a slow shell rc or stale cwd stalled every client command, the reaper, and persist capture. The flow is now two-phase: reserve ids and geometry under the lock, drop it, spawn, then re-lock to complete and wire the pane (abandoning the reservation if the tree moved underneath it).
- **par-mux `reap_dead_panes` decides and kills under one tree guard** (QA-115; `src/mux/tree.rs`). The reaper re-locked the tree four times per dead pane, so a concurrent split could turn a last-pane kill into a `kill_window` over a pane that had just gained a sibling. The decision, the kill, and the post-kill active-pane read now share one guard; broadcasts still run after the lock drops.

## [0.52.0] - 2026-09-25

### Security
- **par-mux: the Linux socket fallback no longer lives in shared `/tmp` unguarded** (`src/mux/ipc.rs`). Without `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, `default_socket_path` returned `/tmp/par-mux-<name>.sock` directly, and `connect_or_spawn` attached to whatever server was listening there — another local user could pre-bind the path and receive a client's keystrokes, `set-environment` values, and clipboard. The fallback base is now the tmux-style per-UID directory `<tmp>/par-mux-<uid>/` (mode `0700`), and both `prepare_socket_path` (bind) and `connect_local_stream` (connect) verify the directory's owner and mode before using it, failing closed with `PermissionDenied` when it is owned by another user, grants group/other access, or is not a directory. Accepted connections are additionally refused unless the peer runs as the daemon's user (`SO_PEERCRED`/`getpeereid`), dropped without disturbing the listener. Explicit `--socket` paths and `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` bases are unchanged. **Behavior-affecting:** the default fallback socket path moved one directory deeper, so daemons running from before the upgrade are not found at the new path (a fresh daemon spawns; kill the old one); persisted state is keyed by socket stem and is unaffected. See [docs/MUX.md](docs/MUX.md#socket-and-state-paths).

### Added
